### B1981-missing-fs-vfs-surface (lane drop fold)

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED pending | MISSING | low | **devtmpfs admitted tmpfs option NAMES without checking their spellings** — fixed: tmpfs publishes a validate-only entry point and devtmpfs's constructor calls it, so `-o size=64mb` fails the mount there exactly as it does on a real tmpfs. Was: It borrows the tmpfs parameter table, so an unknown key now fails as the reference's does, but tmpfs's value parser is crate-private — a malformed value under a known key reaches the mount unvalidated. One exported validate-only entry point from the tmpfs mount-options module closes it. | B1981. `fs::tmpfs::validate_opts`, pinned by `the_validate_only_entry_point_answers_exactly_what_a_mount_would` (positive control: entry point made unconditionally Ok -> RED). The devtmpfs CALL SITE is in target-gated `registry.rs` and only the kernel build covers it. | — |
| FIXED b87e7ab8b | INFRA | low | **Repository text cited external implementation files by path and line in 22 files (33 citations)** — fixed: each now states the behaviour, ordering or invariant it stood in for, and one reproduced C ladder under `///` was rewritten as prose. Was:, which the semantic-verification rule forbids — `waitwake.c:738`, `swapfile.c:3610-3614` and similar appear in doc comments and test comments. None are in `vfs`/`fs`/`ext4` after this lane rewrote the one quoted C block it owned (a `chroot(2)` ladder reproduced verbatim under `///`). The rest are a mechanical sweep: state the resulting behaviour, keep the provenance in the test. | B1981. `grep -rnE '[a-z_0-9]+\.[ch]:[0-9]+' crates/ tools/ docs/` -> 33 hits before, **0 after** (b87e7ab8b); `cargo test -p tty -p ipc -p sched -p modules` green after the rewrite. | — |
| FIXED | DEFECT | high | **A `MAP_PRIVATE` mapping of a huge-page file wrote through to the file.** The huge fault arm resolved every mapping to the file's own page before the shared/private split, so a private mapping's writes were visible to the file and to every other mapper — and `fork` shared the leaf unconditionally, so a child's writes reached the parent. Fixed: a private read installs the file's page with the write bit stripped, a private write takes a copy the mapping owns outright (`huge_cow_frame` → pool page → copy), the write-protection fault copies rather than re-sharing, the displaced page's reference goes back to the backing, and `fork` W-strips a private huge VMA at the huge granule. | B1981. `mm-vmm/src/tests_hugetlb_cow.rs` — 5 tests driving the real fault arms through a recording MMU. Positive control 8: re-sharing the private page fails 3 of 5, restoring passes 5. | — |
| FIXED | DEFECT | med | **Huge pages were counted into `RssFile`.** A hugetlbfs mapping is `VmaBacking::File`, so `class_of` charged it to `MM_FILEPAGES` and one 2 MiB fault raised `VmRSS` by 4 KiB — wrong twice over: the reference keeps hugetlb out of every `MM_*` counter, and the leaf holds 512 base pages, not one. Fixed: an `RssClass::Hugetlb` outside `RssPages::total()`, counters in base pages via `pages_per_leaf` (asked identically by install and remove, so the two cannot disagree), and a `HugetlbPages:` row in `/proc/pid/status`. `statm` needed no change — it reads only the three resident classes. | B1981. `rss::tests::resident_set_excludes_hugetlb_pages`; `mem_render::tests::{hugetlb_pages_are_reported_on_their_own_row_and_in_no_rss_row, statm_resident_excludes_hugetlb_pages}`. Positive control 9: folding hugetlb into `total()` fails 2, restoring passes 17. | — |
| FIXED | MISSING | med | **The huge-page pool was invisible to userspace.** `/proc/meminfo` named no `HugePages_*` and there was no `/sys/kernel/mm/hugepages/`, so an operator could size the pool through `vm.nr_hugepages` and had no way to see what it held. Fixed: `HugePages_Total/Free/Rsvd/Surp` (page counts, not KiB — they cannot ride the KiB loop), `Hugepagesize`, and `Hugetlb` summed across both granules; plus `/sys/kernel/mm/hugepages/hugepages-{2048,1048576}kB/` with the five attributes, `nr_hugepages` and `nr_overcommit_hugepages` writable. Every value is read live from the pool, so no second copy can drift from it. | B1981. `sysfs::mm::tests` (7). `procfs/src/meminfo.rs` reads `pmm::hugetlb::{nr,free,resv,surplus}_hugepages`. | — |
| FIXED | DEFECT | med | **A block leaf alive at process exit leaked its huge page forever.** `free_user_tree_leafmap` skipped block leaves at both L1 and L2 (`is_huge_or_block(e) { continue; }`), so a hugetlbfs mapping still present at teardown never dropped its reference and the page never returned to the pool. Fixed: both levels now call the leaf callback with the granule, and the teardown/zap paths release through the pool. | B1981. `hal/src/pt_walker/free.rs`; `PageSize::from_walk_level` + `hal::tests` (2). Positive control 5: making the granule always answer `P4K` fails 2. | — |
| FIXED | DEFECT | med | **A partial-huge-page `munmap` tore down a whole huge page.** The zap loop cleared whatever leaf covered the address, so unmapping 4 KiB inside a 2 MiB mapping removed all 2 MiB and then stepped past the end of what it had already destroyed. Fixed: a range that would cut a huge mapping off a huge-page boundary is refused with `EINVAL`, which is what the reference's split hook answers. | B1981. `address_space::huge_split` (6 tests). Positive control 6: dropping the boundary checks fails 2. | — |
| FIXED | INFRA | low | **Pool release was `O(nr_hugepages)`.** `owned` was a `Vec` scanned linearly on every release. Fixed: a `BTreeSet`, so release is `O(log nr)`. | B1981. `mm-pmm/src/hugetlb/pool.rs`. | — |

### B1981-missing-fs-vfs-surface (wave 2)

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED 3e50dc9ff | MISSING | low | Landlock logging is inert: the three `LANDLOCK_RESTRICT_SELF_LOG_*` flags, the `quiet_*` ruleset masks and the per-rule `LANDLOCK_ADD_RULE_QUIET` marking are all validated and carried, but nothing consumes them because this kernel has no audit subsystem. Identical to a Linux built without audit support, and no access decision changes — recorded because "stored but unconsumed" is otherwise indistinguishable from a bug. | `crates/kernel/landlock/src/uapi.rs`; `Ruleset::{quiet_fs,quiet_net,quiet_scoped}` are consumed by the `ADD_RULE_QUIET` admission test, `FsRule::quiet`/`NetRule::quiet` are not consumed at all. Closed by 3e50dc9ff: the quiet masks and per-rule quiet marking now suppress real audit records, and a domain reports its denials and its own creation. Positive control: an object marking ignored by `quieted()` goes RED. | unowned |
| FIXED 3e50dc9ff | MISSING | low | `memfd_create(MFD_HUGETLB)` returns ENOSYS because there is no hugetlbfs in this kernel at all — consistent with `mmap(MAP_HUGETLB)` returning EINVAL and with `cachestat`'s unreachable hugepage EOPNOTSUPP leg. This is the answer an unconfigured hugepage build gives, not a stub, but it IS absent Linux surface: a real distro kernel has `CONFIG_HUGETLBFS=y`. Closing it means a hugetlbfs lane, not a memfd change. | F783; `319_memfd_create.rs` hugetlb leg. Closed by 3e50dc9ff: hugetlbfs exists — a huge-page pool with the reference's reserve/surplus/overcommit accounting, the filesystem with all seven options enforced, and huge leaves installed through the existing MMU path. `MFD_HUGETLB` and `MAP_HUGETLB` both work against real glibc userspace (2 MiB-aligned mapping, written and read back, second mapper sees the same page), and `cachestat`'s hugepage leg is reachable. 118 tests; 7 positive controls. | — |
| FIXED 3e50dc9ff | MISSING | low | fanotify `FAN_AUDIT` verdicts record the daemon's audit-rule justification (`PermState::set_audit_rule`) but nothing emits it — this kernel has no audit log. `PermState::audit_rule()` is the read side with no consumer. | C265. `crates/kernel/fs/src/inotify/types.rs`; parse + validation covered by `inotify_fan_tests.rs`. Closed by 3e50dc9ff: an audit subsystem exists with its netlink socket, and the fanotify verdict now emits the daemon's justification. 95 tests in the new crate; positive controls on the capability ladder, the lost-record accounting and the recorded flag word. | unowned |
| FIXED 3e50dc9ff | MISSING | med | `sync(2)`'s device pass is structurally a no-op for the submit half and a per-disk barrier for the wait half, because there is no block-device page cache — device writeback does not exist. Consequence: `sync(2)`'s per-mount barrier count is still 2; the double-commit fix shows up in `syncfs`, freeze and unmount instead. | recorded by the implementing lane in the code Closed by 3e50dc9ff: block devices have a page cache, so the submit half writes back dirty device pages and the wait half waits for them. The pass takes no barrier at all, matching the reference, and the barrier bookkeeping that existed to avoid double-flushing is gone rather than left callerless. | unowned |

### B1981-missing-fs-vfs-surface

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED PENDING | MISSING | blocker | **x86 boots multiboot2 (BIOS/CSM) only — no UEFI path, so the kernel cannot be loaded on a board without a CSM.** The premise was wrong in both halves. `grub2-mkrescue` was already writing a HYBRID ISO carrying two El Torito boot images — a BIOS one and a UEFI one — and GRUB's x86_64-efi build loads the same kernel through the same multiboot2 entry-address tag after exiting boot services, so the kernel's handoff needed no change at all. What was genuinely absent was any way to SELECT UEFI firmware in the harness, any gate that would fail if the UEFI path broke, and correct text in the boot specs, all three of which this branch adds. The kernel's own multiboot2 header carries no boot-services tag and no EGA console tag, which is what makes the firmware-agnostic handoff work. | Boot evidence: `OXIDE_QEMU_UEFI=1 tools/boot-smoke.sh x86` reached live userspace in 87 s on the first attempt, serial RX probe answered. ISO evidence: the El Torito catalog reports `img 1 BIOS /boot/grub/i386-pc/eltorito.img` AND `img 2 UEFI /efi.img`. **Positive control:** an otherwise identical ISO rebuilt BIOS-only (`grub2-mkrescue -d /usr/lib/grub/i386-pc`, catalog then reports only the BIOS image) boots under the same OVMF firmware to `BdsDxe: No bootable option or device was found` with ZERO kernel output lines and zero entry-trampoline markers — so the passing gate is measuring the UEFI path and not a silent BIOS fallback. New gate: `make smoke-uefi-x86`. | B1984 |
| FIXED PENDING | MISSING | med | **No FADT parse — no ACPI reset.** Half of this row (the reset half) is closed here; the poweroff half stays open with a corrected reason in `known_issues.md`. The FADT is now decoded and the reset register it describes is honoured. `reboot(2)` on x86 walks a real ladder in the reference's order — the firmware-described reset register, then the legacy keyboard controller's reset pulse, then the chipset reset-control port, then a triple fault as the terminal rung — instead of jumping straight to the triple fault. The reset register is admitted through the reference's ladder: table revision 2 or later, the flag bit that claims it, and an address space of port, memory or bus-0 PCI config. The declared bit width and offset are deliberately ignored, matching the reference, because firmware fills them wrongly often enough that honouring them would refuse resets the hardware performs. | `firmware::acpi::fadt` (new): `parse_fadt` + `reset_action`, both ungated and hosted-tested — 15 new tests. `power::reset` (new): ladder order and port encodings, ungated, 6 new tests; `power::reset::x86` performs the writes. Offsets are pinned by reproducing all five conformant FADT lengths from the field offsets themselves, so a whole-struct drift goes red, not just a single field. **Positive controls (12, each reinstated then restored):** shifting the flags offset by one, shifting the sleep-control offset, removing the revision gate, removing the flag-bit gate, removing the PCI device/function bound, retyping a legacy PM block out of port space, dropping the 64-bit DSDT pointer precedence, moving the triple fault off the end of the ladder, adding the firmware rung when no register exists, duplicating a rung, and dropping the reset-control read-modify-write mask — all went RED; all restored GREEN. | B1984 |
### B1988-earlycon-and-boot-debug-params

The boot-parameter surface for a boot that hangs and prints nothing. Every row
was re-verified against the code before implementation. `<sha>` is filled at
merge; the branch is the record until then.

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1988 | MISSING | high | **A boot produced no output before the serial console driver registered — ~3.4 s of x86 boot and ~6 s of arm boot were invisible, so a hang anywhere in memory, scheduler or device init left nothing to diagnose with.** The only early sink was `debug-boot`, a compile-time Cargo feature: a shipped build could not be made to talk without a rebuild. `earlycon` / `earlyprintk` now bring a console up from the command line, writing straight to the UART with no driver model, no allocation and no lock that depends on later init. | Plain boot's first kernel line is `[4.164]`; with `earlycon` it is `[0.744]`. `crates/shared/bootparam`; 12 hosted tests. Positive control: breaking the `io,` io-type decode turns `explicit_port_io_form` RED. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | MISSING | high | **A boot that hung could not name the step it hung in** — the boot sequence was a straight line of unannotated calls. `initcall_debug` now traces each step's entry, return value and duration, entry line FIRST so a step that never returns has already named itself. | 64 `calling` / 62 `initcall … returned … usecs` lines across both arches with the parameter, 0 without. `klog::initcall`. Positive control: moving the entry line after the call turns `the_entry_line_is_emitted_before_the_step_runs` RED. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | MISSING | med | `keep_bootcon` was absent: the boot console had no lifetime at all, so the handover window to the real console could not be observed. The console registry now has a reserved boot slot fanned FIRST, dropped on the real console's registration unless the parameter says otherwise. | `klog::console::SLOT_BOOT` + `klog::bootcon`. Positive control: removing the handover drop turns `handover_drops_the_boot_console` RED. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | MISSING | med | `ignore_loglevel` and `loglevel=N` were absent, so a boot dying before userspace could lower the threshold could not be made to print everything. | `klog::syslog::suppress_console` now answers `lvl >= console_level() && !ignore_loglevel()`. Positive control: reverting the second term turns `ignore_loglevel_defeats_the_console_gate` RED. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | DEFECT | med | **`quiet` was on the composed boot line of every boot and did nothing** — a parameter asserting silence while every consumer expected a talkative boot. Now honoured (installs the quiet console level), and removed from the default line rather than left as a lie. | `cmdline::printk::console_loglevel`; `bootargs.rs` no longer emits `quiet`. Positive controls: dropping the `quiet` arm turns `quiet_is_honoured_rather_than_ignored` RED; `the_default_line_does_not_ask_for_silence` pins the composer. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | DEFECT | high | **An aarch64 panic printed NOTHING** — the panic handler was a bare spin loop, so a panic and a hang were indistinguishable on that arch. Both arches now report through one shared implementation. | `crates/arch/kernel-bin-aarch64/src/main.rs` called `loop { spin_loop() }` with the `PanicInfo` unused; both shims now call `klog::oops::panic_and_stop`. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | MISSING | med | `panic=<n>` was absent and unreachable: the panic handler had no timeout and never called the reboot path, which existed and was fully built. The parameter now works, through a restart callback installed once the power subsystem is up. | `klog::oops::after_panic` + `power::machine::restart` wired from `runtime::init`. Positive control: making the default restart rather than halt turns `the_default_keeps_the_console_text` RED. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | MISSING | med | `oops=panic` was absent: an unhandled kernel fault halted the faulting CPU silently, which on a machine that then sits there is indistinguishable from a wedge. Both arch fault paths now escalate when the parameter is set. | `hal-x86_64/src/fault.rs`, `hal-aarch64/src/fault.rs` at the `!handled` site. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | MISSING | med | `printk.time` and `printk.devkmsg=on\|off` were absent; the timestamp prefix was bound to "is the timer calibrated" with no way to ask for or against it. | `klog::bootcon::{printk_time, devkmsg_mode}`, `klog::ratelimit`. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | COVERAGE | med | **A test derived its expectations from the constant it was checking, so it could not fail.** `the_debug_preset_reaches_the_line` iterated `KERNEL_DEBUG_PARAMS` and asserted each member reached the line — removing `earlycon` from the preset just made it check less. Found by its own positive control. Now the required parameters are spelled out in the test and the constant's size is pinned against them. | Positive control before: defect → GREEN. After: defect → RED, restored → GREEN. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | INFRA | med | Hand-rolled qemu command lines were the only way to pass a debug parameter, which has already produced wrong measurements. There is now one composable place: `OXIDE_CMDLINE_DEBUG=1` prepends the preset, `OXIDE_CMDLINE_EXTRA` composes with it, and `make boot-debug-{x86,arm}` / `make smoke-debug` carry it. Serial logs are kept under `target/boot-logs/` whether the boot passes or fails. | `tools/xtask/src/image_qemu/bootargs.rs`, `Makefile`; 5 hosted composition tests. | B1988 |
| FIXED B1988 | COVERAGE | low | Claim re-verified and REFUTED before acting on it: "`boot-smoke.sh` deletes its log on success, so there is no supported way to keep an early-boot log from a passing run." `SMOKE_KEEP_LOG` and `SMOKE_KEEP_LOG_DIR` both exist and both copy from `cleanup`, which runs on EXIT regardless of outcome. The real gap was only that no make target set them; that is what changed. | `tools/boot-smoke.sh` `keep_log_copy` called unconditionally from `cleanup`, `trap cleanup EXIT`. Confirmed live: `make smoke-debug` PASS on both arches left `target/boot-logs/{x86,arm}.log` plus per-attempt copies. | B1988 |

### B1982-missing-sched-proc-surface
Six rows re-verified against the code and the reference before any work started;
all six were already closed by earlier lanes and had never been flipped.
| FIXED f489ea13f | MISSING | MED | Claim: "`clone(CLONE_SYSVSEM)` WITHOUT `CLONE_THREAD` gets its own empty semaphore undo list; oxide keys the list on the thread-group id." STATED BACKWARDS — the list is keyed on a per-TASK slot and shared by the FLAG, exactly as the reference does. | `Task::sysvsem_undo` (a per-task slot, not a tgid key); `ipc::sysv::sem::undo::copy_semundo(sysvsem, parent, child)` takes the flag and is called from `056_clone.rs` with `(flags & CLONE_SYSVSEM) != 0`; `ipc::sysv::sem::tests::undo` pins "no CLONE_SYSVSEM means a list of its own". | B1982 |
| FIXED f04ebd124 | MISSING | med | Claim: group-stop / continued notification reaches one parent only. Already closed — both notifications are issued, under the reference's exact conditions. | `sched::jobctl::{stop_audience, continued_audience}` are pure `const fn` in an ungated module (`real_parent: gstop_done && (!traced \|\| reparented)`), driven by `live/stop.rs::{notify_stop,notify_continued}` which issue one call per true flag; full truth table in `sched::jobctl::tests`. | B1982 |
| FIXED 6cadc0852 | MISSING | med | Claim: `clock_nanosleep` cannot tell the STATIC per-thread CPU clock from a dynamic per-thread encoding naming pid 0. Already closed — `ClockSpec::CpuEncoded` carries a `dynamic` bit precisely for this split. | `sched::timers::clockid::nsleep_supported` answers `dynamic \|\| !per_thread`, so the static thread clock reports EOPNOTSUPP and the dynamic self-naming encoding reaches the callback and then reports EINVAL; pinned by `static_and_dynamic_cpu_clocks_are_distinguishable_and_answer_differently`. | B1982 |
| FIXED 6cadc0852 | DEFECT | low | Claim: "`CpuMeasure::Sched` samples `utime+stime`... no scheduler-runtime accumulator exists to read." STATED BACKWARDS — the accumulator exists, is charged on every schedule-out, and is what the scheduler clock reads. | `Task::sum_exec_runtime_ns`, charged by `cputime::charge_exec_runtime` from `live/schedule/switch.rs::update_curr`; read directly by `timers::clock::task_cpu_sample` for `CpuMeasure::Sched`, distinct from `Prof` (`utime+stime`) and `Virt` (`utime`). | B1982 |
| FIXED 9c4788dc7 | MISSING | med | Claim: rseq time-slice extension is absent — "no prctl, no `slice_ctrl` word, no flag bits at registration, no preempt-time grant, no revoke timer, no syscall-entry revoke work". Every one of those six exists. | `sched::prctl::rseq_slice` (the prctl), `syscall::rseq` (the ABI word and the REQUEST/GRANTED bits), `sched::rseq::{try_grant_slice, slice_timer_expired, slice_syscall_enter}`, and `471_rseq_slice_yield` reading the state they write. A narrower residual gap is refiled below. | B1982 |
| FIXED (folded duplicate) | MISSING | med | Duplicate of the io_uring registered-ring row already carried in the io_uring section; the two were folded from different lane drop files. | Survivor row restated in `known_issues.md` with the `sched` half closed. | B1982 |
### B1983-missing-security-bpf-surface
| FIXED 5a239d632 | MISSING | MED | `verify_socket_filter` is an opcode whitelist with no register or pointer tracking, unlike the cgroup verifiers beside it | `bpf_verify.rs:166-211` RE-VERIFIED stale: The row predates the path-sensitive verifier. There is no `verify_socket_filter`: `BPF_PROG_TYPE_SOCKET_FILTER` is verified by the one `verify_program` worklist verifier, with an abstract register domain, scalar ranges, branch-state forking with pointer-nullness narrowing, context field bounds, stack-init tracking and reachability. | unclaimed |
| FIXED 5a239d632 | MISSING | HIGH | 16 of 39 `bpf(2)` commands answer EINVAL (PROG_TEST_RUN, PROG_GET_NEXT_ID, RAW_TRACEPOINT_OPEN, TASK_FD_QUERY, four MAP_*_BATCH, LINK_UPDATE/DETACH/GET_FD_BY_ID/GET_NEXT_ID, ENABLE_STATS, ITER_CREATE, PROG_STREAM_READ_BY_FD, PROG_ASSOC_STRUCT_OPS), so `bpftool` does not work end to end | command dispatch in `security/src/bpf.rs` RE-VERIFIED stale: All 39 commands dispatch to an owning module; the command table has no unreached arm. Re-verified against `bpf::dispatch::dispatch`. | unclaimed |
| FIXED 5a239d632 | MISSING | MED | `BPF_PROG_TYPE_LSM` is not loadable, so `security::bpf_lsm`'s registration half has no possible source and no program body ever runs on the `file_open` hook it does reach. The hook itself IS called (`257_openat.rs:504`) — the previous "unreachable dead machinery" reading was wrong | `bpf/attr.rs` `prog_type_supported`; call site `257_openat.rs:504` RE-VERIFIED stale: `prog_type_supported` admits LSM; the load path resolves the hook from the attach target and verifies the program against that hook's contract; the hook fires from the open path. | unclaimed |
| FIXED 5a239d632 | INFRA | LOW | `crates/kernel/security/src/bpf.rs` is 524 lines, over the 500-line cap | `wc -l` RE-VERIFIED stale: The file was split; it is a 156-line manifest. | unclaimed |
| FIXED 5a239d632 | MISSING | med | `attach_btf_obj_fd` / `attach_prog_fd` is decoded by no one. The reference distinguishes a target program fd (`dst_prog`, TRACING/EXT only), a kernel-BTF object fd, and a user-BTF object fd (`-ENOTSUPP`); here a nonzero value is ignored instead of judged. | `bpf/attr/prog_cmd.rs::prog_load_check_attach` consults only `ATTACH_BTF_ID`. The slot is resolved and judged: a loaded program is a target program (and only `TRACING`/`EXT` may name one), type information a loader supplied is `ENOTSUPP`, anything else `EINVAL`; a resolved object with no type id, or combined with a target program, is `EINVAL`. Hosted tests pin each rung with its own reject control. | unowned |
| FIXED 5a239d632 | MISSING | high | No BPF iterator link type exists, so `BPF_ITER_CREATE` can never succeed. Every reachable input lands on the ladder's last rung — "this link is not an iterator link" `-EINVAL` — because the only link kinds this kernel mints are cgroup and LSM links. Needs iterator link targets (task/map/prog/… seq-file iterators) and the anon-inode seq-file fd `bpf_iter_new_fd()` hands back. | `bpf::command::link_cmd::tests::each_link_command_refuses_an_unsupported_kind_with_its_own_errno` pins `iter_verdict` returning `EINVAL` for both existing kinds; the positive control (making `LinkKind::Cgroup` an iterator link) goes RED. Iterator targets exist for programs, maps and links; their stubs are published in the same object the LSM hooks are, so one attach-target mechanism serves both. `BPF_LINK_CREATE` with the iterator attach type mints an iterator link in the one link id registry, and `BPF_ITER_CREATE` mints a descriptor whose read walks the target in id order, running the program per object and once at the end. Verifier controls pin the two-slot read-only context and the two-valued step answer, each against the nearest shape that must be refused. | NEXT |
### B1980-missing-net-socket-surface (implemented)
Six `MISSING` rows closed with code on this branch. Each carries a hosted test that
was confirmed to go RED with the defect reinstated and GREEN with it restored:
| Row subject | What it now does | Test that pins it |
|---|---|---|
| raw sends queue no local error | both raw families report the size refusal on the socket's error queue, through the one existing reporting owner | `sock_v6::tests::a_raw_ipv6_size_refusal_reports_the_local_error_and_the_announcement` |
| path-MTU number ignored the headers the send carried | the reported MTU has the send's extension-header (v6) or option (v4) bytes taken off, computed ONCE so the queued record and the announcement cannot disagree | `socket_error::tests::send_failure::the_reported_path_mtu_excludes_the_headers_the_send_would_have_carried`, `sock_v6::tests::a_raw_ipv6_refusal_takes_the_extension_headers_off_the_reported_mtu` |
| `SO_RCVBUF` did not resize the error queue | the write republishes the budget, and the admission comparator matches the reference's `>=` | `socket_error::tests::queue::the_error_queue_budget_follows_the_socket_receive_budget` |
| TCP output carried no `SO_MARK` into its route lookup | `SockBase::mark` became a shared cell that the connection and listener entries hold, so a write after connect reaches the next segment with nothing to restate; a passive child takes the listener's value as its own, matching the reference's copy-at-clone | `stack_binddev::mark_tests::{a_marked_tcp_connection_transmits_over_the_route_its_mark_selects, a_mark_written_after_connect_reaches_the_next_segment, a_passive_child_takes_the_listening_socket_mark_as_its_own}` |
| metrics / MSS / path-MTU queried unmarked | all three take the mark, on both the bound and unbound branches | `stack_binddev::mark_tests::{route_metrics_come_from_the_route_the_socket_mark_selects, tcp_mss_comes_from_the_route_the_socket_mark_selects, path_mtu_comes_from_the_route_the_socket_mark_selects}` |

### B1980-missing-net-socket-surface (re-verified stale rows)
Twelve `MISSING` rows claiming absent net/socket surface were re-verified against
the tree and against the reference before any code was written. All twelve had
already been closed by later lanes without the row being flipped. The closing
commit is in each row's status; the evidence a reviewer can re-check is the
named owner module and its hosted tests:
| Row subject | Closed by | Owner module / test that pins it |
| `SIGURG` to `f_owner` on out-of-band arrival | b7d7328fb | `net::sock::oob_notify`; `stack::tcp_urgent_tests`, `unix_sock::tests::sigurg` |
| `IP_TRANSPARENT` foreign delivery + reply-source selection | 2367eafbd | `net::transparent`; `transparent::tests` |
| UDP receive queues need no option handle for local input | 2367eafbd | `net::transparent::delivers_locally`, decided upstream of demux; `stack_tests::forwarding` |
| TCP6 active open resolves a wildcard source | 7a1431293 | `net::sock::v6_source::v6_connect_source` |
| transmit timestamping records | 3cc091dcd | `net::sock::tx_tstamp`; published from `sock::send::sendto` |
| per-message `SO_MARK`/`SO_PRIORITY`/`SCM_TXTIME`/`SO_TIMESTAMPING` consumers | 3cc091dcd | `net::sock::txmeta::tx_meta` -> `TxMeta`, read by the raw/UDP route lookups and the transmit band |
| netlink has no home for generic SOL_SOCKET state | c313443d2 | `net::SockBase`, embedded by `NetlinkSocket` |
| retraction: netlink's stored-and-unread generic options | c313443d2 | same base as the reference keeps; the discarded set was the real defect and is fixed |
| `IPV6_RECVPATHMTU` has no consumer | 1a1611ab2 | `net::socket_error::pathmtu` + `sock_opts::sol_ipv6::pathmtu`; drained by `recvmsg::inet::recv_pathmtu` |
| `IPV6_PATHMTU` getsockopt | 67f075d7e | `sock_opts::sol_ipv6::get`, `ENOTCONN` when no route MTU |
| `SO_SNDBUFFORCE`/`SO_RCVBUFFORCE` reads answer `ENOPROTOOPT` | 67f075d7e | informational pin; the reference has no read arm either, and neither do we |
| `sockaddr_in6.sin6_flowinfo` never read back | 7a1431293 | `net::sockaddr::SockaddrStorage::inet6_flowinfo`, consumed by `042_connect` |
| FIXED b7d7328fb | MISSING | MED | Out-of-band arrival raises priority readiness but delivers no `SIGURG` to the receiving description's `f_owner`. The reference has TWO independent paths: an unconditional signal to `f_owner` that needs neither `O_ASYNC` nor a fasync registration, plus a fasync notification that deliberately suppresses plain `SIGURG` unless `F_SETSIG` chose a queued signal. Only the second exists here, and it correctly suppresses — so the canonical `fcntl(F_SETOWN, getpid())` + `recv(MSG_OOB)` flow signals nothing at all. Same gap on TCP urgent data: no site raises `POLL_PRI` for it. | `crates/kernel/vfs/src/file/async_notify.rs:263` `kill_fasync` returns early on `!is_async()` and again on `dfl == SIGURG && chosen == 0`; `crates/kernel/net/src/unix_sock/stream/send.rs:151` is the only OOB-arrival notification and calls only `wake_peer_subs(POLL_IN\|POLL_PRI)`; `grep -rn "Sigurg\|SIGURG" crates/` shows no delivery site outside the suppressed fasync path. The socket's own description is already reachable — `crates/kernel/net/src/sock/types.rs:133` holds the `Weak<vfs::File>` the reference's `sk->sk_socket->file` provides, set at `crates/kernel/net/src/unix_sock/gc.rs:163`. | unassigned — the canonical owner (`vfs::File` `f_owner` delivery) and the shared trigger (socket layer, common with TCP urgent) are both outside B1938's `net/src/unix_sock/**` boundary; B1938 refused to add a duplicate delivery path in `net` |
| FIXED 2367eafbd | MISSING | med | `IP_TRANSPARENT` is consumed only as a bind permission (non-local bind). Foreign-address delivery and reply-source selection are absent. Not a stored-but-unconsumed bug — the option does change behaviour — but the Linux surface is partial. | `net::sock::nonlocal` is the sole consumer. | — |
| FIXED 2367eafbd | MISSING | MED | UDP receive queues carry no live option handle either (`UdpRxQueue`/`Udp6RxQueue` hold only threaded-through atomics), so any future ingress-side decision that needs a socket option has nowhere to read it. Not reached by this lane — the transparent permission's ingress half is a routing decision, not a lookup one, so the demux needs no socket flag — but recorded because the shape is the same one that blocks the raw row above. | `crates/kernel/net/src/stack/types/udp.rs:63-92`; demux address match is `stack/core.rs:333` `if !q.bound_ip.is_unspecified() && q.bound_ip != dst { continue; }`, which matches a foreign bound address correctly with no flag needed | unclaimed |
| FIXED 7a1431293 | MISSING | MED | TCP6 active open never resolves a wildcard source: `connect_tcp6_locked` leaves `local_ip` as `Ipv6Addr::ANY` for a non-loopback destination and that value reaches `push_ipv6_header` verbatim, so the SYN leaves with an unspecified source. Every other v6 transmit path calls `v6_select_source`. Found while mapping source selection for this lane; out of its scope (it is the wildcard case, not the foreign-address one). | `crates/kernel/net/src/sock/tcp_lifecycle.rs:236-249`; contrast `stack_ipv6/udp.rs:441-454` and `stack_ipv6/tx.rs:259` | unclaimed |
| FIXED 3cc091dcd | MISSING | MED | Transmit timestamping generates nothing. The record, its selector and key words, and `publish_timestamping` exist and are tested, but no send path records a transmit timestamp: there is no per-send key allocation, no `SOF_TIMESTAMPING_TX_*` handling, and no `scm_timestamping` ancillary encoder. A socket that enables it will never see a record. | Grep: `SOF_TIMESTAMPING_TX_` has no definition in the tree; `publish_timestamping` has no non-test caller. | unclaimed |
| FIXED 3cc091dcd | MISSING | med | The per-message `SO_MARK`, `SO_PRIORITY`, `SCM_TXTIME` and `SO_TIMESTAMPING` values are admitted exactly as the reference admits them (capability, band and width), but nothing consumes them on transmit. The socket-level `SO_MARK` is in the same state: it is stored by the option table and never reaches the route lookup, which does take a mark (`route::lookup_result_mark_in` has no caller outside forwarding). One owner has to consume both, and that owner is the transmit path's mark plumbing, not the ancillary rule | `net::sock_opts::sol_socket::set` stores `Action::Mark`; `grep` for a reader on the socket TX path finds none | sock_opts / transmit lane |
| FIXED c313443d2 | MISSING | med | A netlink socket has no home for most generic SOL_SOCKET state. `SO_PRIORITY`, `SO_MARK`, `SO_LINGER`, the timestamp personalities, `SO_BINDTODEVICE` and the rest are now VALIDATED by the canonical ladder but land in the generic flag/scalar word with no family behaviour behind them, and `SO_SNDTIMEO` has no wait to bound. The Linux shape is one `struct sock` base shared by every family; this tree has `InetSocket.opts` and a partial copy on `NetlinkSocket`. | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/netlink_fd/sol_socket.rs` `apply`'s final arm; `crates/kernel/netlink/src/netlink_socket.rs:45` | — |
| FIXED 1a1611ab2 | MISSING | low | `IPV6_RECVPATHMTU` stores and reads back its bit with no consumer: no receive path emits the `IPV6_PATHMTU` ancillary message the bit asks for. | `sol_ipv6/set.rs:199` writes `flag::RXPATHMTU`, `get.rs:96` reads it, no other reader in the tree | — |
| FIXED 67f075d7e | MISSING | LOW | `IPV6_PATHMTU` as a `getsockopt` — the current route MTU, ENOTCONN when there is none — is not implemented. | FIXED c313443d2 | MISSING | med | **Retraction of part of the row that opened this lane.** B1961 recorded that netlink's generic options "land in the generic flag/scalar word with no family behaviour behind them". For the options that DO land there this is not a defect: the reference keeps the same state on its one socket base and its netlink code consults only the receive buffer, the send buffer, the two timeouts and the error — every other generic option is stored and unread for a netlink socket there too. The real defect was the discarded set, fixed above. | Reference netlink code consults `rcvbuf` (receive admission), `sndbuf` (send preflight), both timeouts and the error; ours consults the same four | B1964 |

### B1985-fault-fill-failure-reports-sigbus
| FIXED f89821ff6 | DEFECT | med | A file-mapping fill that FAILS is delivered to userspace as `SIGSEGV`/`SEGV_MAPERR`, where the reference answers `SIGBUS`/`BUS_ADRERR`. `mm-pmm/src/user_as/fault/entry.rs::handle` collapses the resolver's `Result` to a bare `bool`, so `Err(Error::Io)` from the short-read guard in `mm-vmm/src/address_space/fault/fill.rs:330` is indistinguishable from "no VMA covers this address"; `force_user_fault_x86` then classifies the signal from the `#PF` error code alone. The fill code's own comment already names the intended answer ("`filemap_fault` VM_FAULT_SIGBUS leg", `fill.rs:210`, `:320`). Besides being the wrong signal, this is why the row above cannot be told apart from a lost mapping without a debug build. | `entry.rs:345-360` returns `true`/`false` only; `signal.rs:32-41` derives `(cls, addr)` from `err`/`cr2` with no input from the resolver. No test can currently fail on it: nothing pins the signal a failed file fill produces. Closed by `vmm::fault_signal`: the resolver's failure reason reaches the signal decision through one ungated mapping consulted by both architectures. `fault_signal::tests` (9) pin the reason->signal table; `tests_shortfill::{failed_file_fill_classifies_as_a_bus_error,absent_mapping_still_classifies_as_a_segmentation_fault}` drive the production file-fault arm against a short-reading backing and a hole, so the fill's own error value is what gets classified. Positive controls: collapsing `signal_for`'s BusError arm back to the hardware classification turns 5 tests red; collapsing `failure_of`'s `Io` arm turns 4 red; both green on restore. 393 vmm + 149 pmm tests pass, both arch kernels build, `make hosted-gate` PASS, `make smoke` PASS on x86 (64s) and arm (122s), attempt 1 each. | B1985-fault-fill-failure-reports-sigbus |
### B1981-missing-fs-vfs-surface

| FIXED 09765a7f6 | MISSING | med | **13 of 21 registered filesystems publish no parameter table, so their options are still accepted-and-ignored.** `sysfs`, `debugfs`, `tracefs`, `configfs`, `securityfs`, `efivarfs`, `pstore`, `bpf`, `fusectl`, `mqueue`, `hugetlbfs`, `binfmt_misc`, and `devtmpfs` take `None`; their constructors discard the data string entirely. devpts and cgroup2 are no longer members of this set: each now publishes and consumes its Linux parameter table. Leaving the residual types at `None` avoids declaring an option surface none of them implements; each gains a table with its enforcement. | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/fsmount_common/registry.rs` `register_filesystems` Closed by 09765a7f6: every pseudo-filesystem that took `None` now publishes the reference's own parameter spec and enforces it — tracefs/debugfs/efivarfs/bpffs parse uid/gid/mode onto a superblock-owned root attribute that survives an inode-cache rebuild, and sysfs/configfs/securityfs/fusectl/mqueue/binfmt_misc declare the empty spec so an unknown key is refused. devtmpfs borrows the tmpfs table. Two remain `None` because they have no backend to configure (pstore, hugetlbfs) — each has its own row. `kernfs::mount_opts`, `tracefs::{mount_opts,context}`, `syscalls::fsmount_common::pseudo_params`; +34 tests across kernfs/tracefs/syscalls; positive control: enforcement removed -> 6 RED, unknown-key refusal removed -> 10 RED. | — |
| FIXED 09765a7f6 | MISSING | med | The watch queue holds each note as its own allocation instead of a preallocated note array, so `IOC_WATCH_QUEUE_SET_SIZE` reserves a COUNT rather than memory. The syscall-visible contract is identical (same depth, same rounding to a whole page of notes, same EBUSY on a second call, same loss behaviour when full), but a queue's memory is charged as it fills rather than up front, and the pipe-buffer accounting Linux applies (`account_pipe_buffers`, the unprivileged EPERM when a user is over the soft/hard buffer limits) has no equivalent here. A local unprivileged user can therefore hold more notification memory than Linux would allow. Fix with the pipe-buffer user accounting, which does not exist in this kernel yet for ordinary pipes either. | B1659, `watch_queue/queue.rs::set_size`. Closed by 09765a7f6: pipes now carry the reference's per-user page charge (`vfs::pipe_limits`) — a ring is charged at creation, cut to the minimum past the soft limit, refused with ENOMEM past the hard one and released with the ring, and `IOC_WATCH_QUEUE_SET_SIZE` charges its whole pages against that same account before the depth exists. `fs.pipe-max-size`, `fs.pipe-user-pages-soft` and `fs.pipe-user-pages-hard` are live sysctl-bound variables. The already-sized rung now precedes the depth range, matching the reference's order. 10 + 6 + 2 tests; positive controls: soft/hard ladder disabled -> 3 RED, charge and release dropped -> 5 RED, reservation charge dropped -> 2 RED. | — |
| FIXED 09765a7f6 | MISSING | med | Notification records are delivered only for KEY events. Linux also posts `WATCH_TYPE_MOUNT_NOTIFY`/`WATCH_TYPE_SB_NOTIFY` from the mount and superblock code in trees that carry those patches, and the generic `watch_queue` device (`/dev/watch_queue`) is absent here too — a notification pipe is reachable only through `pipe2(O_NOTIFICATION_PIPE)`. The mechanism is general (types are a filterable field and the filter code accepts any type below `WATCH_TYPE__NR`); what is missing is other subsystems calling it. | B1659; `WATCH_TYPE_NR` is 2. Withdrawn by 09765a7f6: the claim describes trees carrying out-of-tree patches, not the reference. The reference declares exactly two watch types (meta and key-notify) and registers no watch-queue device — a notification pipe is reachable only through `pipe2(O_NOTIFICATION_PIPE)` there too. This kernel matches; the filter refuses any type at or above the declared count (`watch_queue::filter`, pinned in `watch_queue::tests`). | — |
| FIXED 09765a7f6 | MISSING | med | **tmpfs accepts 15 of its 22 declared parameters without acting on them.** `TMPFS_PARAMS` lists what the reference accepts; `mount_opts.rs` honours only `mode`/`uid`/`gid`/`size`/`nr_blocks`/`nr_inodes`. `huge`, `mpol`, `inode32`, `inode64`, `noswap`, `casefold`, `strict_encoding` and the six quota-limit keys are admitted and dropped. Listing them is deliberate — omitting one would make a mount the reference accepts fail — but a `mount -o noswap` still swaps. | `crates/kernel/fs/src/tmpfs/params.rs`, `crates/kernel/fs/src/tmpfs/mount_opts.rs` Closed by 09765a7f6: tmpfs acts on the options it declares — `noswap` reaches the shrinker and the pageout path, `inode32`/`inode64` reach the inode allocator, the size grammar refuses a malformed value instead of truncating it into a valid one (`size=64mb` mounted before), and `huge`/`mpol`/`casefold` are refused with the reference's errno rather than dropped. An unacceptable option now fails the mount. `fs/src/tmpfs/mount_opts/**`; 88 -> 135 tests across the touched files; positive controls: `may_swap_out` forced true -> RED, truncating value parser -> RED, identity `constrain_ino` -> RED. | — |
| FIXED 09765a7f6 | MISSING | Med | The named namespace `fsmount(FSMOUNT_NAMESPACE)` builds has the new mount as its ROOT. The reference instead copies the caller's namespace root and mounts the new tree ON TOP of that copy. `/` resolves to the new filesystem either way, but the reference's namespace also carries the underlying root copy, and a caller that unmounts the top has something beneath it. | `vfs::mount::create_ns_mount`; `vfs/tests/mount_fsmount_namespace.rs::the_namespace_form_puts_the_mount_in_a_namespace_a_task_could_enter` pins the current shape. Pinned by `vfs/tests/mount_ns_root_invariant.rs::a_copied_namespace_gets_the_copy_of_the_root_it_was_copied_from`. | unowned |

| FIXED 09765a7f6 | MISSING | high | ext4 `FALLOC_FL_COLLAPSE_RANGE` / `INSERT_RANGE` answer `EOPNOTSUPP` from the backend: both re-index every extent past the range and the extent tree has no shift primitive (row 285). | `ext4/src/rootfs/inode/fallocate.rs` admits both modes and has no arm for either Withdrawn by 09765a7f6: both modes are implemented — `ext4::extent_rw::shift` is the shift primitive the row says is absent, and `fallocate.rs` has an arm for each that admits the range, flushes, shifts and invalidates to EOF. | unowned |
| FIXED 09765a7f6 | MISSING | med | `S_SWAPFILE` is never set, so a live swapfile is refused by an ext4-private pin (`EBUSY`) instead of the generic gate (`ETXTBSY`) (row 285). The swap layer holds a block-device handle, not an inode reference. | `S_SWAPFILE` has no producer outside test fixtures Withdrawn by 09765a7f6: `S_SWAPFILE` has a production producer — the ext4 swapfile backing taken by `swapon(2)` claims it and releases it on drop, and the generic gate answers ETXTBSY (`fs::fallocate::vfs`). Pinned by `ext4/tests/swapfile_inode_flag_image.rs`. | unowned |
| FIXED 09765a7f6 | MISSING | med | `fcntl` has no `F_GETDELEG`/`F_SETDELEG` (1039/1040). Every other command the reference's `do_fcntl` handles is present. Needs a lease-FLAVOR bit: the reference's get-lease query filters the lease list by flavor, so `F_GETLEASE` must report only plain leases and `F_GETDELEG` only delegations, while both share one break path. Blocked on a vfs change this lane does not own (`vfs::File::lease` is a single `AtomicI32` with no flavor; `lease_conflicts` matches the bare type, so an OR-ed flavor bit breaks it). Note the asymmetry: set-lease rejects a directory fd, set-deleg does not. | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/072_fcntl.rs` has no case for either; `crates/kernel/vfs/src/file/lease.rs` stores one unflavored type. Withdrawn by 09765a7f6: both commands exist (`syscalls::fcntl_deleg`), the lease word carries the flavour packed into the one word the row asks for, and the get-lease query filters by flavour. | B1970 (vfs) + syscalls |
| FIXED 09765a7f6 | MISSING | med | `Inode` has no read-open counter. The lease/open admission implements the writer-count half of the reference's rule exactly, but an exclusive lease should also require that no OTHER reader holds the file open. Needs a counter on `Inode` incremented on a read-mode open and released from the description's drop. | `vfs::file::lease_policy::open_conflicts` covers writers only Closed by 09765a7f6: `Inode` carries a read-open counter released from the description's drop, and `File::lease_open_conflict` reads both live counters plus this description's own contribution, so an exclusive lease is refused while another reader holds the file open. | unowned |

Archive of rows retired from `scratch/known_issues.md`. A row moves here only
when the fix is merged; the SHA stays with it. Kept so a later lane can see what
shape these defects took — several were subsystems that compiled, tested and
shipped with nothing calling them.

Older tables use the pre-D473 5-column shape: `Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence |
Owner`. The live ledger gained a `Class` column in D473; archived rows keep the
shape they were retired in; post-D473 imports retain the `Class` column.

## Namespaces / procfs

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED cb759577e | DEFECT | med | `/proc/<pid>/status` exposed internal UID/GID credentials unchanged when the proc mount belonged to a different user namespace. | Each procfs instance captures the mounting user namespace; its pid status renderer translates all four UID and GID slots plus supplementary groups through the canonical user-namespace engine, munging unmapped IDs to overflow. The hosted mapped/unmapped regression, full 165-test procfs suite, both target checks, and paired smoke passed; changing a mapped expected UID makes the regression fail. | B1936-procfs-userns-ids |

## Keyring

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1865 | high | Six live `request_key(2)` rows still claimed that the images lacked keyutils, no real construction completed, the helper's fork/exec credential hooks were unproved, the nested-helper pool case had no boot proof, and aarch64 had never run it. Those were duplicate, stale descriptions of the B1672/B1704 state; the unique implementation defects and their fixes already live below. | All four current lite/gnome × x86_64/aarch64 images contain the distribution `/sbin/request-key`. On current `main` (`e7631f1af`), canonical `make -j2 smoke-request-key` passed first attempt on x86_64 and aarch64 using the exact `REQUEST-KEY-PROBE: PASS` marker. The probe requires both the ordinary construction payload and a nested construction payload carrying the inner key serial, so success traverses the real helper, handler exec/fork, token inheritance, answer caching, and concurrent UMH pool growth. Linux reference inspection confirms `prepare_creds` retains `request_key_auth`, process-keyring search follows the token to the original requester, and `UMH_WAIT_PROC` work runs on concurrency-capable unbound workers. | B1865-request-key-live-proof-fold |
| FIXED 81667bf9e | low | `try_fill_iter` carried a seemingly redundant `total <= cap` term in its atomic-write guard. | The capacity floor is now explicitly tested as at least `PIPE_BUF`; therefore `total <= PIPE_BUF` already implies `total <= cap`. The guard is correct and no behavior change was needed. Focused pipe invariant regression and full pre-push gates passed. | D497-pipe-atomicity-guard-ledger |
| FIXED 5a9cf0965 | low | A closed notification pipe left its key watches attached, so subsequent key events posted into a queue nobody could read. | Each queue now tracks its reverse key-watch membership; pipe teardown removes those exact watches without posting a removal record into the dead pipe. The registry teardown regression and `cargo test -p fs --lib` (938 tests) passed, along with the full pre-push gates. | D495-key-watch-queue-close-cleanup-ledger |
| FIXED a3d215fc4 | low | The keyring UAPI header incorrectly described capability bits as unimplemented and unread. | The header now separates intentionally unreferenced UAPI ids from capability bits, which are computed by the implementing modules. Focused DH, public-key, and watch capability regressions pin the contract. | D493-keyring-capabilities-ledger |
| FIXED B1666 | high | B1579's recorded hazard — "a usermode helper started concurrently with early userspace on SMP=2 wedges (kernel-context VFS lookup vs a concurrent user lookup)" — does NOT reproduce, and the real user-reachable defect on that path was a different one: ALL helpers were serviced by a SINGLE thread that, under `UMH_WAIT_PROC`, blocks for the whole lifetime of the helper program. That is head-of-line blocking for every other pending helper, and a permanent DEADLOCK for a helper that itself asks the kernel for a helper — which `/sbin/request-key` does by design (the construction default names the ORIGINAL requester's keyring precisely so a nested request works). The nested request could only be served by the thread already waiting for the program that issued it, and the requester's wait is uninterruptible. | B1666. NEGATIVE result on the recorded wedge: x86_64 `smp=2` KVM boot with `debug-umh`, all five self-test probes correct while systemd ran concurrently — `absent-wait-exec rc=-2`, `notaprog-wait-exec rc=-13`, `present-wait-exec rc=0`, `present-wait-proc rc=0`, `absent-wait-proc rc=-2` at t=92.0-93.3 s, boot continued to a GNOME session at t=202 s. (The mechanism B1579 described — a loader resuming on another process's page tables after a sleep — was itself fixed inside B1579 by borrowing the address space rather than switching only the page-table root; the row outlived its cause.) FIX: `umh::pool` sizes the servicing contexts on demand — the thread that takes a request starts a successor first, capped at the queue depth, so nothing that fits on the queue waits behind another request rather than behind its own helper. Hosted `tests/pool.rs` 8 tests (`umh` 28 -> 36 passed / 0 failed); both arches type-check with and without `debug-umh`. | B1666 |
| FIXED 579d405bd | high | `inherit_session` had NO caller outside its own test. No forked child ever inherited a session keyring; nothing purged a dead task's entries, so a RECYCLED tid inherited the previous occupant's keys, and every task touching `@s`/`@t`/`@p` leaked a keyring plus its quota charge permanently. | B1649. Now `lifecycle::{fork,exec,exit,fsids_changed}` wired at clone/execve/exit/commit_creds; `exit_purges_the_tid_and_frees_the_keyring`, `exit_refunds_the_quota_charge`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | high | Between minting an authorisation token and handing the helper the keyring holding it, NEITHER was reachable from any gc root. Any concurrent `collect()` — an unrelated task unlinking a key is enough — destroyed both and stranded the requester on a key no helper could then answer. A real intermittent production failure, surfaced only because the hosted tests run in parallel against the global store. | B1649. `Store::collect` now roots live tokens and the keyring linking them. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | high | `KEYCTL_GET_PERSISTENT` aliased the USER keyring — wrong owner, wrong lifetime (the user keyring dies with the last session; the persistent one must outlive logout), and gated on `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` where the reference uses `CAP_SETUID`. | B1649. Now a real `_persistent.<uid>` in a `.persistent_register`, destination mandatory, expiry refreshed per use; `the_persistent_keyring_is_not_the_user_keyring`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | high | `KEYCTL_SEARCH` flattened every failure to ENOKEY, discarding WHY the search failed. That both hid EACCES/EKEYREVOKED/EKEYEXPIRED from callers and made negative-key caching impossible, so an unresolvable name would re-run the helper on every request. Three pre-existing tests asserted the flattened behaviour and encoded the wrong belief. | B1649. `ops/search.rs` now reproduces the skip-reason propagation and the `success > ENOKEY > EAGAIN > other` merge. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | med | `request_key` did not distinguish `callout == NULL` from `callout == ""`; both suppressed construction. The empty string must upcall. | B1649, `an_empty_callout_string_still_upcalls`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | med | `KEYCTL_REVOKE` did not retry with `KEY_NEED_SETATTR` on EACCES, so a key whose mask grants Setattr but not Write could not be withdrawn by its holder; and it used a partial lookup, so a second revoke reported EACCES instead of EKEYREVOKED. | B1649, `revoke_falls_back_to_setattr_permission`, `revoking_twice_reports_the_key_is_already_revoked`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | med | `KEYCTL_SET_TIMEOUT` and `KEYCTL_GET_SECURITY` had no authorisation-token path, so a helper could not bound or inspect the key it was asked to build. `KEYCTL_JOIN_SESSION_KEYRING` accepted a `.`-prefixed name, which would place a caller inside `.persistent_register`. | B1649, `set_timeout_accepts_the_authorisation_token_instead_of_setattr`, `a_dot_prefixed_session_name_is_refused`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 68f197dc4 | med | `/proc/keys` and `/proc/key-users` were empty static stubs and `/proc/sys/kernel/keys/*` did not exist at all. | B1649. Both live and per-reader filtered; four ceilings plus `persistent_keyring_expiry` bound to the live values `key_alloc` and `KEYCTL_GET_PERSISTENT` consult. | B1649 |

## Drivers / devices

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1875 | blocker | **Physical hardware had no framebuffer source: the complete VT/fbcon/fbdev stack could bind only to virtio-gpu, while the Multiboot2 handoff neither requested nor retained a firmware scanout.** | The x86 header now requests a loader-selected mode, the tag-8 parser validates and preserves exact packed-RGB geometry in `BootInfo`, and `drv-simplefb` registers a page-offset-aware, driver-owned WC mapping only after PCI probing finds no native fbdev. The fbdev mode retains firmware pitch, depth, and channel masks; damage copies convert non-XRGB formats. Hosted parser/format/damage tests and both release builds pass. A forced QEMU std-VGA boot with no virtio-gpu registered the 1280x800 framebuffer and reached userspace. Its 125 MiB repeated full-frame write took 0.27 s with WC versus 3.10 s under a temporary UC control; the control was restored and never committed. Full measurements are retained in `scratch/simplefb-performance-20260806.md`. | B1875-physical-framebuffer-source |
| FIXED B1875 | low | **Growing `BootInfo` with the framebuffer handoff deepened the already-ratcheted x86 boot chain by 16 bytes, so the stack gate rejected `_start` at 20,016 bytes.** | Both architecture boot builders now construct the complete handoff in boot-owned static storage and pass a shared reference into `kernel_main`, instead of returning the enlarged structure through `_start_rust`'s stack. The x86 chain fell to 19,904 bytes (96 bytes below its prior baseline), and the aarch64 chain remains below its 13 KiB ceiling at 12,129 bytes. Both stack gates pass. | B1875-physical-framebuffer-source |
| FIXED 18936f7b5, a5a9a5b0d, 667c8a2da, 32b37265d | high | **The real-hardware ledger claimed AP bring-up was a counting stub after both architecture paths had already shipped.** | `kmain` calls the architecture owners, not the unused generic scaffold: x86 copies the real-mode-to-long-mode trampoline below 1 MiB, sends INIT/SIPI, then installs per-CPU GS, GDT/IDT/TSS, syscall and IRQ stacks, LAPIC timer, runqueue, and online state; arm64 performs PSCI CPU_ON and reaches the same scheduler participation. SMP=2 boots and watchdog reports already exercise both CPUs. The stale row and real-hardware section are retired instead of building a second startup path. | B1875-physical-framebuffer-source |
| FIXED 5f78b2e22, fe49e882f | med | **The synchronous virtio-block path needed a 64-probe IRQ-enabled bridge before it could sleep, unlike an ordinary completion wait.** | The driver checks the used index once and parks directly; only explicitly polled I/O may loop. Syscalls run ordinary process work IRQ-enabled and mask before return work; synchronous user faults inherit process IRQ state while atomic faults stay masked. The focused regression and its restored-bridge positive control pass, `drv-virtio-blk` passes 30/30, both release targets build, and three one-vCPU Firefox boots prove the completion IRQ wakes the parked task. Process-context softirq drains use each architecture's IRQ stack, matching Linux and keeping both task-stack gates green. | B1872-virtio-blk-direct-wait |
| FIXED c65d199e4, ebbc681be | low | virtio-gpu reused its persistent CTRLQ command frame after a descriptor timeout, allowing a late device reply to be mistaken for the next command's response. | Every runtime submit path now quiesces its owning context on an unretired descriptor; subsequent callers cannot reuse its CTRLQ or CURSORQ frame before teardown's confirmed reset. The direct quiesce regression and all 85 `drv-virtio-gpu` tests pass. | B1904-virtio-gpu-timeout-poison |
| FIXED 91415b8d3 | med | PCI configuration access was limited to the 256-byte conventional header and rewrote an entire dword for every byte/word store, re-emitting neighbouring write-one-to-clear bits. | One shared PCI ECAM contract now exposes the full 4 KiB function window; both architectures issue native byte, word, and dword transactions. The sysfs `config` file carries the same window. PCI has 35 passing tests including a 0x100 read; sysfs has 119 passing tests including end-of-window I/O. | B1905-pci-ext-config |
| FIXED d236c8433 | med | PCIe extended capabilities and Device Serial Number were never decoded into sysfs, despite the full configuration window being available. | The canonical PCI enumeration owner walks the bounded extended-capability chain, captures DSN in `PciIdent`, and publishes `serial_number` only when the value exists. PCI has 36 passing tests including a chained DSN record; sysfs and the target-gated PCI boot owner pass. | B1906-pci-dsn-capability |
| FIXED 44edea321 | low | `dma_mask_bits`/`consistent_dma_mask_bits` reported the PCI default 32 for every function because no per-device DMA mask was tracked in the canonical device model. | PCI devices begin at the 32-bit defaults; a bound module driver's streaming and coherent mask changes update that canonical device, and sysfs computes each live mask width. Direct 48/40-bit sysfs and PCI module-bridge tests pass; the old 32-bit expectation is a red positive control. Both architecture smoke boots passed on the first attempt. | B190701-pci-dma-mask |
| FIXED 6f3428564 | med | `resourceN` was a rendered text triple instead of the mmap-able PCI BAR window. | Each resource is now a binary 0600 BAR file whose canonical device resource supplies the raw physical range to the existing device `PhysRange` mmap owner. Memory BARs map with device cache attributes; I/O and unaligned BARs are refused. Sysfs suite (123), both kernel checks, and first-attempt paired smoke passed. | B190801-pci-resource-mmap |

| FIXED 02a25b560 | low | `TCP_INFO` left the activity ages `tcpi_last_data_sent`, `tcpi_last_data_recv`, and `tcpi_last_ack_recv` zero because TCP retained no distinct event clocks. | `TcpConn` owns the three clocks at validated input and successful sequence-consuming output; `TCP_INFO` derives their millisecond ages from the same state. Focused ownership and ABI tests, full net/syscall suites, both feature targets, and both smoke boots passed. | B1826-tcp-info-timing |
| FIXED 8951b67a8 | low | `TCP_INFO` left `tcpi_max_pacing_rate` zero despite `SO_MAX_PACING_RATE` retaining the socket's configured ceiling. | The ABI reads the generic socket-option owner before state-specific projection, preserving unlimited/default and configured ceilings for listeners and connections. | FIXED 7b56347ba | low | `TCP_INFO` reported `tcpi_rcv_mss` from peer advertised receive MSS rather than the locally observed receiver MSS. | `TcpConn` owns the receiver-MSS observation and live receive-window policy hint; the ABI projects that state. | FIXED c26a09cf9 | low | `TCP_INFO` left `tcpi_ato` zero despite TCP owning the adaptive delayed-ACK interval. | Validated payload arrivals update the interval, the delayed-ACK timer consumes it, and the ABI projects the same capped value. | FIXED 42ba52ddb | low | `TCP_INFO` left receiver-window threshold, receiver RTT, and receive-space telemetry absent or reported receive-buffer capacity. | TCP now retains the advertised-window threshold, receiver-window RTT, and application-copy receive-space sample; the ABI projects those canonical receive owners. Focused ownership and ABI tests, full net/syscall suites, both feature targets, and both smoke boots passed. | B1830-tcp-info-receiver-autotuning01 |
| FIXED 1dce293d2 | low | `TCP_INFO` left delivery-rate fields zero despite TCP retaining the acknowledged data needed to form a rate sample. | The canonical transmit owner snapshots delivery state on every emitted segment; ACK processing selects the newest acknowledged snapshot, updates delivered segments and the app-limited sample, and the ABI converts it to bytes per second. Focused ownership and ABI tests, full net/syscall suites, both feature targets, and both smoke boots passed. | B1831-tcp-info-delivery-rate01 |
| FIXED ed0bcb6a6 | low | `TCP_INFO` left busy, receive-window-limited, and send-buffer-limited durations zero despite TCP owning each active send state. | The canonical send admission, output, and ACK paths advance one connection-owned chronograph; the ABI snapshots completed plus active intervals in microseconds. | FIXED 7f7feea19 | low | `TCP_INFO` left `tcpi_pacing_rate` zero despite the TCP output scheduler owning a live cwnd/RTT-derived rate. | The TCB retains its current rate and deadline; canonical application, ACK, and timer output clamp it with the one shared `SO_MAX_PACING_RATE` cell and release queued payload only when eligible. `TCP_INFO` projects that TCB state. | FIXED F799 | med | Physical virtio-net interfaces were projected under `devices/virtual/net`, with no model-parent `device` link, so udev could not derive a transport path or predictable name. | The interface registry now retains its exact driver-model parent; physical interfaces live below that parent at `.../virtioN/net/<if>`, class and uevent paths use the same chain, and `device` resolves to the transport. | FIXED 20f124f24 | med | `SO_PASSSEC`/`SCM_SECURITY` and `SO_PASSPIDFD`/`SCM_PIDFD` were not emitted by the shared receive path; netlink had no `SO_PASSSEC` state. | Unix and netlink retain each queued record's send-time security label; one receive-control owner emits credentials, labels, rights, then pidfds. `SCM_PIDFD` reserves and commits its descriptor only after cmsg copyout. Focused ancillary suites, both target checks, and paired boot smoke passed. | F800-scm-security-pidfd |
| FIXED 9e10a9fe1 | low | `syscalls::socket_control_tests` was recorded as proving nothing about `listen`/`accept` behaviour. | B1715 replaced the source greps with the ungated live `sock_route` owner; its behavioural suite covers `EBADF`, `ENOTSOCK`, `EOPNOTSUPP`, endpoint routing, and positive controls. | FIXED 24a6a7fd6 | low | `netlink_getsockopt_policy::requested_len` is the single live owner for NETLINK `getsockopt`'s decoded `optlen`; it rejects a negative capacity with `EINVAL` before either answer path can produce output. | FIXED 566b906a2 | med | `IPV6_USE_MIN_MTU` was accepted and retained as private socket state although no current socket option handler exists. | The option now returns `ENOPROTOOPT`; unreachable state and get/set plumbing are removed. | FIXED b8c02bbb9 | low | `IPV6_NEXTHOP` was accepted and retained as inert private socket state. | The option now returns `ENOPROTOOPT`; unreachable state and the private route interpretation are removed. IPv6 admission regression and full pre-push gates passed. | B1796-ipv6-nexthop-sticky |
| FIXED 5ca8b9d45 | low | Sticky `IPV6_PKTINFO` was written but never affected IPv6 transmit or got read back. | UDP, raw IPv6, and TCP active-open now consume its source/interface with explicit per-message or scope state taking precedence. `getsockopt` returns the stored `in6_pktinfo`; focused precedence and payload tests plus full pre-push gates passed. | B1795-ipv6-pktinfo-sticky |
| FIXED fefe15763 | med | An unbound UDP send or connect could allocate any ephemeral source port without checking the Landlock UDP bind rule. | The shared UDP autobind lifecycle now checks a synthetic bind to port zero before allocation, covering both send and connect while leaving already-bound sockets alone. | FIXED a74ddeb75 | med | `TCP_DEFER_ACCEPT` dropped the completion ACK of a passive fast-open child, leaving an already accepted connection in SYN-RECV for the defer period. | Request deferral now bypasses a child marked fast-open before testing its bare ACK, matching the reference's fast-open ordering. `a_fast_open_child_is_published_once_and_gives_its_charge_back_on_completion` covers the combined configuration and failed before the change; fast-open and defer-accept suites plus full pre-push gates passed. | B1793-tcp-defer-accept-fastopen |
| FIXED 3a4fca1d6 | med | A valid fast-open SYN carrying data and FIN delivered the data but dropped FIN, leaving the child in SYN-RECV and its acknowledgement short of the peer's FIN. | The passive fast-open path now advances `rcv_nxt` for FIN and applies the ordinary `SynRecv` FIN transition to CLOSE-WAIT. `a_fast_open_syn_fin_delivers_data_then_enters_close_wait` checks delivery, state, and acknowledgement accounting; the focused test failed before the implementation and passes after it. Full pre-push gates passed. | B1792-tcp-fastopen-syn-fin |
| FIXED 724771cb7 | low | `IP_MULTICAST_ALL` defaulted on, but IPv4 had no interface-level multicast admission, so an unjoined group could reach a socket before its per-socket policy ran. | IPv4 ingress now consults the canonical per-interface group state before every protocol fanout, with aggregate source filtering and the all-hosts, IGMP, and unspecified-source exceptions. `ipv4_multicast_host_admission_requires_interface_membership` failed before the gate and passes after it; `ipv4_multicast_source_filter_drops_denied_udp_source` proves a denied source does not reach an unrelated default-delivery socket. | FIXED 22a159aae | med | `IP_UNICAST_IF` was accepted and read back but did not constrain IPv4 unicast route or source selection. | IPv4 transmit now resolves the stored namespace-local ifindex after `SO_BINDTODEVICE`, then passes it to the existing route owner. `ipv4_unicast_if_resolves_the_namespace_index_and_yields_to_bind_device` covers namespace lookup and binding precedence; full pre-push gates passed. | B1788-ipv4-unicast-if |
| FIXED 79c43c444 | low | An unset `IP_TTL` was flattened to 64 before route selection, bypassing a route's IPv4 hoplimit metric. | Linux resolves socket TTL first, then route hoplimit, then the default.
