| FIXED e2f08bc81 | low | The `tcp_fastopen_key` sysctl text parser rejected hexadecimal groups longer than eight digits, while Linux `%x` consumes the entire group and stores its low 32 bits. | Linux `sscanf_key` uses `%x` into `u32`; Oxide now consumes every hex digit with `wrapping_shl`, matching truncation. `oversized_hex_groups_wrap_like_linux_sscanf_percent_x` passes, along with full pre-push gates. | B1784-tcp-fastopen-sysctl-overflow |
| FIXED b0d961b1a | med | `classify_arm_abort` sent every non-translation data/instruction abort through demand paging as a protection fault, including external aborts, ECC/parity errors, alignment faults, and reserved syndromes. | Linux dispatches only translation, access-flag, and permission DFSCs to the VM fault path; other syndromes preserve their architecture-specific signal path. Oxide now accepts `0x04..=0x0f` only, with translation distinct from access/permission. Aarch64 kernel check and full pre-push gates passed. | B1782-arm-abort-classification |
| FIXED 050d1239a | low | An AF_INET6 raw socket reached the SOL_IP tables, so `IP_ROUTER_ALERT` could set IPv4 option state without an IPv4 Router Alert chain slot. | Linux `ipv6_{set,get}sockopt` delegates SOL_IP only when `sk_type != SOCK_RAW`; raw IPv6 receives `ENOPROTOOPT`. Both raw option dispatchers now reject the entire SOL_IP level for `Raw6`; `cargo check -p syscalls` and full pre-push gates passed. | B1780-raw6-router-alert |
| FIXED 1a1b14c3e | low | `connect(AF_UNSPEC)` on an unconnected TCP socket returned `EINVAL` where the reference returns 0. | `SockKind::TcpInit` now maps to an explicit successful disconnect no-op, matching `tcp_disconnect` on a `TCP_CLOSE` socket. Kernel-target regression test added; `cargo check -p net` and full pre-push gates passed. | B1778-tcp-unspec-disconnect |
| FIXED a0e144fc5 | med | `IPV6_JOIN_ANYCAST` / `IPV6_LEAVE_ANYCAST` routed to the multicast helper, leaving no distinct anycast socket state, device refcount, local-input ownership, or acquisition capability screen. | Separate socket and per-device anycast ownership now drives local delivery and NDP response; close and interface removal release it. Acquisition requires CAP_NET_ADMIN in the socket namespace; leave remains available to an inherited socket. `cargo test -p net anycast`; full branch gate passed. | B1776 |
| FIXED a0e144fc5 | med | The anycast pair was listed as declared in UAPI but absent from the IPv6 socket-option set path. | The IPv6 option dispatcher now imports `ipv6_mreq` and calls the dedicated anycast owner for both option numbers. | B1776 |
| FIXED 4d0724171 | high | NetworkManager re-applied eth0's IPv4 address every four seconds because `RTM_NEWADDR` discarded `IFA_BROADCAST`, ignored `IFA_PROTO` and `IFA_RT_PRIORITY`, and readback synthesized a different address record. A companion C272 row reported unaged DHCP lifetimes. | B1748 made the `net::iface_addr` record canonical for every field, preserves only caller-supplied optional address attributes, stamps installation time and ages preferred/valid lifetimes on readback. `rtnetlink_tests::addr_fields` covers new-address input, canonical storage, dumps, omitted attributes, and lifetime semantics. | B1748 |
| FIXED a80571e94 | high | `drv-virtio-net` kept a second IPv6 neighbour cache and emitted its own solicitations when transmit lacked an L2 destination. It could disagree with the canonical IPv4 ARP state and dropped the first packet on a miss. | B1754 moved both families to the canonical `net::neigh` state machine and pending queue, attaches the resolved MAC before dispatching the retained packet, and removes driver-owned learned state. `the_driver_resolves_no_unicast_neighbour_of_its_own` proves an unknown unicast has no driver answer; `arp_miss_leaves_solicitation_to_net_stack` proves it emits no driver solicitation. | B1754 |
| FIXED B1765 | high | `RTM_GETADDR`, `RTM_GETNEIGH`, and `RTM_GETRULE` accepted ordinary requests and returned multipart dumps even though they have no one-shot handler. A caller could mistake the first dump row for the requested object. | B1765 gates those three types at the route-netlink dispatcher on `NLM_F_DUMP`; the non-dump form now returns `EOPNOTSUPP`. `dump_only_get_requests_are_rejected_before_the_dump_builder` exercises the public socket path for all three. `RTM_GETROUTE` remains a one-shot lookup because it has a distinct handler. | B1765 |
| FIXED B1767 | low | `listen(2)` on an AF_NETLINK socket returned `EOPNOTSUPP` before network-security admission ran, so a deny policy was invisible. | B1767 gives the canonical netlink owner a `listen` operation that admits first and then returns its unsupported-operation errno. `listen_admits_before_its_unsupported_operation_errno` proves `EOPNOTSUPP` normally and `EACCES` under a denial. | B1767 |
| FIXED 125bd3848 | med | `napi::skb_page_frag_refill` allocated a raw buffer, discarded it, returned success, and left its `struct page_frag` argument untouched. Every call leaked and no caller received a fragment to reuse. | B1770 now stores the compatible `page`, `offset`, and `size` state, resets exclusive ownership, reuses borrowed capacity, and drops the fragment's reference before replacement. `page_frag_refill_populates_and_reuses_the_page_fragment` covers all three paths; 222 `modules` tests, both kernel architectures, and serial-only smokes passed before merge. | B1770 |
| FIXED ff7fdebd3 | med | Virtio ring-pointer proofs across blk/gpu/rng/vsock/snd/net depended on a queue-size cap hidden in the queue programmer. A bad handoff could still license stores outside the one frame backing each split-ring area. | B1772 makes `MAX_QUEUE_SIZE` the canonical `VirtQueueResource::is_runtime_valid` contract, so all driver `require_queue` consumers reject oversized rings. Tests prove descriptor, avail, and used areas fit one frame and reject an oversized handoff; all 57 `virtio` tests and the full pre-push gate passed. | B1772 |
| FIXED 566807b61 | med | Virtio drivers accepted queues too small for their published descriptor ids. | B1774 adds `require_queue_at_least`; VSOCK requires 8 RX descriptors, blk 3, GPU 4 control/2 cursor, and sound 2 control/3 PCM. 58 virtio tests, affected-driver checks, and the full pre-push gate passed. | B1774 |
| FIXED dcf0e7dbf | high | `systemd-resolved` allocated no DNS scope during boot despite a valid link, DHCP address, DNS server and route. The stale B1760/B1749/B1738/C275/D476 rows all described this one defect from different probes. | B1761 identified the Linux ABI omission: multicast delivery discarded `NETLINK_CB(skb).dst_group` and `recvmsg` emitted no `SOL_NETLINK` / `NETLINK_PKTINFO` cmsg. systemd-resolved enables that option and dispatches its route callbacks by packet-info group, so it classified every notification as group zero. Queueing and copyout now preserve the group. Serial-only x86 verification reports `Current Scopes: DNS LLMNR/IPv4` and `Current DNS Server: 10.0.2.3` without restarting resolved; x86_64 and aarch64 kernel checks pass. | B1761 |
| FIXED 068551621, dcf0e7dbf | high | Name resolution was reported as still failing through the `127.0.0.53` systemd-resolved stub after DHCP had supplied `10.0.2.3`. | The original observation predated the loopback-bind fix and netlink packet-info fix. B1768 serial-only probe on current main ran `getent hosts one.one.one.one` inside the guest and returned `2606:4700:4700::1001` and `2606:4700:4700::1111`; it therefore traversed the guest resolver path successfully. | B1768 |
| FIXED B1655 | med | `TCP_ZEROCOPY_RECEIVE` returned ENOPROTOOPT. Needed mm-side page mapping into the caller's address space. | B1655. `mmap(2)` on a TCP socket fd now builds a receive window (read-only, `EPERM` on write/exec, `ENODEV` for every other socket); the option remaps whole receive-queue pages into it. Operand layout, optlen versioning, errno ordering, copy-buffer fallback and straggler, `length`/`recv_skip_hint`/`inq`/`err` rules covered by 26 hosted tests in `net::sock_opts::sol_tcp::zerocopy::tests` plus 6 in `syscalls::tcp_zerocopy::window::tests`. | B1655 |

## Security / Landlock

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED 402793b5a | med | Landlock slots 444/445 had no live coverage of fault recovery in their target-gated `copy_from_user` paths; slot 446's live privilege/flag/fd ladder was likewise unproven. | One portable probe now drives cross-page faults through create head, create extension tail, path rule and network rule copies, plus the query/size/tail and restrict-self orderings. Every record matches the Linux oracle on x86_64 and aarch64. Positive control replaced only the ABI-10 guest's faulting head pointer and turned the frame red (`EFAULT` vs `ENOMSG`), then restoration returned green. | B1849-landlock-uaccess-conformance |
| FIXED 402793b5a | low | Landlock's correct ABI pin named external implementation paths instead of limiting repository documentation to the observable compatibility baseline and invariants. | `docs/27§6` now pins Linux 7.2 / Landlock ABI 10 without external implementation paths. The existing code/doc drift test and the new exact in-guest `landlock_create_ruleset(...VERSION)` probe both require ABI 10. | B1849-landlock-uaccess-conformance |
| FIXED 833887264 | low | Landlock erratum 3 was not advertised because the ledger said refer checks omitted the mount-point hierarchy after reaching a directory disconnected from a bind root. | Re-audit found the implementation already carries the reference's two-stage mask walk: `collect_domain_accesses` consumes the disconnected ancestry, then `walk::from` resumes at the saved mount root and crosses its attachment hierarchy. A real refer decision test splits required rights across disjoint trees: both halves allow, either half alone returns `EACCES`; deleting the second walk turns the allow into `EXDEV`. Erratum bit 3 is now reported and locked to `docs/27§6`; 108 Landlock tests and the spec-lint ratchet pass. | B1848-landlock-disconnected-hierarchy |
| FIXED a313bde28 | high | `LANDLOCK_RESTRICT_SELF_TSYNC` directly overwrote sibling domains during one best-effort registry walk, so a concurrent clone escaped the policy and racing TSYNC callers could leave a mixed thread group. | Siblings now run pseudo-signal task work on their own return-to-user path, park at preparation and commit barriers, and are rediscovered until a full scan is empty. TSYNC and exec share one process writer exclusion; contenders restart before mutation. The clone-round and ABI-status positive controls fail with the defect restored; 1,193 scheduler tests, 107 Landlock tests, both kernel builds, and both frame/stack gates pass. | B1847-landlock-tsync-atomic |
| FIXED 4ecabf32d | med | Landlock's target ABI was not pinned independently of the kernel's `uname(2)` release, and the live ledger still claimed ABI 8 after the implementation had advanced to ABI 10. | `docs/27§6` pins Linux 7.2 / Landlock ABI 10, records every cumulative rung and open mismatch, and names `uapi::TARGET_ABI_VERSION` as the code owner. A regression test reads the documented target and fails on drift; its red/green positive control passed. | B1846-landlock-abi-10-contract |

## Filesystem / mount

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED 71f64eb8c | low | cgroupfs control-file inode numbers packed `(cgid << 8) \| slot` into a bounded region, so cgroup IDs at or above `2^19` reused control-file inode numbers and exported handles. | The hierarchy now owns one checked inode sequence shared by directory and control-file nodes; exported handles resolve through that live node state. The `2^19` boundary regression, 83 cgroup tests, 1,235 syscall tests, and paired smoke passed. | B1918-cgroup-inode-range |
| FIXED a8eabf96f | high | The IPv4 route-MIB regression module was compiled into release kernels instead of only the hosted test binary, so the release warning gate rejected its test-only imports and constants before a smoke boot could begin. | `tests_ipv4_route_mib` is now test-gated like its sibling regression modules. Its focused regression passes; it restores the release build needed by the paired smoke. | B1902-mount-option-consumption |
| FIXED 4311ba003 | med | tmpfs retained both `size=` and `nr_blocks=` while parsing mount data, so `resolve_blocks` always preferred `size=` even when a later `nr_blocks=` supplied the final block limit. | Parsing now clears the earlier representation whenever either block-limit spelling is accepted. `last_block_limit_option_wins` covers both orderings; focused tmpfs mount-option tests pass and `cargo test -p fs --lib --no-fail-fast` passes 960 tests. | B1902-mount-option-consumption |
| FIXED 1f70f7a94, bb3d58489, 1178ab29a, 51f9f6709, 9d8eee8e5, fbc37d70f | high | The live ledger still claimed every filesystem silently accepted every unknown option and that proc/cgroup2 confinement options were inert. | Canonical `fs_context` admission now rejects keys outside a filesystem's declared table for both `fsconfig(2)` and `mount(2)`. tmpfs, ramfs, ext4, proc, autofs, fuse, devpts, and cgroup2 publish tables; proc consumes `gid`/`hidepid`/`subset`, and cgroup2 consumes its flags. Focused VFS, procfs, cgroup, tmpfs, and ext4 suites pass. The 13 tableless filesystems and accepted-but-unconsumed tmpfs/ext4 options remain separately open. | B1856-reconcile-fs-param-ledger |
| FIXED 9d8eee8e5 | high | devpts discarded every mount option, so `uid=`, `gid=`, `mode=`, `ptmxmode=`, `max=`, and `newinstance` were not enforced. | devpts declares and enforces the complete reference parameter table. Hosted mount/stamping tests and both guest boots verify the boot-time `gid=5,mode=620,ptmxmode=000` contract. | B1721-devpts-mount-options |
| FIXED 9d8eee8e5 | med | devpts `mode=` and `ptmxmode=` were not parsed with Linux's octal `fsparam_u32oct` semantics. | Modes are parsed as octal, permission-masked, and rendered in round-trippable octal form; focused positive and masking tests pass. | B1721-devpts-mount-options |
| FIXED db7e0d4cf | med | `DevptsFs` was process-global, so separate devpts mounts shared one root, option set, and PTY namespace. | Every mount now owns its `DevptsFs`, root, immutable mount options, and reusable index allocator; `/dev/ptmx` resolves the instance through the opened mount exactly as Linux does. Hosted multi-mount tests and both guest boots pass. | B1845-devpts-per-mount-instances |
| FIXED db7e0d4cf | low | `max=` bounded a monotonic lifetime allocation total rather than the number of live PTYs. | Each devpts instance owns a reusable bitmap allocator; teardown returns the index and `max=` bounds concurrent live pairs. The reuse positive control fails when freeing is disabled and passes after restoration. | B1845-devpts-per-mount-instances |
| FIXED db7e0d4cf | low | PTY slave ownership and mode stamping had hosted tests but no in-guest allocation probe. | The dedicated x86_64 and aarch64 smoke target allocates a real PTY and verifies the slave reports mode 620 and gid 5; both arches pass. | B1845-devpts-per-mount-instances |
| FIXED 51f9f6709 | low | `mount -t proc -o hidepid=…` / `-o subset=…` had been recorded as returning `EINVAL` instead of applying its confinement. | Procfs declares and consumes the `gid`, `hidepid`, and `subset` mount parameters into per-mount state; root lookup, readdir, and PID access enforce that state. Current hosted parser and mount-admission regressions pass. | D498-procfs-hidepid-subset-ledger |
| FIXED 0c6257856 | med | `read(2)` on an fscontext fd had been recorded as returning `EINVAL` while its diagnostic ring was unreachable. | Context inodes use dedicated file operations: each read returns the oldest newline-terminated message, an empty ring yields `ENODATA`, and a short buffer yields `EMSGSIZE` without consuming the message. `cargo test -p vfs --test fs_context_log` passes all 9 regressions. | D499-fscontext-read-error-log-ledger |
| FIXED a96f86b2c | med | Core-dump pipe writes did not stop when the dumper was frozen. | The core-dump wait rule now stops for freezer state or a new fatal kill, while ignoring the delivered signal that initiated the dump. Hosted pipe decision coverage and the 939-test FS suite pass. | D500-pipe-freezer-write-abort-ledger |
| FIXED f28f74eca | med | Core-dump mapping metadata had been recorded as naming only a backing inode rather than a self-contained path. | `FileBacking` carries the mapper's path and core-dump planning emits it with the mapping offset into `NT_FILE`; focused walk tests prove named and nameless backing behavior. | D501-coredump-vma-file-path-ledger |
| FIXED 74cbee0fd | med | `POR_EL0` was not saved in or restored from the aarch64 signal frame. | The feature-gated POE record now preserves the overlay across signal delivery and sigreturn; malformed, wrong-sized, disabled-feature, and duplicate records are rejected. Focused ARM signal tests pass. | D502-arm64-por-el0-signal-frame-ledger |
| FIXED 118603986 | low | `AT_HWCAP2`'s `HWCAP2_POE` bit was not published, so a userspace library probing permission-overlay support saw nothing even once the overlay was on. | The canonical CPU-to-exec auxiliary-vector path now emits `AT_HWCAP2`; bit 63 appears only after the EL0 permission-overlay ABI is enabled. Ordinary exec, usermode helpers, and both smoke loaders carry the same word. Hosted ARM capability tests, the full pre-push gate, and post-merge UART smokes passed. | D503-arm64-poe-hwcap2-ledger |
| FIXED dc170eb04 | med | A valid `POE_MAGIC` record placed in a signal frame's permitted `extra_context` area was accepted but its `POR_EL0` value was discarded on `rt_sigreturn`. | Restore now selects the POE record from the same primary-or-extension region scan as the FPSIMD record, while retaining cross-region duplicate rejection. Focused ARM signal tests, the full pre-push gate, and post-merge UART smokes passed. | D504-arm64-poe-extra-context-restore-ledger |

| FIXED 2b44a8a29 | high | **No architecture-neutral write-combining policy existed; x86 programmed no PAT and arm64 exposed no Normal-NC leaf policy, so a physical framebuffer could only be mapped strongly uncached.** | x86 now programs one Linux-compatible PAT policy on the BSP and every AP, including the legacy Intel errata layout and correct 4 KiB/large-leaf encodings. arm64 MAIR owns Normal-NC AttrIdx2. Raw-PFN VMAs carry one driver-selected write-back, write-combining, or device policy through the production fault path; PMM-backed virtio scanout remains write-back while physical framebuffer drivers can select WC without a second registry. Focused suites pass 155 x86 HAL, 149 arm64 HAL, 337 VMM, and 29 fbdev tests; paired release smoke passed first attempt, and Firefox run `2088783` rendered valid/invalid pages in 4.950/1.461 s at 0.153-0.155 us kernel time per syscall. Measurements are retained in `scratch/write-combining-performance-20260806.md`. | B1874-x86-write-combining |
| FIXED F822 | med | A private file mapping's COW pages had no anonymous reverse-map owner, so the default core filter could reduce modified data to an identifying header page. | Private COW installation now creates and attaches the VMA's canonical anonymous rmap owner, records the new page there, and marks VMA-owned private-page history. VMM and coredump selection regressions cover the classification. | F822-coredump-mapping-classification |
| FIXED F822 | med | io_uring and aio ring mappings backed by a kernel frame classified as private memory with no backing and were omitted from core dumps. | The canonical KernelFrame backing is classified as its shared mapped object class; the coredump adapter regression pins the mapped-shared filter decision. | F822-coredump-mapping-classification |
| FIXED F822 | low | An anonymous mapping became indistinguishable from untouched memory after all resident pages were evicted, so the default filter omitted it. | VMA-owned anonymous-page history, rather than RSS, now drives the private filter class and survives reclaim. | F822-coredump-mapping-classification |
| FIXED F823 | med | A `PT_LOAD` boundary page containing both file bytes and `.bss` was split into kernel-owned bytes, so it no longer classified as file-backed. | The VMA retains the whole file-backed boundary page; its canonical per-mapping backing delegates object identity and returns zeroes past the segment's file extent. Focused loader regressions cover the mapping boundary and byte contents. | F823-elf-load-bss-boundary-mapping |
| FIXED F824 | med | A position-independent image with no interpreter kept kernel-owned backing for every segment because the loader applied its relative relocations before entry. | The loader now maps untouched executable file bytes and transfers control; the no-interpreter position-independent entry path owns its own relocation. A biased file-backed PIE regression pins the mapping class. | F824-elf-pie-self-relocation |
| FIXED F825 | low | Later `NT_PRSTATUS` records repeated the process CPU-time snapshot, losing each worker thread's user and system CPU time. | Each thread snapshot now owns its `utime`/`stime`; the live capture reads the group sample for a leader, own counters for a non-leader, and shared child accounting for `cutime`/`cstime`. The two-thread note regression proves the records differ where required. | F825-coredump-thread-times |
| FIXED F826 | med | x86-64 core dumps emitted no `NT_X86_XSTATE` note even though the note assembler supported one. | Live capture synchronizes the canonical task FPU buffer once, emits its leading legacy region as `NT_PRFPREG`, and emits the complete XSAVE image unchanged as `NT_X86_XSTATE` when extended state is active. The note regression pins the complete descriptor and its header bytes. Hosted coredump tests, both feature targets, and paired smoke passed. | F826-coredump-x86-xstate |
| FIXED F828 | med | The socket (`@`) destination delivered nothing. A pattern beginning with `@` silently produced no dump and no file. | Direct socket patterns now validate and expand one absolute pathname, resolve its socket inode from the initial mount namespace, connect through the initial AF_UNIX owner, send the complete image without SIGPIPE, then shut down the write side. Hosted pathname validation and both kernel target gates pass. | F828-coredump-socket-delivery |
| FIXED F829 | med | The `@@` coredump-socket request/ack protocol was absent, so a collector could not select kernel generation, userspace generation, rejection, or an exit wait. | The socket ABI owner now emits the 16-byte feature request, validates size, spare, supported mask, and exactly one primary owner, then returns the required 32-bit marker. Negotiation runs before image construction, so userspace and reject choices build no kernel image; kernel/userspace choices shut down write and honor wait. Direct `@` wait follows `core_pipe_limit`. Ten hosted protocol regressions, a red/green positive control, both kernel builds, feature/frame/task-stack/IRQ gates, and per-crate host/test builds pass. | F829-coredump-socket-request-ack |

Retired to CLAUDE.md as standing hard rules (`Conflict resolution is where
coverage dies`, `Verification must be able to fail`) — a lesson that lives only
in a ledger row is one nobody reads before the next merge.

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

| FIXED 611f4df7b | high | Three concurrent futex tests drove one process-global fake clock, allowing one test to move another's deadline backwards and park it forever after a spurious wake. | A guard serializes the clock-driving tests and resets time on acquisition while remaining held across spawned waiters. | FIXED 39e78b9de | low | Two `/proc/<pid>` inode-number tags collided: the projected-id map file and the personality file shared tag `0x2e`, so the two files of one process shared an inode number. | `projid_map` now uses an unused tag while `personality` retains `0x2e`; the shared tag constants and a hosted regression prove distinct inode identities across representative PIDs. `cargo test -p procfs --lib` passed (163 tests), along with the full pre-push gates. | D494-procfs-pid-inode-tag-collision-ledger |
| FIXED D439 | med | Two lanes splitting one file for the size cap along different axes produces a conflict where the STALE side looks plausible. B1641 and B1649 both split `procfs/ctl.rs`; B1649's copy of the `net` subtree predated B1641 making `rmem_default`/`wmem_default` live and adding `tcp_rmem`/`tcp_wmem`, so resolving line-by-line would have silently reverted two leaves to dead `Const` and deleted two more, with nothing red. Rule: take the other side wholesale and re-apply your own delta; verify by MULTISET count, not name set. | B1649 merge of `27cdb991f`; 100 declarations both sides, none dropped, none duplicated. | D439 |
| FIXED D439 | med | Verification that returns green without exercising what it claims to. Two confirmed instances: `procfs/ctl.rs` is target-gated so `cargo check` compiled none of it (break appeared only in the kernel build); a set-based duplicate-test check that structurally could not detect duplicates (a set dedupes the very thing being looked for). Prefer a positive control — reinstate the defect and confirm the check goes red. | B1641. | D439 |

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED F777 | high | Four `RLIMIT_*` defaults were `RLIM_INFINITY` where Linux's `INIT_RLIMITS` (after `fork_init`'s two `max_threads/2` overwrites) sets them otherwise: `NICE` and `RTPRIO` are `{0,0}`, `MSGQUEUE` is `{819200,819200}`, `NPROC` is `{max_threads/2, ...}`. NICE/RTPRIO are the load-bearing pair — the `setpriority(2)` and `sched_setscheduler(2)` ladders read exactly those slots and are written correctly, so an infinite default made both structurally unable to refuse anything and any unprivileged process could take nice -20 or SCHED_FIFO 99 with no `CAP_SYS_NICE`. A procfs test had pinned the wrong MSGQUEUE value as if it were correct. | F777. 16-slot `INIT_RLIMITS` conformance table in `sched::tests::rlimit_prio`, plus a `nice_to_rlimit` allowance test proving the default admits no priority increase; procfs render test repointed. | F777 |
| FIXED F777 | high | `setrlimit(2)` and `prlimit64(2)` gated the hard-limit RAISE on `has_cap(CAP_SYS_RESOURCE)` — a plain effective-set test — where Linux asks `capable()` = `ns_capable(&init_user_ns, ...)`. Root inside an unprivileged user namespace could therefore hand itself any hard limit after one `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)`. `perm_common::capable` already encoded the correct test and neither slot called it: machinery without callers. | F777. Both slots now route through the ungated `rlimit_policy::cap_sys_resource`; two new tests drive that exact helper over a real `sched::Task` moved into and out of a non-initial user namespace. | F777 |
| FIXED b8d233ca8 | med | `TCP_DEFER_ACCEPT` completed the handshake and withheld the child from `accept`, so the peer observed ESTABLISHED while its bare acknowledgement should have left the request half-open. | A listener now keeps a SYN-RECV request socket outside the accept queue; a bare acknowledgement is deferred, while data or the final solicited acknowledgement promotes it. Request-timer and end-to-end defer-accept regressions cover the state and peer-visible handover. | B1656-tcp-defer-accept-minisock |
| FIXED B1656 | high | `net` did not compile for the plain `cargo check -p net` target: `sock_v6_name` is ungated but imports the socket type, which only the kernel, test and hosted configurations have. Fixed by giving the module the same gate as the type it reads — the gate still includes `test`, so nothing that exercised it stops being exercised. | B1656 |
| FIXED F779 | high | Child resource accounting (`getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN)`, `times(2)`'s `tms_cutime`/`tms_cstime`) folded a child into its parent at EXIT rather than at REAP. `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` covers children that terminated AND were waited for, so three things were wrong: an unreaped zombie was already counted; a `waitid(WNOWAIT)` peek left the child waitable but accounted (systemd's peek-then-reap SIGCHLD handler is the real caller); and an auto-reaped child (`SIGCHLD`=`SIG_IGN` / `SA_NOCLDWAIT`) was accounted at all, which upstream never does. | F779 (#4338). Fold moved to the one reap arm that consumes the zombie, gated on thread-group leadership, crediting the reaper. 7 tests in `sched::tests::child_rusage` over the real zombie list; both pre-existing exit-time tests went red against the fix before being rewritten. | F779 |

| FIXED B1685 | med | **`name_to_handle_at` on cgroupfs returned EOVERFLOW**, so the service manager could not read any cgroup id (`Failed to get cgroup ID of cgroup /sys/fs/cgroup/system.slice/<unit>, ignoring: Value too large for defined data type` on every unit start). Root cause: the FID codec was VFS-global — one 12-byte `(ino, generation)` payload for every filesystem — while the cgroup-id reader sizes its buffer to `sizeof(uint64_t)` = 8 and does not run the grow-and-retry protocol, so every call overflowed. Fixed by adding the per-superblock encode/decode half of `s_export_op` (the decode half already existed) and giving cgroup2 the kernfs handle: 8 bytes, `FILEID_KERNFS`, payload = the node id, which is the directory's inode number. | B1663 boot log, ~40 occurrences from `system-getty.slice` onward. Now: 9 hosted tests in `cgroup::export::tests` + 4 in `vfs::export::kernfs_fid::tests`. | B1685 |

| FIXED B1687 | high | **nautilus dies with SIGSEGV** (`exit=139`) at window realisation. Bisected to the GTK4 renderer: `GSK_RENDERER=cairo` -> survives 60 s (`exit=124`), `GSK_RENDERER=ngl` -> survives (`124`), `GSK_RENDERER=vulkan` -> SIGSEGV (`139`). Under `GSK_RENDERER=vulkan` + `VK_LOADER_DEBUG=all` the last line is the loader printing the `vkCreateInstance` layer callstack down to `<Drivers>` — i.e. it dies inside driver instance creation / physical-device enumeration, after all 11 mesa ICD manifests are found. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | high | The Vulkan SIGSEGV needs the FULL mesa ICD set: restricting the loader to one ICD (`VK_DRIVER_FILES=lvp_icd...`) survives, to `virtio_icd` survives, and to `lvp:virtio` together survives (`exit=124` each) — but with all 11 ICDs enumerated it faults (`139`). `NODEVICE_SELECT=1` does NOT help (`139`), so `VK_LAYER_MESA_device_select` is ruled out. The crasher is therefore one of the nine hardware ICDs (asahi, broadcom, freedreno, intel_hasvk, intel, nouveau, panfrost, powervr_mesa, radeon) probing the DRM device, which on a machine with no matching hardware must enumerate zero devices and return cleanly. Suspected kernel side: what `/dev/dri/renderD128` + its sysfs identity (`/sys/dev/char/226:128/device/{subsystem,vendor,device,...}`) report to `drmGetDevices2()`, letting a wrong-vendor ICD claim the node. | B1663 in-guest bisect, all on one boot. Per-ICD single-driver runs partially complete at time of filing. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | high | The GTK4 Vulkan SIGSEGV is caused by ONE mesa ICD, `powervr_mesa` — not by the ICD count and not by the DRM device. ANY pair containing it faults (`lvp+powervr` 139, `panfrost+powervr` 139); pairs without it are clean (`lvp+virtio` 124); the driver ALONE is clean (124). Adding it to a clean set of seven flips that set to 139, adding `panfrost` instead does not. An alternate set of NINE without it survives, so it is not a count threshold. `chmod 000 /dev/dri/renderD128` does NOT prevent the fault, so the render node and every DRM ioctl are ruled out. `NODEVICE_SELECT=1` does not help, so `VK_LAYER_MESA_device_select` is ruled out. | B1663 in-guest bisect: 11 single-ICD runs, five set sizes, an alternate-nine control, two eight-ICD runs, a trio, two pairs, one render-node-denied run. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | med | The "large `PT_TLS`" lead for the `powervr_mesa` SIGSEGV does NOT survive its own follow-up check and must not be treated as the cause. `libpowervr_rogue.so` (4352 B of TLS, faults) and `libvulkan_radeon.so` (48 B, does NOT fault) use the IDENTICAL relocation type — `R_X86_64_TLSDESC`, two vs three of them. Same `dlopen` code path, same lazy `__tls_get_addr` resolution, no initial-exec relocation anywhere, so no static-TLS-surplus failure is involved in either. The libraries differ only in TLS SIZE, and 4352 B is not a large allocation. Anyone picking this up should treat the TLS angle as unconfirmed and start from the faulting `ip` instead. | B1663/B1667, `readelf -rW` on both libraries extracted from the image. Recorded because a negative result here saves the next lane the same detour. | — |
| FIXED B1681 | HIGH | Every pipe-destination dump was silently truncated at the first full ring. The ring was a fixed 4 KiB inline array and the blocking write returned `Erestartsys` as soon as any signal was deliverable — permanently true for the thread the core dumper runs on, since it is inside the delivery of the signal that killed it. This is why `coredumctl list` stayed empty with a valid `core_pattern` and a listening socket. | `pipe::ring::tests::a_fresh_ring_holds_the_default_pipe_size_not_one_page`; ring default now 64 KiB, grown on demand to a 1 MiB ceiling; `WriteAbort::OnFatalKill` gates the dumper's wait on `sched::live::fatal_kill_pending_self` | B1681 |

| FIXED C256 | HIGH | **The N22 conformance channel was measuring our libc, not our kernel.** `xtask glibc-test` called `sysroot::build_sysroot`, which built `crates/user/{glibc,ldso,folded-stub,crt1}` into `target/sysroot/<triple>/lib`, then linked each guest binary `-nostdlib -Wl,--dynamic-linker=/opt/oxide-conformance/lib/ld-linux-* -l:libc.so.6` and shipped that libc into the image. So a guest-vs-oracle mismatch could be a bug in our from-scratch glibc rather than in the kernel, and there was no way to tell which from the result. The guest binary is now an ordinary dynamically-linked PIE with the platform loader path, resolved by the image's own Fedora `ld-linux` + `libc.so.6` — the same pair every other program in the guest uses. | `file t_mmsg.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.guest` → `interpreter /lib64/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2`; aarch64 → `interpreter /lib/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1`; 218/218 oracles recorded + guest binaries linked on both arches | C256 |

All four are gone now that `conformance` no longer builds a sysroot. | 661 LOC deleted; `cargo check -p xtask` clean with `crates/user/ldso` dropped from `tools/xtask/Cargo.toml` | C256 |

| FIXED F789 | high | `F784` appended 66 duplicate `NEEDS-AUDIT` rows to the compliance matrix, one beside each row it wrongly believed absent. The two rows for a number carry disagreeing statuses and any reader takes whichever it reaches first. **`F784`'s premise was false: the matrix tracked all 385 native syscalls the whole time.** Its checker split rows on `|` without honouring the markdown `\|` escape, so it silently skipped every pipe-bearing row and read the absence as "untracked". 65 removed here (row 319 by the `F783` lane). The "matrix tracks 319 of 385" headline is retracted in the file's own header. | F789. Post-fix: 385 rows / 385 distinct numbers, zero duplicates, every row 14 escape-aware columns. Verified no data loss: every one of main's 385 real rows survives with identical status and byte-identical note text after normalising escapes. | F789 |
| FIXED F789 | med | 46 matrix rows carried a bare `\|` inside the notes cell — flag ORs written as prose (`MAP_SHARED\|MAP_ANON`, `O_RDONLY\|O_NONBLOCK`). A bare pipe adds table columns, so the notes spill into phantom cells, every column after the split reads as garbage, and the row renders wrong. This is what made the rows invisible to `F784`'s parser. Escaped; `make matrix-gate` now fails on any new one. | F789. `matrix-lint` reported these as a NOTE before, never a failure. | F789 |
| FIXED F789 | high | **`tools/matrix-lint.py` existed, already printed the exact 65 pipe-bearing rows that `F784` went on to duplicate, and was wired into no gate — so nobody ever saw the output.** It also failed on clean `main` (29 errors) because its hardcoded status list had drifted from the legend it validates against: it lacked `NEEDS-REWORK`, which the legend defines and 15 rows use. A lint that is red on arrival gets dismissed as broken by the first person to wire it up. Statuses are now parsed from the legend table in the matrix itself, so adding one there is sufficient and the list cannot drift again. Wired into `make ci` as `matrix-gate`. | F789. Four positive controls, each isolated to a row that cannot fail for any other reason: duplicate, unescaped pipe, off-legend status, unbackticked status — all four go red naming the right row and invariant, and green on restore. | F789 |

| FIXED F789 | high | The compliance matrix on `main` carries **66 duplicate-numbered rows**: the `F784-matrix-untracked-syscalls` completeness pass appended rows for syscalls that were already tracked instead of reconciling with the existing ones, so 66 slots now have two rows with different `Status` and different `Notes` — e.g. `319:memfd_create` had one `PARTIAL` row and one `NEEDS-AUDIT` row. Any tooling that reads status as awk `$9` gets whichever row it hits first, and the row authority rule ("add/remove/renumber rows only by re-reading `syscall_64.tbl`") is what should have caught it. Reproduction: `awk -F'\|' '{n=$2; gsub(/ /,"",n); if (n ~ /^[0-9]+$/) print n}' scratch/syscall-compliance-matrix.md \| sort -n \; verified matrix duplicates: 0 | uniq -d \| wc -l` → 66. F783 removed only the duplicate `319` row, the one this PR audits; the other 65 belong to the F784 lane. A matrix lint asserting one row per number (and `NF == 14`) would prevent recurrence. | F783; `awk` count above against `origin/main` 5b7cd66a9. | F784 lane |

| FIXED B1687 | high | What is unique about `powervr_mesa`: it is the only ICD whose `DT_NEEDED` list carries `libpowervr_rogue.so`, and that library (present, 9.4 MB) is the only one in the set with a LARGE `PT_TLS` segment — `p_memsz 0x1100` (4352 B) of zero-init TLS. `libvulkan_radeon.so` also has `PT_TLS` but only 0x30 (48 B) and does not fault; `lvp`, `virtio`, `intel`, `panfrost` and `powervr_mesa` itself have none. 4352 B is far past glibc's static-TLS surplus, so this is the one `dlopen` in the whole enumeration that forces the heavy TLS path (static-surplus placement or DTV growth) in an already-multithreaded process. Program-header shape, alignment and `GNU_PROPERTY` are otherwise identical across all of these libraries. | B1663, `readelf -lW` / `readelf -dW` on the libraries extracted from the image. Hypothesis, not yet confirmed against a faulting `ip`. | —; verified root cause was missing PCI sysfs attributes; both apps open |
| FIXED B1687 | med | The static-TLS-surplus explanation for the `powervr_mesa` SIGSEGV is refuted outright, by two independent arguments. (1) That failure shape REQUIRES initial-exec TLS relocations — those are the ones resolved at load time out of the static block, and exhausting it is what makes `dlopen` fail. There are **zero** `R_X86_64_TPOFF64` relocations in ANY of the twelve libraries in the set. Every TLS reference is `R_X86_64_TLSDESC`, i.e. lazy general-dynamic, allocated on demand and never from the surplus, so the surplus is not consumed by these `dlopen`s at all. (2) A controlled comparison: `libvulkan_asahi.so` has the IDENTICAL `PT_TLS` `p_memsz 0x1100` (4352 B) and the same 2 TLSDESC relocations as `libpowervr_rogue.so`, and does NOT fault — it is clean alone and present in every clean seven-ICD set. `libvulkan_freedreno.so` carries a LARGER `PT_TLS` (`0x1160`, 3 TLSDESC) and also does not fault. TLS size is not the discriminator. Separately, the shape does not even match the symptom: static-TLS exhaustion makes `dlopen` return an error the Vulkan loader handles by skipping the ICD, it does not SIGSEGV. | B1663/B1667: `readelf -lW` + `readelf -rW` across all 11 ICDs and `libpowervr_rogue.so`; behavioural control from the single-ICD run matrix. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | high | What remains genuinely unique to `powervr_mesa` after TLS is eliminated: it is the only ICD that pulls an ADDITIONAL large private dependency at `dlopen` time — `libpowervr_rogue.so`, 9.4 MB, which no other ICD needs. Note the split: `libvulkan_powervr_mesa.so` itself has NO `PT_TLS`; the TLS lives in that dependency. So the distinguishing property is "one extra large shared object mapped during ICD enumeration", not anything about TLS. Next step is the faulting `ip`, not more inference. | B1663/B1667, `readelf -dW` across the set. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | blocker | **PCI devices in sysfs do not publish `config`, `revision`, `subsystem_vendor` or `subsystem_device`.** Only `vendor`, `device` and `class` exist. The reference always exposes the config-space blob plus those attributes for every PCI device. This breaks libdrm's `drmGetDevices2()` two-call protocol and kills GNOME Settings and Files. | B1663/B1667. Measured in-guest on `/sys/devices/pci0000:00/0000:00:04.0` (the virtio-gpu): `MISS config`, `MISS revision`, `MISS subsystem_vendor`, `MISS subsystem_device`; `vendor=0x1af4`, `device=0x1050` present. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | blocker | Consequence, measured directly: `drmGetDevices2(0, NULL, 0)` returns **1** but `drmGetDevices2(0, arr, 1)` returns **0** and leaves the caller's slot untouched. libdrm only fetches full device info on the second (array-supplied) pass, so the count-only pass accepts the virtio-gpu and the fetch pass drops it. Five-line reproducer, no GNOME/Vulkan/nautilus needed: `python3 -c 'import ctypes; d=ctypes.CDLL("libdrm.so.2"); n1=d.drmGetDevices2(0,None,0); a=(ctypes.c_void_p*n1)(); print(n1, d.drmGetDevices2(0,a,n1))'` prints `1 0`; the reference prints equal numbers. | B1663/B1667, in-guest. The count/fill mismatch is MEASURED; attributing it to the missing attributes is strong inference from libdrm's fetch-only-when-storing structure, not separately instrumented. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | high | Consequence, the reported crash: mesa's `powervr_mesa` ICD sizes its device loop from the FIRST `drmGetDevices2` count and dereferences the slot the second pass never wrote — allocator garbage. Faulting instruction is deterministic across 3/3 runs: `ino 7949979242215779635` (`libvulkan_powervr_mesa.so`) `off 0x1dd7b`, `mov (%rax,%r13,8),%rbx` / `cmpl $0x2,0xc(%rbx)` with `rbx=0x562d675f1695` (odd, unaligned), `error 0x4` = user-mode read of a non-present page. That kills GTK4's default Vulkan renderer during ICD enumeration, which is why gnome-control-center and nautilus both die. Fix the sysfs attributes and both apps should open. | B1663/B1667 boot, `<comm>[<pid>]: segfault at …` line from the B1667 report, `objdump` against the library extracted from the image. Explains every earlier negative result: not TLS, not the render node (the parse is sysfs-only, never opens it), not the device-select layer, not an ICD count. | — |
| FIXED B1691 | high | **Double free in `linux_pci/registry.rs`.** `bind_model_device` keeps the `LinuxPciDev` in a local `Box` and only relinquishes it via `Box::into_raw` on the success path, but the failure path called `remove_binding`, which did `drop(Box::from_raw(rec.dev))` on that same pointer — then the local `Box` dropped at scope exit and freed it again. Fires whenever a PCI driver's `probe` returns non-zero, i.e. a driver rejecting a device it matched by ID. No test covered probe failure, which is why it was never observed. | Fix: `remove_binding` now only `swap_remove`s the record and leaves ownership with `bind_model_device`'s `Box` (its unsafe block disappears). Single call site. The mirror path `unbind_model_device` was already correct — there the `Box` really was leaked into the table by the success path. | B1691 |
| FIXED B1691 | high | **Unbounded write into the caller's `args` array in `linux_string/match_parser.rs`.** `pattern_match` stored one `substring_t` per `%` conversion in the pattern with no cap. The Linux contract is `substring_t args[MAX_OPT_ARGS]` with `MAX_OPT_ARGS == 3`, and `match_one` returns no-match as soon as `argc >= MAX_OPT_ARGS`. A token-table pattern with 4+ conversions wrote past a conforming caller's 3-element array. | Fix is Linux-shaped: `const MAX_OPT_ARGS: usize = 3;` plus `if ai >= MAX_OPT_ARGS { return false; }` before the store. Existing test (1 conversion) still passes; `cargo test -p modules` 191 passed / 0 failed. | B1691 |
| FIXED B1691 | med | **Safe `fn`s dereferencing caller-supplied raw pointers.** A safe fn carrying a memory-safety precondition is unsound by Rust's definition, and the crate's `#![forbid(unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn)]` cannot see it. Converted to `unsafe fn` + `// Precondition:` (all call sites were already inside `unsafe fn`s, so no behaviour changed): `linux_string/parse.rs` `skip_ws`/`only_ws_or_nul`/`eq_nocase`; `linux_string/format.rs` `push_cstr`; `linux_usb/core.rs` `bind_interface`/`bind_driver_to_interface`/`id_matches`; `linux_block/core.rs` `disk_name`; `linux_block/mq.rs` `alloc_request`/`apply_limits`/`mq_ops`/`mq_ops_from_set`; `linux_alloc.rs` `valid_page`; `linux_pci/registry.rs` `copy_driver_name`; `linux_netdev/alloc.rs` `ensure_registered_name`; `linux_netdev/skb.rs` `skb_data`. | `cargo check -p modules --tests` clean; `cargo test -p modules` 191 passed. | B1691 |
| FIXED B1692 | HIGH | `program_queue` used the DEVICE-supplied `queue_size` unchecked while allocating exactly ONE frame per ring area. A device advertising more descriptors than a frame holds (QEMU accepts `queue-size=1024` on virtio-blk) makes every driver's own descriptor/avail/used store run off the end of that frame — a kernel heap wild write whose value comes from the ring index. Now renegotiated down per Virtio 1.2 §4.1.4.3 and the queue refused if the device does not accept. | `virtio::queue_cfg::tests::oversized_device_queue_size_is_negotiated_down_to_one_frame`; positive control: with the clamp disabled the test FAILS (`ring.size` 1024 vs 256), with it enabled it passes | B1692 |

The doc on `reset_device` says in as many words that an unconfirmed reset means freeing is unsafe. On an unconfirmed reset this returned a physical address the device still lists as resource backing to the buddy allocator. | `post_init::release::tests::an_unconfirmed_reset_releases_nothing_so_the_device_keeps_its_frames`; positive control: with the reset gate removed from `releasable_dma` that test FAILS, with it present it passes | B1692 |

| FIXED B1692 | MED | virtio-gpu probe freed the command frame after a CTRLQ poll TIMEOUT — i.e. exactly when the descriptor is still device-owned and the device may still write the reply into it. | FIXED B1692 | HIGH | DRM ioctls dereferenced USER pointers raw (`read_volatile(arg as *const T)`) behind nothing but a range check. A range check proves the address is in the user half, not that it is mapped readable; such a dereference from kernel mode has no exception-table entry, so the fault handler finds no VMA, `handled=false`, and the CPU OOPSes and halts — an unprivileged local DoS via any bogus ioctl pointer. It also imposed the struct's alignment on a pointer userspace chose. Converted `dumb/ioctl.rs`, `node/atomic_ioctl.rs` and `kms_ext.rs` cursor paths to a new `drm::uarg` that goes through `uaccess::copy_{from,to}_user` (exception-table-backed) and `read_unaligned`. | `drm::uarg::tests::a_null_user_pointer_is_reported_not_dereferenced`, `…::every_drm_ioctl_arg_struct_fits_the_transfer_buffer`; `crates/kernel/uaccess/src/copy.rs` vs `hal-x86_64/src/fault.rs:229` (fixup only for exception-table RIPs) | B1692 |
| FIXED B1692 | HIGH | `MODE_ATOMIC` summed the per-object property counts in `atomic_property_count`, range-checked `props_ptr`/`prop_values_ptr` against that total, then RE-READ each per-object count from the same user array in the copy loop. Userspace rewriting `count_props[]` between the two passes walked the flattened index past the checked extent, reading `props_ptr + pos*4` outside it — a user-controlled out-of-range read whose values reach `atomic::commit`. The running total is now re-checked per object. | `node::atomic_ioctl::tests::a_count_grown_since_validation_is_refused` + `an_overflowing_running_total_is_refused`; positive control: deleting the `end > count` guard makes the first FAIL | B1692 |
| FIXED B1692 | HIGH | virtio-input's evdev ioctl helpers (`uwrite`/`uread`/`uzero`/`uread_i32`/`uread_u32`) dereferenced user pointers raw behind `valid_user_range`, which checks only `0 < arg` and `arg+len <= USER_VA_END`. Same kernel-oops-on-bad-pointer defect as the DRM row. `EVIOCGMASK` was the worst case: its zero-fill tail loops over a user-supplied `codes_size`, up to 4 GiB of raw stores. All five now go through `uaccess` and return `EFAULT`; every `unsafe` in `devfs/ioctl.rs` and `ioctl/mask.rs` is gone. | 114 input tests still pass through the new path; both kernel targets compile. NOTE: no positive control is achievable hosted — the hosted `uaccess` shim is a plain `copy_nonoverlapping` and `access_ok` enforces the same predicate as `valid_user_range`, so hosted behaviour is identical before and after. The fault-recovery gain is verified by construction, not by a red-to-green test. | B1692 |
| FIXED B1692 | HIGH | virtio-rng `uninstall` freed `bounce_pa` without clearing it. `fill`/`fill_from_device` clone the `Arc` out of `RNGS` and drop the registry lock before taking the record lock, so a clone winning the record lock after the free programs a freed frame as a device-WRITE descriptor and kicks the device — DMA into a recycled frame. `disarm_and_free` now zeroes it under the record lock before freeing; the same window in `install`'s publish-failure path and in `shutdown` is closed too. | `uninstall_disarms_a_handle_cloned_before_removal`; positive control: reverting to a plain read+free makes it FAIL | B1692 |

Both sites now call `virtio::reset_device`, which refuses zero and polls the status read-back. | `uninstall_without_a_config_window_writes_nothing`; positive control: restoring the raw store kills the test binary with SIGSEGV | B1692 |
| FIXED B1692 | MED | virtio-snd `install` ran `prepost_eventq` — which publishes WRITE descriptors over `event_buf_pa` and kicks the device — BEFORE the duplicate-key check that exists for a same-key install race. | FIXED F790 | HIGH | **`make smoke-drm-render` could never pass, and the reason was invisible.** The probe fetched counted DRM arrays into FIXED 16-slot buffers. | FIXED F789 | high | **The syscall compliance matrix tracks only 319 of 385 real x86_64 syscalls — 66 are absent entirely**, so every coverage statistic derived from it (`IMPL`/`PARTIAL` counts, "what is left") is computed over 83% of the surface and silently understates it. So this is a TRACKING gap, not a code gap, which is the dangerous kind: a lane assigned "close the PARTIAL rows for clone/execve" finds no such rows and may conclude the work is done. That happened on this lane — 4 of 10 assigned rows did not exist. Untracked: 0:read, 2:open, 9:mmap, 15:rt_sigreturn, 22:pipe, 26:msync, 28:madvise, 29:shmget, 35:nanosleep, 45:recvfrom, 48:shutdown, 56:clone, 59:execve, 64:semget, 65:semop, 67:shmdt, 68:msgget, 69:msgsnd, 70:msgrcv, 72:fcntl, 73:flock, 82:rename, 85:creat, 94:lchown, 101:ptrace, 117:setresuid, 126:capset, 132:utime, 141:setpriority, 149:mlock, 153:vhangup, 157:prctl, 161:chroot, 167:swapon, 188:setxattr, 189:lsetxattr, 190:fsetxattr, 203:sched_setaffinity, 216:remap_file_pages, 218:set_tid_address, 219:restart_syscall, 230:clock_nanosleep, 233:epoll_ctl, 240:mq_open, 241:mq_unlink, 242:mq_timedsend, 243:mq_timedreceive, 244:mq_notify, 245:mq_getsetattr, 248:add_key, 249:request_key, 254:inotify_add_watch, 264:renameat, 272:unshare, 279:move_pages, 288:accept4, 289:signalfd4, 290:eventfd2, 293:pipe2, 294:inotify_init1, 309:getcpu, 316:renameat2, 318:getrandom, 319:memfd_create, 322:execveat, 452:fchmodat2. Recommended fix: add the 66 rows mechanically (number, ABI, name, and slot path are all derivable from the dispatch table) with an honest status meaning "implemented, never audited" — introducing a new status value touches column 9, which tooling parses, so it needs a coordinator decision before anyone writes it. | Computed against the Linux x86_64 syscall numbering (`common`+`64` ABIs) vs. column 1 of the matrix, at `origin/main` 429775d38. | FIXED B1704 | high | **The `request_key(2)` construction never completed on a booted system** because the assumed instantiation token did not survive the helper's own credential transitions. `/sbin/request-key` assumes authority over the key, EXECS the handler its configuration names, and that handler FORKS `keyctl instantiate` to answer. The exec hook DROPPED the token and the fork hook never COPIED it, so the program that finally answers held no authority and `instantiate_core` returned EPERM — the helper exited non-zero and the key was left under construction, then negated. Neither hook is what the reference does: `prepare_exec_creds` touches only the thread and process keyrings, and `prepare_creds` takes a reference to the same token for the child. | `keyring/lifecycle.rs` `fork`/`exec`; four positive controls, each reinstating one defect, go RED. | this lane |
| FIXED B1704 | high | Second half of the same failure, and the one the exec/fork fix exposed: with the token finally reaching `keyctl instantiate`, the answer had nowhere to go — the destination the stock handler names is `%S`, the requester's session keyring, which grants its owner View and Read through the user byte and Write only through the possessor byte. Possession was computed from the CALLER's keyrings only, so the helper was denied and the construction ended EACCES -> negate -> ENOKEY. `lookup_user_key` decides possession by searching the process keyrings, which follows the token into the requesting task's keyrings. In-guest before: `[KEYRING] instantiate tid=4282 rc=-13`; after: `rc=0`. | `keyring/perm.rs` `is_possessed`; `chain::a_helper_caches_the_answer_in_the_requester_s_session_keyring`, control RED. | this lane |

| FIXED B1705 | med | Nothing selects which mappings are dumped: `CoreSegment::dump_size` is supplied by the caller and the builder honours it verbatim. | FIXED B1706 | high | `ptrace` read the x86_64 tracee's saved entry frame as **16 quadwords at `kernel_stack - 0x80`** while the frame the entry asm pushes is **22 quadwords at `kernel_stack - 0xb0`**. Consequence was silent wrong data, not a crash: `PTRACE_GETREGS` / `PTRACE_GETREGSET(NT_PRSTATUS)` / `PTRACE_PEEKUSER` reported the frame's `r11` as `orig_rax` and its `rcx` as `rip`, and `PTRACE_SETREGS` / `POKEUSER` wrote a debugged process's registers into the wrong slots. Invisible at a syscall stop (the `syscall` instruction genuinely parks the user RIP in `rcx`), visible at every fault/signal stop — i.e. exactly the attach a debugger performs. Fixed by deriving both the base and the field access from `PtRegs`, the struct the entry path pushes. | `t_ptrace_getregs` (new, `userspace/glibc_conformance/`): `pc_in_crash_fn` and `setregs_redirected_pc` at a SIGSEGV stop. Hosted: `s101_ptrace_regs::tests::x86_tests::*` (14 tests, was 9). Positive control: `u[U_RIP] = f.rcx` turns `x86_frame_maps_onto_the_right_user_regs_fields` and `x86_round_trip_preserves_every_general_register` RED. | B1706 |
| FIXED B1706 | med | Split source of truth about register layout: the live ptrace path carried its own frame offset table AND its own `struct user_regs_struct` index table, both duplicated by the core-dump `NT_PRSTATUS` path. Two paths could (and did) tell different stories about the same task. Frame layout now has one owner per arch (`PtRegs` / `SvcFrame`, reached by field name — no offsets restated anywhere); the ABI index tables have one owner (`hal::uregs::{x86_64::user_regs, aarch64::user_pt_regs}`) that `syscalls/101_ptrace` and `fs/coredump/gregset` both consult. | `crates/arch/hal/src/uregs.rs`; both consumers now `pub use` the same constants. | B1706 |
| FIXED B1706 | med | `PTRACE_GETREGS` reported a stale `rax` and a bogus `orig_rax` at any NON-syscall stop. `orig_rax` came from a frame slot that has no syscall meaning on a trap frame, and `rax` always came from the task's `ptrace_stop_rax` cell, which is only written at syscall stops — so a tracer stopped on SIGSEGV read whatever the last syscall stop left there. Now follows the frame's entry tag: on a `syscall` frame `rax` is the recorded stop value and `orig_rax` the syscall number; on a trap frame `rax` is the architectural register and `orig_rax` is the no-syscall marker, which is what the core-dump block already reported for the same frame. | `x86_trap_frame_reports_no_syscall_and_the_architectural_rax`, `x86_setregs_on_a_trap_frame_installs_the_architectural_rax`. | B1706 |
| FIXED B1707 | high | `PTRACE_GETSIGINFO` reported every fault stop as a kill record and filled `si_addr` with the TRACER's pid (filed by B1706). Two causes, both fixed: (1) `101_ptrace/sig.rs::getsiginfo` wrote only the `_kill` arm (`signo@0`, `code@8`, `pid@16`, `uid@20`, `value@24`) and never consulted the record's `_sigfault`/`_sigsys`/`_sigpoll` arm, so `si_addr` — bytes 16..24 in the fault arm — was never written; (2) `101_ptrace/stop.rs::notify_with` substituted the tracer's pid into `info.pid` whenever it was zero, unconditionally, so on a SIGSEGV record that word landed in `si_addr`. A debugger could not tell a wild pointer from `kill(2)`. `getsiginfo` now renders through the one `siginfo_t` writer; `notify_with` publishes the record exactly as its producer built it, and the SYNTHESISED event records name the TRACEE (`task_pid_vnr(current)`, `current_uid()`) as the reference kernel does. | Hosted: `hal::siginfo::tests` (18, was 8) + `s101_ptrace_event::tests` (18, was 14). In-guest x86_64: `t_ptrace_siginfo` prints `signo_is_segv=1 code_is_fault=1 addr_matches_fault=1 addr_is_not_a_pid=1`, matching the host oracle. Positive control: deleting the `_sigfault` arm from `write_siginfo` (the literal `_kill`-only write) turns 6 tests RED, one reporting `si_addr` = `0x99_00001234` — a pid and uid packed into the address field, the exact defect shape; deleting the reader's fault arm turns 3 RED. Both restored GREEN. | B1707 |
| FIXED B1707 | high | `PTRACE_SETSIGINFO` and every other `siginfo_t` copy-IN (`rt_sigqueueinfo`, `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`, `pidfd_send_signal`) decoded only the `_kill` arm, so a tracer's GETSIGINFO→SETSIGINFO round trip on a fault stop DESTROYED `si_addr` and re-read it as a sender's pid. The reference kernel's `kernel_siginfo_t` is the union itself, so its copy-in preserves the arm for free; ours is decomposed, so the arm is now recovered from `(si_signo, si_code)` by the same classifier the write side uses. | `hal::read_siginfo`; `every_arm_survives_a_flat_round_trip`, `the_caller_supplied_signal_selects_the_arm_not_the_buffers_si_signo`. | B1707 |
| FIXED B1707 | med | Split source of truth about `siginfo_t`: FOUR writers (`hal::write_siginfo`, `signal_common::write_user_siginfo`, `101_ptrace/sig.rs`, `101_ptrace/info.rs`), TWO readers (`signal_common::read_user_siginfo`, `101_ptrace/sig.rs`) and TWO independent copies of the `(signo, si_code)` → union-arm decision (`fs/signalfd/siginfo.rs`, and the implicit one in every hand-rolled `_kill` store). The two hand-rolled ptrace writers and the ptrace reader are deleted; `fs/signalfd` now consumes the classifier instead of carrying its own. One owner: `hal::siginfo` — `layout` (classify), `write_siginfo` (producer-tagged arm), `read_siginfo` (flat decode). | `crates/arch/hal/src/siginfo.rs`; `signalfd` tests unchanged and still green against the moved classifier. | B1707 |

| FIXED B1708 | med | Sticky `IP_OPTIONS` do not reach TCP segments — `send_tcp_ipv4_segment_in` passed a literal `None` while UDP, raw and ping carried the compiled area. The socket's `IpOpts` is now an `Arc` shared onto `TcpEntry.ip_opts` the way `ip_mtu_discover` is, so the area has one home; `TcpTxPolicy::ipv4_options` hands it to the IPv4 transmit path, and a compiled source route retargets the route lookup, the strict-route screen, the path MTU and the wire destination. Closes the curated rows "Sticky `IP_OPTIONS` do not reach TCP segments", "TCP does not emit IP_OPTIONS", and the residual caveat on the STALE "IP_OPTIONS never emitted on transmit" row. | `net::tests_tcp_ip_options_tx` (4 tests). Positive control: restoring the literal `None` at the call site turns `a_tcp_segment_carries_the_sockets_sticky_option_area` and `a_tcp_source_route_retargets_the_wire_destination_only` RED, 2 passed / 2 failed; restored, 1742/0. | — |
| FIXED B1708 | med | The option area was not charged against the MSS, so a connection carrying one would have emitted segments that no longer fit the path MTU. `net::tcp_ext_hdr` is the ungated owner of the rule (subtract the area, then floor at the minimum send MSS — that order, not the reverse); it is applied at active open, at a learned-PMTU reduction, and at `NetStack::tcp_sync_mss`, which `IP_OPTIONS` set-time now calls for an established connection so a late `setsockopt` cannot leave a stale MSS. | `net::tcp_ext_hdr::tests` (4), `tests_tcp_ip_options_tx::the_sticky_option_area_is_charged_against_the_connections_mss`. Positive control: making `ext_hdr_len` return 0 turns 2 tests RED; restored GREEN. | — |
| FIXED B1709 | med | `SO_NO_CHECK` was admitted, stored and readable with zero consumers: the IPv4 UDP builder always computed a checksum, so the option could not change a single wire byte. The flag now reaches `UdpHdr::build_into_opts` through the socket-owned transmit path (v4 and the v4-mapped AF_INET6 path), and the segmentation planner is handed the real value instead of a hardcoded `false`, so a socket asking for both `UDP_SEGMENT` and a suppressed checksum is refused rather than emitting unchecksummed segments. Closes the `SO_NO_CHECK has zero consumers` half of the curated `IP_CHECKSUM inert` row. | `net::udp::tests::suppressed_checksum_leaves_the_field_zero_and_the_datagram_acceptable`, `net::udp::tests::a_computed_zero_checksum_is_transmitted_as_all_ones`, `net::tests_udp_no_check_tx` (3). Positive control: dropping the suppression branch in the builder turns 3 of them RED; restored, 1739/0. | — |
| FIXED B1710/B1711/B1712 | med | TCP Fast Open is implemented end to end: namespace enable bits + keys, listener accept-queue bound, cookie mint/verify with rotation, SYN-with-data on both sides, client deferral (`TCP_FASTOPEN_CONNECT`/`MSG_FASTOPEN`), destination cookie cache and blackhole pause. Every failure rung falls back to an ordinary handshake. | B1641. | — |

Two causes, both in the ptrace stop path: (1) `sig.rs::getsiginfo` writes only the `_kill` arm of the `siginfo_t` union (`signo@0`, `code@8`, `pid@16`, `uid@20`, `value@24`) and never consults `SigInfo.fault` / `.sys` / `.poll`, so `si_addr` — which occupies bytes 16..24 in the `_sigfault` arm — is never written; (2) `stop.rs::notify_with` substitutes the tracer's pid into `info.pid` whenever it is zero, unconditionally, so on a SIGSEGV record that word lands in `si_addr`. A debugger therefore cannot learn the faulting address of the process it is attached to. ## Retired by D473 — rows whose status already said closed

Moved verbatim from the live ledger. Column shape is the ledger's old
5-column form: `Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner`.

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1720 | high | `fsmount(2)` returns an anon-inode fd over a deferred mount object, not an `O_PATH` fd over a real vfsmount in an anonymous mount namespace. The fd carries `mnt_id == 0`, is `FileType::Regular` so it is `ENOTDIR` as a dirfd, is invisible to `statmount`/`listmount`, and between `fsmount` and `move_mount` the mount exists nowhere and can be neither observed nor referenced. `FSMOUNT_NAMESPACE` is unimplementable until that changes. Also missing: the `SB_NOUSER → EINVAL` check after mount creation, and `FMODE_NEED_UNMOUNT`/dissolve-on-close semantics. | `fsmount_common/fd.rs` `install_fd` → `vfs/src/file/model.rs` `File::new` (`mnt_id = 0`); `pathresolve/at.rs` rejects a non-directory dirfd. `move_mount` mode (a) grafts the superblock through a private-inode side channel instead. Blocks `432`. **Fixed by B1720:** `fsmount` now creates a REAL mount in an anonymous mount namespace (`create_anon_mount`) and returns an `O_PATH` fd over it, so the fd carries a real `mnt_id`, resolves as a dirfd, and dissolves its mount on close if never moved (`File`'s teardown → `dissolve_anon`, Linux's `__fput` → `dissolve_on_fput`). `move_mount` grafts that same mount, so the id is stable across the move. NOTE on the row's own text: invisibility to `statmount`/`listmount` was NOT a defect — `grab_requested_mnt_ns` selects the caller's namespace, so an anonymous mount is correctly invisible there. 9 hosted tests; positive control turns 3 red. | B1720 |
| FIXED B1719 | high | **`hidepid=` / `subset=pid` are NOT a parameter-table problem — procfs cannot express them at all.** Of the 22 registered filesystem types only 5 (`tmpfs`, `ramfs`, `ext4`, `autofs`, `fuse`) even receive the mount data string; the other 17, `proc` included, take `_` and discard it. And `procfs::static_files::proc_root()` returns a **process-global cached `Arc`**, so every `mount -t proc` in every namespace shares ONE root inode, leaving nowhere for per-mount option state. Per-mount `hidepid=` needs per-superblock root inodes first. **Consequence for the security framing: listing `hidepid` in a parameter table would NOT make `ProtectProc=` work — only implementing it does. Conversely a table that OMITS `hidepid` makes the support probe answer "no", and systemd then correctly stops claiming a confinement it is not getting.** Omission is the cheap honest fix; implementation is the real one. | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/fsmount_common/registry.rs:118` (proc ctor discards data); `crates/kernel/procfs/src/static_files.rs:73-76` (cached singleton root). **Fixed by B1719:** the singleton is gone — `ProcfsFs::new(info)` builds a root inode per mount carrying that mount's `Arc<ProcFsInfo>`, published in `sb.s_fs_info` as the reference does. `hidepid=`/`subset=`/`gid=` are declared and enforced: `subset=pid` clears lookup and readdir of non-process entries, `hidepid` gates pid dirs at NO_ACCESS on lookup and INVISIBLE on readdir through `has_pid_permissions`. Guest proof, one subset mount: x86 `sub_meminfo=0 sub_pids=59`, arm `sub_meminfo=0`, where before it was 1. | B1719 |
| FIXED B1718 | high | The trailing-symlink follow for `open(O_CREAT)` was a SECOND resolver living in the ABI shim: `pathresolve::resolve_parent_at_flags` re-entered the whole path walk once per link hop on a rewritten pathname, kept its own hop budget (`MAX_CREATE_LINK_HOPS`) beside the walk's `MAX_SYMLINK_DEPTH`, and probed the leaf with `vp.inode.lookup(name)` — an inode-level lookup that bypasses the dcache the rest of the walk resolves through. The reference follows the trailing component inside the SAME walk (`open_last_lookups` resolves the leaf, `step_into(WALK_TRAILING)` picks the link up and the walk continues on its target). Moved into the walker: a `LOOKUP_PARENT` walk with `follow` now resolves a LAST_NORM leaf through the same `lookup_child` every other component uses, and follows it through the same frame push, budget and `RESOLVE_*` gates. | `crates/kernel/vfs/src/namei/walk.rs` (leaf follow), `crates/kernel/vfs/src/namei/child.rs` (the shared resolution, extracted so there is one owner). `crates/kernel/vfs/tests/namei_create_trailing_link.rs` — 14 tests. Positive control: disabling only the follow decision turns 7 of 14 RED (`hop1` reports `(10,"hop1")` where the target is `(11,"stub")`), and the 4 no-follow cases stay green because they must be insensitive to it. | B1718 |
| FIXED B1718 | med | **8 tests in `syscalls/src/pathresolve/trailing_link.rs` had never executed once.** `pathresolve/mod.rs` is `#![cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]`, so the module and its `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` compiled out entirely — `cargo test -p syscalls a_plain_create_follows_the_link` matched 0 tests in the whole package while reporting `ok`. The decision they covered now lives on `LookupFlags::set_open_create_trailing` in `vfs`, where the tests run, and is asserted against the real walker rather than in isolation. | Measured before the change: `cargo test -p syscalls -- trailing_link` → `running 0 tests`; by test name → 0 matched across every binary. After: the same rules are 3 of the 14 tests in `vfs::namei_create_trailing_link`, and `the_flag_rule_and_the_walk_agree_on_the_same_link` ties the flag to the walk's actual answer. | B1718 |
| FIXED C268 | high | **`make smoke-x86` FAILS on `origin/main` today — 3/3 attempts time out at 600 s — while the kernel it boots is healthy.** The gate greps for `Reached target basic.target`, and that line no longer reaches the serial console: a boot prints 7 other `Reached target` lines at t≈5 s (cryptsetup, getty, integritysetup, paths, remote-fs, slices, veritysetup) and then never that one. The kernel is fine — the same boot's sysrq task dump at t=554 s shows a full GNOME session scheduling normally (`gnome-software`, `fwupd`, `flatpak-system-helper`, `gsd-printer`, `systemd-userwork`), `Started systemd-journald.service`, and zero `File exists` errors — and systemd cannot reach `graphical.target` without passing `basic.target`. So the pre-push gate every kernel lane depends on is currently a guaranteed red, and a lane that trusts it will read its own change as a boot regression. This is the curated row that opens "The `boot-smoke` marker rides on systemd output, not the unconditional console path … it is fragile to exactly the quieting just done" — it has now actually broken. | Measured on this box, same command, same profile, back to back: detached worktree at `origin/main` (`0f39c79cb`) → attempts 1, 2, 3 all `timeout after 600s without login marker`, `exit=2`; branch `B1718` → identical shape on both the gnome and the `OXIDE_QUICKBOOT_PROFILE=lite` rootfs. `state.md` records this same gate as `PASS 66s` on `db658b904`, so it broke between that commit and `0f39c79cb`. Fix is a marker on the unconditional console path, not a systemd message. **Fixed by C268:** the gate no longer waits for a line the image decides whether to print — it types `echo OXIDE-AL"IVE"-OK` at the serial debug shell and matches the reply. The nonce is split by quotes in the command and whole only in the output, so the guest's echo of the typed bytes cannot match it and only a shell that EVALUATED the line can. Measured: x86 PASS 54s attempt 1, arm PASS 64s attempt 1, against 3/3 timeouts on the same box before. Positive control: a probe command whose output cannot match fails the run (exit=2). | C268 |
| FIXED B1702 + B1718 | high | `open(path, O_WRONLY\|O_CREAT\|O_TRUNC)` on a **dangling symlink whose parent directory exists** returns **EEXIST**. The reference follows the link and creates the target; EEXIST is only correct with `O_EXCL`. Minimal in-guest reproducer: `ln -sf /run/systemd/resolve/stub-resolv.conf /etc/rc2` (that directory exists, the file does not), then `: > /etc/rc2` → `sh: /etc/rc2: File exists`, and `python3 -c "import os;os.open('/etc/rc2',os.O_WRONLY\|os.O_CREAT\|os.O_TRUNC,0o644)"` → `FileExistsError: [Errno 17]`. This is why nothing in the guest can write `/etc/resolv.conf`, which ships as exactly such a dangling symlink — so it is on the path between a working IP stack and working name resolution. Needs its own lane: the fix sits in the `O_CREAT` open path and its errno ordering against `O_EXCL`/`O_NOFOLLOW` has to be audited against the reference. | See the archived open-flag truth table in `fixed-issues.md` (D473). **Closed in two parts.** The user-visible errno was fixed by `B1702` (`0aa0e2c97`), which made the create follow the trailing link. `B1718` then removed the reason it had been wrong: the follow was a SECOND resolver in the ABI shim that re-entered the whole walk per hop, kept its own symlink budget, and probed the leaf through the inode instead of the dcache. The follow now happens inside the walk, as `step_into(WALK_TRAILING)` does upstream. Verified by 14 tests over the real walker with a positive control that turns 7 of them red. | B1702, B1718 |
| FIXED C247 | low | `metadata/index.md` counters go stale and collide — index read `D 424` while `D432` was already merged. | `tools/next-branch.sh` derives the number from git refs + merge subjects and maxes against the table; `--check` fails when the table is behind, wired into `make ci` as `counter-check`. Positive control: the check reported `C247 already exists in git` before the table was bumped. | C247 |

### Net / socket

| FIXED 7d6992594 | med | Interface teardown closed and drained the IPv4 neighbour cache but left the retired generation's IPv6 neighbour cache open, so a retained handle could still expose stale NDP entries after unregister. | The common teardown now clears both family-generic neighbour caches before the generation drains. | FIXED B1757 | med | An unqualified `ICMP_TYPE_ECHO_REPLY` in pattern position became a binding, so every ICMP type incremented the echo-reply counter and the following match arm was unreachable. | `each_icmp_type_counts_in_its_own_column` pins the counter split; restoring the unqualified name fails it. The warning-gate residual from this lane is folded into C288's tooling record. | B1757-icmp-echo-reply-catchall |

| FIXED F808 | low | `TCP_ZEROCOPY_RECEIVE` copied stream bytes into freshly allocated window pages because the TCP receive queue had no page-granular owner to donate. | TCP now owns page-backed receive segments, coalesces contiguous arrival data while preserving timestamp boundaries, and transfers an aligned full-page object-frame reference directly to the receive window. Ordinary reads, OOB removal, queue accounting, and low-memory fallback retain the same canonical stream. Targeted TCP tests (111), modules tests (213), and both feature-gate architectures passed. | F808-tcp-zerocopy-receive-page-donation |

| FIXED ae95e44da | low | A pathname or abstract AF_UNIX server cached its publishing Landlock domain at `bind(2)`, rather than consulting the server socket's retained file credentials at each check. | The registry now retains only a weak link to the bound socket; pathname resolution and abstract-scope checks read the domain from its canonical `f_cred`. Socket creation, accept, and socketpair capture that immutable security credential; focused Landlock tests, all 2,027 net tests, 1,224 syscall tests, and both feature-gate architectures passed. | B1837-unix-owner-domain-live |

| FIXED a06dbab2b | med | Fast Open now implements all four reference blackhole-detection rungs. The out-of-order receive queue retains payload, urgent metadata, and FIN together, so a sole bare FIN is detected both when it was queued before local close and when it arrives after close. | `TcpConn::ooo_buf` owns complete out-of-order segments; `tcp_conn::active_fastopen` tests both close orderings; x86 and ARM smoke pass. | F807-fold-f806-ledger |

| FIXED 29cd0cbd6 | med | Default-parallel `net` tests could deadlock while a global AF_PACKET registry retained sockets from unrelated network namespaces. | AF_PACKET registration is now namespace-scoped; 50 default-parallel full-net runs passed. | B1824-hosted-preempt-thread-state |

| FIXED 29cd0cbd6 | med | Hosted preemption state reused a 64-slot CPU array across more test workers, making one worker observe another's softirq context. | Hosted preemption and pre-task reschedule state are thread-local; regression test holds 128 contexts simultaneously. | B1824-hosted-preempt-thread-state |


### Drivers / devices

| FIXED 7a3ffec4e | high | `/dev/snd/controlC0` inherited the sound driver's default EOF read and always-readable poll mask. After `alsactl` subscribed with `O_NONBLOCK`, each empty read returned 0 while poll kept reporting `POLLIN`, leaving the daemon permanently runnable and consuming about 68% of the only vCPU. | ALSA control subscription is now per open description. An unsubscribed read returns `EBADFD`; a short event buffer returns `EINVAL`; an empty subscribed nonblocking read returns `EAGAIN`; a blocking read sleeps interruptibly; and poll reports readable only when an event exists. A red/green poll control and all 23 sound tests pass. In clean-release KVM, `alsactl` changed from runnable at ~68% CPU to sleeping at 0.0%; the 400k-syscall probe fell from 1.53–1.65 s to 0.75–0.76 s on SMP=1 and measured 0.76–0.78 s on SMP=2. | B1868-runtime-tax-attribution |

| FIXED 40910dfdb | med | `blk_execute_rq` returned before an asynchronous driver's completion, reported `BLK_STS_IOERR`, and leaked its wait record. | The synchronous path now uses the canonical Linux completion primitive and sleeps until `end_io` publishes the actual status. A worker-thread regression fails if the wait is bypassed; the focused Linux-block suite passes 27 tests. | B1860-block-completion-bio-contracts |
| FIXED 40910dfdb | low | `bio_add_page` could substitute an unrelated fixed bounce buffer for an invalid page, could not represent multiple page vectors, and exposed behavior tied to truncation; `blk_mq_end_request_batch` completed nothing.
