C and Rust share one asserted 64-bit layout and callback surface. Positive controls turn the asynchronous wait, batch drain, and second-vector regressions red; all 218 modules tests, the full workspace, hosted/x86_64/aarch64 KPI header compiles, both release builds, and concurrent x86_64/aarch64 smoke pass. | B1860-block-completion-bio-contracts |

| FIXED fbf4eb380 | med | `usb_find_interface` compared the interface registration flag to the requested minor, while `UsbInterface` carried no class-device minor and the class-device registration surface was absent. | Interfaces now own the assigned minor; `usb_register_dev`/`usb_deregister_dev` reserve and release the canonical USB minor table, and lookup requires both the bound driver and assigned minor. Regression covers allocation, duplicate rejection, lookup, release, and reuse. `cargo test -p modules --lib` (213 passed) and both feature-gate architectures passed. | B1838-linux-usb-interface-minor |
| FIXED 20f3cf186 | med | `usb_ep_free_request` could drop a UDC-owned request as a shim-owned `Box` when the endpoint or its free hook was absent. | Request destruction now always dispatches to the endpoint's paired `free_request` operation; an invalid endpoint violates the USB gadget KPI rather than selecting a second allocator. The gadget regression proves the endpoint hook runs exactly once; all 213 modules tests passed. | B1839-usb-gadget-request-allocator |
| FIXED 4092ae74c | low | The test-only `reset_wait_cells` cleared heap-owned wait cells despite `wait_cell` returning stable references to them. | Removed the reset path, so test and kernel builds share the same never-remove lifetime rule. Focused sync tests and all 213 modules tests passed. | B1840-sync-wait-test-lifetime |
| FIXED 8b7e2fccb | low | `free_bytes` and `linux_page_phys` were safe functions despite dereferencing caller-supplied allocation and page-descriptor pointers. | Both helpers now require an explicit unsafe call at every allocator, vmalloc, vmap, cache, and DMA boundary; each boundary states the matching live-object contract. All 213 modules tests passed. | B1841-linux-alloc-raw-contracts |
| FIXED e39485072 | low | `free_netdev`'s header magic check was described as safely rejecting a foreign pointer even though reading the header already requires a valid netdev allocation. | The safety contract now states the actual KPI: only a live `alloc_netdev*` result may be freed; magic detects corruption after that precondition, not arbitrary pointers. Focused netdev modules tests passed. | B1842-netdev-free-contract |


### Memory / page cache

| FIXED fdc8eb35a | low | The x86 PKRU default had no debugfs control despite owning an atomic kernel-wide default. | `/sys/kernel/debug/x86/init_pkru` now appears only when OSPKE is enabled, uses owner read/write permissions, accepts base-0 integer input, and delegates to the sole PKRU default setter. | B1925-pkru-debugfs-control |

| FIXED 3f47f7d92 | low | `cachestat` could not report ext4 pages in writeback because the frame store carried no per-index in-flight state. | The frame store now owns counted per-index writeback state, so concurrent cache-stat walks report it and reclaim excludes the page until the final overlapping flush completes. | B1923-cachestat-writeback-state |

| FIXED f8c9cb756 | low | `cachestat` eviction shadows could grow without bound for a live ext4 inode under repeated page reclamation. | The ext4 frame store now retains a small floor plus a resident-cache-scaled history budget and discards oldest surplus shadows; the regression proves pruning preserves the newest history. | B1921-cachestat-shadow-reclaim |

| FIXED 6b29690ac | blocker | File-backed demand faults installed exactly one 4 KiB page, with no Linux `do_fault_around` equivalent to map adjacent page-cache residents. | Read and execute faults now inspect a naturally aligned 64 KiB window, clamped to the VMA, EOF, and page-table leaf, and install only already-resident cache frames without allocation or I/O. Speculative PTEs remain read-only so private COW and shared dirty-fault semantics stay on the demand path. A positive control that disabled neighbor installation turned the focused regression red; the restored implementation passed `vmm` (330) and `fs` (958) tests, full CI, and paired first-attempt smoke on x86_64 (54 s) and aarch64 (100 s), both with serial RX. | B1862-file-fault-around |


### Filesystem / mount

| FIXED 6db582ff4 | med | autofs and FUSE published complete parameter tables but their classic constructors scanned the rendered option blob again, so admission and construction could disagree and descriptor values could be resolved too late. | Both filesystem types now install subsystem-owned typed `FsContextOps`; `fd=` is pinned and validated during parse, Linux value/range/required-field rules are enforced once, and `get_tree` consumes that exact state. FUSE `max_read` bounds live reads, subtype and shown options use the parsed values, and the audited wire target is pinned to current ABI 7.45 with extended INIT. Focused suites passed (FUSE 29, autofs 3, VFS parser 13), the full workspace had zero failures, and x86_64/aarch64 smoke passed first attempt. | B1859-fs-context-autofs-fuse |


### Process / exec
| FIXED 17ea211b8 | high | Once any inotify or fanotify mark existed anywhere, every successful non-empty read and write walked the global notification-group and watch registry, so unrelated byte I/O paid O(all groups × watches) and the clean-release 400k-syscall probe spent 0.73–0.75 s in the kernel. | Fsnotify marks now maintain exact per-event summaries on their attached inode plus child and filesystem-scope admission gates. The read/write hook skips group dispatch unless the target inode, its parent, or a matching broader scope can contain the event; mask replacement and every `Watch` teardown update those summaries from the canonical mark lifecycle. A positive control that disabled direct-inode admission made the focused regression fail; all 959 `fs` tests pass. In settled SMP=1 KVM, successful raw/libc one-byte I/O fell from 1.69–2.07 µs to 0.18–0.20 µs, and GNU `dd`'s 400k calls fell from 0.75–0.78 s wall / 0.73–0.75 s system to 0.07–0.10 s wall / 0.05 s system. | B1869-syscall-oncpu-attribution |
| FIXED B1866 | high | Six D513 performance rows remained `OPEN` after their distinct defects had merged: syscall-return timer/registry work and CPU accounting in B1861, contaminated syscost tracing in B1861, file fault-around in B1862, and IRQ-driven UART TX in B1863. | The original rows are folded out of the live drop; the four existing fixed records remain the unique implementation history. The only residual from the original syscall-tail report—unconditional disabled tracepoint hook calls plus missing post-fix measurements—is now the curated `NEXT` row. | B1866-performance-findings-queue |
| FIXED 70d465784,c91a2c6c8 | high | Disabled `sys_enter` and `sys_exit` tracepoints still entered two AtomicPtr hook wrappers on every syscall instead of riding Linux's per-task `SYSCALL_WORK_SYSCALL_TRACEPOINT` gate. The enabled path was also not operable from normal tools: tracefs control inodes used the default `truncate → EROFS`, so the `O_TRUNC` in `echo 1 > events/syscalls/sys_enter/enable` failed before its write hook ran. | Tracefs now serializes the syscall-event family under a tracepoint lock, stamps one per-task syscall-work bit across the task registry on the zero/nonzero edge, reconciles tasks at publication, and orders hook install/remove around that bit. Dispatch tests the current task's work word before either hook. Writable tracefs controls use the Linux `tracefs_file_inode_operations`/`simple_setattr` equivalent, admitting shell-redirection truncation as a no-op. Positive controls made the disabled-path source contract and control-inode truncate regression red; focused sched/tracefs/syscall tests pass. A live clean-release guest enabled both events with shell redirection, recorded `sys_enter`/`sys_exit`, retained exits after entry was disabled, and returned both controls to zero. The required benchmark rerun is recorded as the new queue-head residual rather than hidden here. | B1867-syscall-tracepoint-work-flag |
| FIXED 3e663504d | blocker | Every syscall return scanned the current process's POSIX timer slots, took the global timer backend lock, resolved non-leader timer owners through the global task registry, recomputed/rearmed the hardware deadline, sampled the clock again for coarse timers, and wrote a diagnostic syscall ring even when its consumers were disabled. | The production syscall tail contains no timer walk or coarse-timer service. Wall-timer mutation/rearm owns queue synchronization and hardware reprogramming; CPU timers run from the scheduler tick; diagnostic history is compiled only for its consumers. Positive controls made the return-path contract test fail when the scan was restored. Full CI and paired first-attempt smoke passed. | B1861-syscall-return-accounting-fastpath |
| FIXED 3e663504d | blocker | Timer-sampled task accounting assigned the whole inter-tick interval from the privilege level interrupted at one instant, so a 400k-syscall run reported 0.821 s user and 0.000 s system. `/proc/<pid>/stat` compounded the defect by publishing scheduler runtime as utime and hardcoding stime to zero. | Task-owned Linux generic-vtime state now closes intervals at syscall, IRQ, and exception entry/return, context switch, and exit; off-CPU time is excluded and the real utime/stime clocks feed the thread group and procfs. Hosted transition tests include a positive control that turns red if the off-CPU sentinel is removed. Full CI, both architecture structural gates, and paired first-attempt smoke passed. | B1861-syscall-return-accounting-fastpath |
| FIXED F80902 | low | accept4 copy-out failure relied on implicit `Drop` and had no hosted proof that the accepted child was released while returning the original copy error. | `net::sock::complete_accepted` is the canonical ungated finalization owner; it releases the child before returning the ABI copy-out error, and hosted tests pin both failure release and successful retention. | F80902-accept4-copyout-coverage |
| FIXED F809 | low | `accept4`'s empty-backlog wait decision was kernel-gated, leaving nonblocking, interrupt, and timeout ordering covered only by source inspection. | `net::sock_intr::accept_wait_verdict` canonically orders O_NONBLOCK, signal restart/EINTR, timeout EAGAIN, and park; INET/UNIX and VSOCK accept loops consume it, and hosted tests cover each verdict. | F809-accept4-blocking-wait-coverage |
| FIXED F810 | med | `listen` and `accept4` duplicated target-gated network-security hook calls, so their namespace, family, and operation wiring had no hosted behavioural proof. | `net::sock::{admit_listen,admit_accept}` call the single `security_admission::check` owner before protocol dispatch; hosted tests deny each operation and pin the exact socket namespace and family. | F810-listen-accept-admission-coverage |
| FIXED F811 | low | `IP_BIND_ADDRESS_NO_PORT` and `IP_LOCAL_PORT_RANGE` were read independently in target-gated UDP/TCP and v4/v6 bind/autobind paths, with only the isolated decision covered hosted. | `net::sock::bind_port_policy` owns one socket snapshot; every allocation path uses it, and hosted coverage pins defer behavior, explicit-port allocation, and the exact per-socket range. | F811-bind-port-policy-wiring-coverage |
| FIXED F812 | low | The target-gated connect path handed the socket `IpOpts` to its TCP entry with no hosted proof that the entry retained the socket's actual owner. | `net::sock::tcp_entry_ip_options` is the one handoff owner used by the lifecycle; hosted coverage proves pointer identity and that a mutation after handoff is visible through the entry handle. | F812-tcp-ip-option-handoff-coverage |
| FIXED F813 | low | The target-gated TCP setsockopt shim read Fast Open enable bits and initialized default keys without hosted proof that either action used the socket's namespace. | `net::sock::tcp_fastopen::{setsockopt_bits,complete_setsockopt}` are the sole shim adapters over the existing namespace owners; hosted coverage proves distinct sockets read distinct bits, key initialization affects only the requesting namespace, and no-effect writes draw no keys. | F813-fastopen-namespace-wiring-coverage |
| FIXED F814 | med | TCP Fast Open connect and send result arms were target-gated, leaving deferred-connect success, write-open rejection of deferral, and nonblocking byte-count versus `EINPROGRESS` conversion without hosted proof. | `net::sock::fastopen_result` is the single result-policy owner consumed by connect admission and Fast Open send; hosted coverage pins deferred `connect` return, blocking/nonblocking opened results, write deferral rejection, and byte-count conversion. | F814-fastopen-connect-result-coverage |

| FIXED F816 | med | `IPV6_AUTOFLOWLABEL` was accepted and readable but every UDP, TCP, and raw IPv6 packet builder emitted a zero flow label. | The canonical `Ipv6Opts` object now reaches active and passive TCP entries; the shared IPv6 header builder applies explicit labels first and otherwise derives a stable automatic label for enabled sockets. Hosted tests pin precedence, automatic-label stability, and TCP's retained owner. | F816-816-ipv6-autoflowlabel |
| FIXED F817 | med | `IPV6_ADDR_PREFERENCES` was accepted and readable but did not affect IPv6 source-address selection. | IPv6 address records now classify privacy addresses, and source selection ranks public versus temporary candidates from the socket preference. UDP, TCP, and raw IPv6 output pass the canonical socket preference to that selector. Hosted coverage pins both preference outcomes; full net tests, both kernel feature checks, and x86/aarch64 boot smoke passed. | F817-ipv6-source-preferences |
| FIXED F818 | med | `IP_RECVERR_RFC4884` was accepted and readable but ICMP extended-error records always reported ordinary `ee_data = 0`. | The canonical socket error queue now retains RFC4884’s extension offset and invalid-extension bit only when the socket option enables it; the normal error payload and queue remain the sole source of truth. Hosted regressions cover disabled, valid, and malformed extensions. | F818-ipv4-socket-option-consumption |

Hosted regressions cover socket-to-control precedence, TCP retention, and on-wire extension ordering. | F819-ipv6-sticky-extension-headers |

## Historical lane-drop archive

C289 moved every already-fixed row out of the last mixed lane drops. These
tables retain the post-D473 class column and source heading so historical
evidence has one canonical home.

### B1719-procfs-per-superblock-root

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1719 | DEFECT | high | **`procfs::static_files::proc_root()` was a process-global cached `Arc`, so every `mount -t proc` in every namespace shared ONE root inode.** There was nowhere for a mount's own options to live, which is why `hidepid=`/`subset=` could not be expressed at all and `ProtectProc=`/`ProcSubset=` silently did nothing for every service that asked for them. The reference builds a fresh root inode per superblock in `proc_fill_super` and shares only the static `proc_dir_entry` skeleton (which `crate::reg` already is). Now: `ProcfsFs::new(info)` builds its own root per mount, the root carries `Arc<ProcFsInfo>`, and the same `Arc` is published in `sb.s_fs_info` where the reference keeps it. | `crates/kernel/procfs/src/fs_info.rs` (identity + decisions, ungated), `fs_impl.rs`, `static_files.rs::build_root`, `live/root.rs`. Guest proof on both arches, one `subset=pid` mount: x86 `sub_meminfo=0 sub_pids=59`, arm `sub_meminfo=0`, zero faults — against `sub_meminfo=1` before. | B1719 |
| FIXED B1719 | MISSING | high | **`hidepid=` and `subset=` were undeclared and unimplemented**; the parameter table was `Some(&[])`, so `mount -t proc -o hidepid=2` returned EINVAL — honest, but the confinement was simply absent. Both are now declared and ENFORCED: `subset=pid` removes every non-process entry from lookup and readdir (Linux `proc_lookup`/`proc_readdir` `pidonly` gate), and `hidepid` gates pid directories at the reference's two thresholds — `HIDEPID_NO_ACCESS` on lookup, `HIDEPID_INVISIBLE` on readdir — through the same `has_pid_permissions` ladder, including the `gid=` group exemption and the absolute `ptraceable` mode. | `procfs::fs_info` 16 hosted tests incl. every accepted spelling, the rejected values (`3`, `5`, `OFF`, `0x2`), the threshold split (noaccess lists but does not open), the group exemption, and that `ptraceable` ignores it. Enforcement sites: `live/root.rs` (lookup + readdir), `live/pid_access.rs::pid_visible`. | B1719 |
| FIXED B1719 | DEFECT | med | **A filesystem constructor's parameter slice is NOT its option source, and reading it silently dropped every option.** `mount(2)` carries options in the DATA BLOB; the slice holds only values that are pinned open files (`FSCONFIG_SET_FD`). The first cut of this lane read the slice, so every hosted test passed while the guest showed `meminfo` inside a `subset=pid` mount. The wiring decision now lives in `fs_info::info_for_mount(data, pinned)` — ungated, so it is testable — and `vfs::fs::split_monolithic` is the ONE splitter, extracted from `generic_parse_monolithic` rather than copied. | `a_mounts_options_come_from_the_blob_and_never_from_the_pinned_slice` fails against the shape that shipped. Guest before: `sub_meminfo=1`; after: `sub_meminfo=0`. | B1719 |
| FIXED 4905b1ebd | DEFECT | high | **A `#DF` (double fault) — kernel stack overflow — under a burst of serial-driven work.** Typing one compound command at the debug shell (`mkdir`; two `mount -t proc`; three `ls \| grep -c`; one deliberately-failing `mount -o hidepid=3`) double-faults the guest at t=7.73s: `vec=8 (#DF) rip=ffffffff8006d7af rsp=fffffb0000006000 rbx=00000000000003f8`. `rbx` is the COM1 base and `rsp` is on a stack guard page, so this is the guard-page catch of a stack overflow in the serial/klog path, not a procfs fault. NOT caused by this lane: the same kernel runs a plain `mount -t proc` + `ls` probe clean, and so does `origin/main`. What is not yet known is the depth chain that overflows — the fault is 12 frames of `[FAULT]` register dump with no backtrace. | Reproduced once on `B1719` x86 with the compound probe; `origin/main` and `B1719` both clean with the single-mount probe (`plain_meminfo=1`, 0 faults, exit=0 on both). Log kept: the `[FAULT]` block at `[7.730]`. Needs a lane with `debug-stack-guard` to name the overflowing path. | unowned |

### B1720-fsmount-real-vfsmount

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1720 | DEFECT | high | **`fsmount(2)` returned an anon-inode fd over a deferred object, not a mount.** The fd carried `(sb, root)` and nothing else: `mnt_id == 0`, `FileType::Regular` (so `ENOTDIR` as a dirfd), no mount object at all between `fsmount` and `move_mount`, and nothing to tear down if the fd was simply closed. The reference creates a real `vfsmount`, parks it in an ANONYMOUS mount namespace, and hands back an `O_PATH` fd with `FMODE_NEED_UNMOUNT`. Implemented: `vfs::mount::create_anon_mount` builds a real mount (own id, own root) in a namespace flagged `is_anon`, `fsmount` installs a path fd over it, and `File`'s teardown dissolves it if it was never moved — Linux's `__fput` → `dissolve_on_fput`, at the same place. `move_mount(2)` now grafts THAT mount (`graft_anon_mount_at`), so the id is stable across the move instead of being minted at graft time. | `crates/kernel/vfs/src/mount/anon.rs`, `mntns.rs` (`is_anon`, `allocate_anon`), `file/lifetime.rs` (dissolve hook), `432_fsmount.rs`, `429_move_mount.rs`. 9 hosted tests in `vfs/tests/mount_anon_ns.rs`; positive control: making the namespace non-anonymous turns 3 of them RED. `make smoke` PASS both arches (arm 60s, x86 101s, attempt 1). | B1720 |
| FIXED B1720 | DEFECT | med | **An anonymous mount was reaped the instant it was created.** `Mount` keys its namespace by id only, with no reference — fine for a mount in a task's namespace, fatal for one whose namespace nothing else holds: `MntNamespace::drop` runs `reap_ns`, which unpublished the mount that had just been published. Linux's `mount` points at its `mnt_ns` with a reference; ours now holds one for the anonymous case (`Mount::anon_ns`), released on graft or dissolve. | Found by the first run of `an_fsmounted_filesystem_is_a_real_mount_before_it_is_moved_anywhere`: `mount_by_id` returned `None` immediately after a successful publish. | B1720 |

### B1722-cgroup2-mount-options

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1722 | MISSING | high | **cgroup2 discarded every mount option.** It registered with `FsType::new` (no parameter table) and its constructor took `_` for the data string, so `-o nsdelegate,memory_recursiveprot` — what systemd passes on a modern distro — never reached the hierarchy. All six reference flags are now declared (`cgroup2_fs_parameters`) and stored in a hierarchy-root flag word using Linux's own `CGRP_ROOT_*` bit values, merged rather than replaced on a second mount so one mount cannot silently clear another's delegation boundary, and rendered by `show_options` in declaration order. | `crates/kernel/cgroup/src/root_flags.rs` (8 tests), `state.rs` (`root_flags`/`add_root_flags`), `fs.rs` (`show_options`), `registry.rs`. | B1722 |
| FIXED B1722 | DEFECT | high | **`nsdelegate` had no enforcement point at all.** A cgroup namespace existed only as a display remap (`nscg::cgroup_ns`, consumed solely by `/proc/<pid>/cgroup`); `cgroup.procs` writes consulted it nowhere, so a task in a delegated namespace could migrate a process into any cgroup in the hierarchy. Now the write path asks the reference's question (`cgroup_procs_write_permission`): under `nsdelegate`, both the source and destination must be visible from the caller's namespace root, and a write across the boundary is **ENOENT** — not EPERM, because the cgroup is not supposed to exist as far as the caller is concerned. The boundary test compares path COMPONENTS, so `/podxyz` is not inside `/pod`. | `tree/hierarchy.rs::is_under_path`, `lib.rs::write_file` gate, `state.rs::caller_ns_root` hook installed by `registry.rs`. Tests in `tests/root_options.rs`. Positive control: replacing the component compare with a raw string prefix turns 2 tests RED, and the failing case is the escape (`/podxyz` reads as inside `/pod`). | B1722 |
| FIXED B1722 | MISSING | med | **`memory.events` was always the subtree aggregate and `memory.events.local` did not exist.** The reference publishes `memory.events.local` unconditionally and switches `memory.events` to the local count under `memory_localevents`; both are implemented, and the new file is in the memory controller's file list so `readdir` shows it rather than it being merely readable. | `tree/accounting.rs::local_memory_events`, `tree/files.rs`, `tree/controllers.rs`, `inode.rs` (read-only mode). Guest, both arches: `/sys/fs/cgroup/memory.events.local` reads `low 0 …`. | B1722 |
| FIXED 4905b1ebd | INFRA | high | **Second independent reproduction of the serial-path `#DF` (kernel stack overflow) filed by B1720.** A COMPOUND command typed at the debug shell faults the guest at the stack guard page with COM1 in `rbx`: B1720 saw `rip=ffffffff8006d7af rsp=fffffb0000006000 rbx=0x3f8`, this lane `rip=ffffffff8006f04f rsp=fffffb0000006000 rbx=0x3f8`. Both times a single-command probe on the same kernel is clean and the plain `make smoke` passes on both arches. It is not specific to a subsystem — B1720's probe mounted procfs, this one cgroup2 — so it is the serial/klog path under a burst of typed shell work. It now blocks in-guest verification of anything that needs more than one command, which is why this lane's flag path is proven hosted only. | Two faults, two lanes, same signature and same guard-page `rsp`. `SMOKE_KEEP_LOG` captures of both kept. | unowned |

### B1723-serial-stack-overflow-df

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED 4905b1ebd | DEFECT | high | **CORRECTED by B1724 — the earlier prescription in this row was wrong and must not be acted on.** There IS a per-CPU hardirq stack (`hal_x86_64::init_percpu_hardirq_stack`, `gs:[24]`), the IRQ entry asm (`oxide_irq_common`) DOES switch RSP to it, and it is armed before the first `sti` on the BSP (`kmain/early.rs:347`, IRQs still masked) and on every AP (`smp_x86.rs:205`, explicitly before its `sti`). So the fix is NOT "add a hardirq stack". The real defect is that **the hardirq stack itself overflows**: it is 16 KiB (`KSTACK_BYTES`, shared constant for task and IRQ stacks) and the tree's own measurements record its peak at **~14.5 KiB** — `kstack.rs:31-36`: *"Measured peaks now: task ~6.7 KiB, per-CPU IRQ stack ~14.5 KiB (dispatcher 13.9 KiB / net-RX softirq 14.5 KiB)"*. That leaves ~1.5 KiB, and a UART RX interrupt taken while the dispatcher/softirq drain is at depth is enough to cross the guard page. `#PF` is not IST-routed, so it escalates to `#DF`. The fix is frame de-bloat on the dispatcher/softirq path (targets already ranked in `scratch/arm-smp2-fault.md`), or a larger IRQ stack — note 32 KiB was tried once and reverted (`kstack.rs:30-33`: it "regressed x86 while fixing nothing that the real fixes did not already fix"), so de-bloat is the direction with evidence behind it. | Faulting address resolved against the build's own ELF: `rip` → `drv_uart_16550::imp::rx_isr_msi`, `rcx` → `drv_serial::deliver`; `rsp=0xfffffb0000006000` is exactly `slot_stack_lo` — the floor of a 16 KiB guard-paged slot, which is what BOTH task and hardirq stacks are allocated from (`sched::kstack::alloc_leaked_top`). Arming verified at both call sites. | unowned |

### B1725-irq-frame-debloat

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED 4905b1ebd | DEFECT | high | **The hardirq stack overflows because the RX/softirq chain spends ~3.2 KiB in three frames, on a stack with ~1.5 KiB of headroom.** Measured from `objdump -d` of the release kernel (`sub $N,%rsp` per function), restricted to functions reachable from the IRQ dispatcher and the softirq drain. Top of the ranking: `net::stack::tcp_listener_deliver::build_passive_child` **1816 B**, `kmain::hooks::tick_poll_combined` 1096 B, `modules::linux_irq::linux_irq_dispatch` 1032 B, `net::stack_ipv6::control::ipv6_control_tick` 952 B, `net::stack::ipv4::deliver_rx_in` 888 B, `net::arp_timer` 888 B, `drv_virtio_input::drain::queue::drain_softirq` 808 B, `net::raw4::rx::deliver_raw4` 776 B. One live chain — `deliver_rx_in` → `deliver_tcp_to_listener` (520 B) → `build_passive_child` (1816 B) — is 3.2 KiB before anything else on the stack, which is why an arriving UART RX interrupt is the last straw. `build_passive_child` is the single biggest and the obvious first target: a passive TCP open builds its child by value on the softirq stack; boxing it (or building it in place) removes ~1.8 KiB from the deepest path. Linux keeps these paths shallow for the same reason — `THREAD_SIZE`/`IRQ_STACK_SIZE` is 16 KiB there too, and a 1.8 KiB frame in `tcp_conn_request` would be treated as a bug. | Command recorded so the ranking is reproducible: `objdump -d --no-show-raw-insn <kernel.elf>` + per-function `sub $imm,%rsp` extraction, filtered to the IRQ/softirq path. Full build: `cargo run -p xtask -- kernel --arch x86_64`. | unowned |

### B1727-nsnet-inline-fastopen-cache

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1727 | DEFECT | high | **`tcp_fastopen::ClientCache` stored its `BUCKETS` lock array inline, making it 8192 B, which made `NsNet` 9336 B and gave `net::net_ns::state::materialize_state` a 9256 B stack frame — over half a 16 KiB kernel stack, reserved on entry whether or not the namespace state had to be created.** Every SYN delivered to a listener reaches it: `deliver_tcp_to_listener` → `tcp_fastopen::plan` → `enable_bits` + `ns_keys` → `materialize_state`, unconditionally, in softirq context. That put the worst interrupt-reachable path at 13872 B of 16384 before the trap frame, and a hardware IRQ provably nests into the softirq drain (`lapic/dispatch.rs` does `sti; do_softirq(); cli`), so the guard page was crossed by arithmetic, not by luck — which is what the ~50% intermittency of the serial `#DF` was. The reference does not inline this: its destination-metrics hash is a separately allocated bucket array the namespace refers to by pointer, and its fast-open context is a pointer in the namespace struct. The bucket array is now a heap allocation built by pushing one bucket at a time, so no array temporary is ever materialised on the stack. | Measured on clean release builds of `main` and this branch. `ClientCache` 8192 → 16 B; `NsNet` 9336 → ~1160 B; `materialize_state` frame **9256 → 1152 B**; worst interrupt-reachable path `rx_drain_softirq` **13872 → 10000 B**, `deliver_rx_in` 13024 → 9152 B — headroom 2512 → 6384 B. `frame-size-gate` x86_64 was **FAIL on main** (`9256 NEW`, ≥8192 ceiling) and now PASSes on both arches; `stack-depth-gate` x86_64 drops from 53 over-ceiling paths to 1. `cargo test -p net` 1947 passed / 0 failed. Guard: `the_bucket_array_is_not_stored_inline` fails if the array returns inline — positive control run, it reports `ClientCache is 8192 B — the bucket array is inline again`. | B1727 |
| FIXED d244bc3c0 | MISSING | low | **`current_unix_registry`, `unix_registry_for_addr` and `unix_registry_for_path` had no callers anywhere in the tree.** Production AF_UNIX sockets already retain their network-namespace owner and select the canonical registry through `unix_registry_for_addr_in`; pathname addresses remain global and abstract addresses use that owner. Removed the three dead current-task/path selectors and their dead numeric-key helpers rather than preserving a second selection API that could drift from socket ownership. | `d244bc3c0`; whole-tree symbol search is clean. `cargo test -p net net_ns --lib -- --test-threads=1`: 21 passed. | B1886 |

### B1728-virtio-probe-stack-depth

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1728 | DEFECT | high | **`register_netdev` ran the receive softirq inline, putting the entire receive-and-forward chain on the probe's own stack.** The call closed a real lost-wakeup window — a completion landing between the RX ring being filled at probe and the `NetRx` handler being installed raised a softirq with no handler and was dropped, so the first frame (a DHCP OFFER) could sit undrained. But draining it inline meant device registration carried `rx_drain_softirq` → ethernet → IPv6 → TCP listener → transmit → and, because that chain reaches a scheduling point, the whole `schedule()` subtree: **14320 B of a 16384 B stack on aarch64**, 13104 B on x86_64. The reference never calls the poll loop from device registration; it marks the receive half pending and lets the softirq run from the interrupt-exit path. Raising is sufficient here because the handler is installed one line above, so the pending bit is now guaranteed to be serviced — the window the inline drain existed to close stays closed, and the work runs on the interrupt stack where it belongs. | `stack-depth-gate` before/after on clean release builds of both arches. aarch64 `VirtioNetOps::probe` **14320 → PASS** (under the 13000 B ceiling), x86_64 **13104 → PASS**. Both arches now report only the 4 allowlisted boot-stack entries. DHCP verified end to end by `tools/boot-smoke-dhcp.sh`, the smoke that exists for exactly this lost-wakeup risk. | B1728 |
| FIXED B1728 | DEFECT | med | **`VirtioPciProbeTrace` was built on every probe and never read unless `debug-boot` was on.** ~408 B — including its own copy of the `MAX_RESOURCE_QUEUES` resource array — written into the probe frame and discarded; the type's own doc comment admitted it ("in a default build the record is still built and passed, but no field is ever read") and carried an `allow(dead_code)` to silence the consequence rather than remove the cost. ### B1729-cgroup-inode-superblock

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1729 | DEFECT | high | **`name_to_handle_at` took the export ops from the INODE's superblock back-pointer, which every pseudo-filesystem leaves unset, so cgroupfs's own 8-byte handle encoder was never consulted and the service manager got EOVERFLOW for every cgroup.** The reference reaches these ops through the dentry (`dentry->d_sb`), which is always populated because an inode cannot exist without a superblock. Here `i_sb` is a `Weak` the inode builder must be TOLD to fill, and only ext4 and devnode fill it — every filesystem that synthesizes inodes on lookup (cgroupfs, procfs, sysfs) leaves it empty. So the width fell back to the VFS-generic 12-byte `(ino, generation)` FID for exactly the filesystems that override it. The cgroup-id reader sets `handle_bytes` to `sizeof(uint64_t)` and does not run the grow-and-retry protocol, so it failed every time. cgroupfs's `CgroupSuperOps` was correct, complete, wired at `fs.rs:38`, unit-tested, and unreachable — the machinery-without-callers class, one indirection further out than usual. Both `name_to_handle_at` and `open_by_handle_at` now take the superblock from the path's mount, falling back to the inode's. | **25 → 0.** A clean release build of `main` produced 25 `Failed to get cgroup ID of cgroup …: Value too large for defined data type` in one boot — one per cgroup, and the ONLY failure of any kind in the log (no failed services, no failed mounts, no dependency failures; `grep -oiE "Failed to [a-z ]+"` returns that one message and nothing else). After the fix, zero on both arches, and zero `Failed to` of any kind. `boot-smoke` PASS x86 76 s, arm 62 s. Hosted: `crates/kernel/vfs/tests/export_sb_source.rs`, 4 tests, with a positive control — reversing the preference to `inode_sb.or(mount_sb)` fails `the_mounts_superblock_is_preferred_over_the_inodes`. | B1729 |

It exercises the mount lookup, export backend, decode permission ladder, reconnect, and file construction as one syscall boundary. | `023cabfec`; `tools/network-conformance-manifest.tsv` rows 303,304. Guest conformance PASS: x86_64 + aarch64, `cgroup_handle: PASS`. | B1884 |
| FIXED c8ec035a4 | DEFECT | med | **`syscalls::socket_control_tests::netlink_getsockopt_imports_optlen_before_dispatch_or_value_copyout` fails on `main`.** Pre-existing, not from this lane — confirmed by running it in the main tree at `010346474`, where it fails identically both in the full suite and in isolation. The assertion is an ordering one (`import < negative && negative < dispatch && dispatch < value`), so it is claiming netlink `getsockopt` imports the caller's `optlen` before it dispatches or copies the value out, and that ordering no longer holds. ### B1730-irq-nesting-unbounded

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1730 | DEFECT | high | **x86_64 sent EOI BEFORE the interrupt handler ran, so a source that was still asserted could re-interrupt while its own handler was executing.** The reference sends it after — `handle_irq_event(desc)` then `cond_unmask_eoi_irq(desc, chip)` — precisely so the in-service bit stays set for the handler's whole duration and the same source cannot re-enter. Ours EOI'd at the top of `oxide_irq_dispatch`, clearing the in-service bit immediately, so a serial port whose receive FIFO refills as fast as it is drained re-interrupted the moment interrupts came back on. aarch64 already had this right (`gic/dispatch.rs` EOIs after `invoke_arm_irq_handler`), so this was a lockstep gap, not an unknown. | `lapic/dispatch.rs` EOI moved from before the `match vec_tag` to after it. | B1730 |
| FIXED B1730 | DEFECT | high | **The bottom-half count was taken INSIDE the drain, after interrupts were re-enabled, leaving a window in which nesting was unbounded.** `irq_exit()` dropped the hardirq count, the dispatcher then executed `sti`, and only afterwards did `do_softirq` add `SOFTIRQ_OFFSET`. An interrupt arriving between the `sti` and that add saw `in_interrupt() == 0`, concluded it was free to drain, and opened its OWN interrupt-enabled window — and so could the next one, and the next, all on the single 16 KiB per-CPU interrupt stack with no depth bound anywhere in `dispatch.rs`, `irq.rs` or `preempt.rs`. The reference closes exactly this by disabling bottom halves while interrupts are still off (`irq_exit_rcu` → `__local_bh_disable_ip(SOFTIRQ_OFFSET)`) and enabling them inside the drain. `bh::softirq_tail_{begin,run,end}` split the accounting so the dispatcher takes the count before `sti` and releases it after `cli`; the `sti`/`cli` stay in arch code. Nesting is now bounded at two levels: a nested dispatch's `softirq_tail_begin()` returns false, so it never opens a window and runs entirely with interrupts off. | The probe that reproduced the fault twice (live-gnome, typing at the serial console) now shows **0 `#DF` and 0 guard-page hits**. Before: `BUG: IRQ stack guard page was hit (stack is fffffb0000006000..fffffb000000a000, rsp=fffffb0000006000, headroom=0)`. | B1730 |
| FIXED B1730 | COVERAGE | high | **A guard-page hit reported raw registers and could not say which stack overflowed.** Task stacks and the per-CPU interrupt stack are slots of ONE allocator window, so `rsp` sitting on a boundary named neither — and the two have different budgets and opposite fixes. The question was answered by hand, from a register dump, across several sessions. The reference classifies the faulting address in its double-fault handler and prints the stack's name and bounds; that is now what happens here, plus the most-repeated return address on the dead stack, which is what distinguishes unbounded nesting from a genuinely deep chain. The distinction mattered: a static depth walk put the whole interrupt path at ~8.7 KB of 16 KB and passed, while the same kernel ran that stack to zero — a gap that is only explicable by repetition, because the walk measures one pass. | First run with the classifier: `BUG: IRQ stack guard page was hit … headroom=0` — one line, where three sessions of register-reading had gone before. `crates/kernel/sched/src/kstack/classify.rs` + tests: 14 hosted tests, including the observed fault address reproduced as arithmetic. | B1730 |
| FIXED 9f753d073 | DEFECT | high | **Five units fail on a live-gnome boot: `sys-kernel-config.mount`, `sys-kernel-debug.mount`, `sys-kernel-tracing.mount`, `systemd-resolved.service`, `systemd-sysctl.service`.** Captured from `systemctl --failed` after letting the boot settle 90 s. The three mounts are configfs, debugfs and tracefs — the same pseudo-filesystems already filed as discarding their mount data entirely. Recorded as a concrete worklist: this is what "the OS does not come up properly" actually consists of now that the cgroup-id failures are gone. | `systemctl --failed --plain --no-legend` on live-gnome, this branch. Note `systemctl` pages by default over serial; pass `SYSTEMD_PAGER=cat` or the probe hangs in `less`. | unowned |

| FIXED B1731 | DEFECT | high | **Only `127.0.0.1` was bindable out of the whole loopback prefix, so the stub resolver could not start.** `bind_screen::classify_v4` admitted an address covered by a LOCAL-table route only when the route's `src_hint` equalled the address itself. The loopback route is registered once with `src_hint: Some(127.0.0.1)` for all of `127.0.0.0/8`, so every other loopback address classified `Other` and `bind()` answered EADDRNOTAVAIL. ### B1732-mount-setattr-detached

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1732 | DEFECT | high | **`mount_setattr` required the mount to be in the CALLER's namespace, which refused every mount made through the new mount API.** `mnt_setattr_attached` gated on `check_mnt`, i.e. `mount.namespace_id() == current_namespace().id()`. A mount produced by `fsmount(2)` lives in the ANONYMOUS namespace `fsmount` puts it in until `move_mount(2)` grafts it, so that test failed for exactly the sequence the API exists to support: `fsopen` → `fsconfig` → `fsmount` → **`mount_setattr`** → `move_mount`. The reference's `do_mount_setattr` checks only that the path names a mount root (`path_mounted`) and has no namespace test at all. Reaching the mount already proves access — it was named either by a path the caller can resolve or by a descriptor it holds. | Guest, before: `mount -t debugfs -o nosuid,nodev,noexec none /tmp/m` fails (32) while the same mount with no options succeeds. `LIBMOUNT_DEBUG=all` named the failing call outright: `fsopen [success] / fsconfig [success] / fsconfig [success] / fsmount [success] / mount_setattr(set=0x0000000e clr=0x00000000) [Invalid argument]`. After: `opt=0`. | B1732 |
| FIXED B1732 | DEFECT | high | **Four boot units failed on this one cause, and the whole failed-unit list is now empty.** `dev-mqueue.mount`, `sys-kernel-config.mount`, `sys-kernel-debug.mount` and `sys-kernel-tracing.mount` are all mounted by the service manager with `nosuid,nodev,noexec`, and every one of those goes through the new mount API, so all four died at the same `mount_setattr`. Was 6 failed units at the start of this campaign. | B1732 |
| FIXED C271 | COVERAGE | high | **Two different features share the name `debug-mount`, so `--features debug-mount` silently selected the wrong probe.** `kmain`'s `debug-mount` forwarded to `pmm`/`vmm`/`ipc` — a write-lock RIP probe that traces MUNMAP, not filesystem mounts — while the mount-syscall trace is `syscalls`'s OWN `debug-mount`, reached through `kmain`'s `debug-mnt`. A probe built with the wrong one compiled to nothing and its silence was read across two boots as "the mount syscall is never called", which was an artifact. **Correction to the original row: `debug-mnt` DOES propagate** — a build with it puts both the syscalls-side (`syscall=mount flags=`) and vfs-side (`[MNTCREATE] via=`) strings in the binary, verified by `strings`. Registration state now matches the reference exactly. | Guest before: `eth0` appears UP in the very first link dump. Both fixtures now bring the link up the way userspace would, so they model the real sequence: register down, bring up, then transmit. ### B1736-getlink-single-device

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1736 | DEFECT | blocker | **`handle_getlink_in` ignored the request and always replied with a full multipart dump, so a query for ONE device returned whatever was first in the table — loopback.** `RTM_GETLINK` is two requests in the reference. With `NLM_F_DUMP` it walks every device and terminates with `NLMSG_DONE`; WITHOUT it, `rtnl_getlink` resolves the single device the caller named — by `ifi_index`, else by `IFLA_IFNAME` — and replies with one non-multipart `RTM_NEWLINK`, or `-ENODEV`. Ours took only the header, had no access to the request body at all, and answered both forms identically. Every client that queried a device by name or index therefore read **loopback's identity and loopback's flags**. That is why `ip link show eth0` printed `lo`, and why the network manager believed a down, unaddressed `eth0` was already up and externally configured: it asked about eth0 and was told about `lo`, which is `<LOOPBACK,UP>`. | Guest before: `ip -o link show eth0` → `1: lo: <LOOPBACK,UP>`. After: `2: eth0: <BROADCAST,MULTICAST> … state DOWN`, the device actually asked for. Hosted: a by-name single get now returns the named device and asserts the reply is one message, non-multipart, with no `DONE`; plus `ENODEV` for an unknown name, `EINVAL` for a truncated request, and attribute-parsing cases. Positive control: disabling the single-get branch fails the new assertion. | B1736 |
| FIXED B1736 | DEFECT | blocker | **The guest now takes a DHCP lease and has working IPv4.** This was the blocker behind "no lease" and behind the `nmcli` oddity reported from the desktop. With the single-device form answered correctly the guest comes up with a full lease and reachable gateway. | Guest after: `2: eth0 inet 10.0.2.15/24 brd 10.0.2.255 scope global eth0`; `ip -4 route` → `10.0.2.0/24 dev eth0 proto kernel scope link src 10.0.2.15` and `default via 10.0.2.2 dev eth0 src 10.0.2.15`; `ping -c2 10.0.2.2` → `1 received, rtt 0.203 ms` (the first packet is lost to ARP resolution). Before: `ip -4 addr` showed only `lo`, no routes, no lease. | B1736 |

### B1737-carrier-model

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1737 | DEFECT | blocker | **The kernel installed the emulator's guest address on every virtio-net link at boot, so the network manager never owned the device and never ran DHCP.** `pci-boot` set `10.0.2.15/24` plus a default route on each registered interface, calling it "the boot-time v1 network identity". A kernel does not know its own IPv4 identity — a DHCP client learns it — and a manager that finds a link already carrying an address and a default route it did not configure marks the device externally-connected and declines to own it. It then never activates the link, never performs DHCP, and never publishes the DNS servers the lease carries, which is why `/etc/resolv.conf` had no `nameserver` line and names failed while a query aimed at a server's address still worked. | Guest before: `eth0:connected (externally)`, address present but no `nameserver`. After: `eth0:connected`, `inet 10.0.2.15/24 … dynamic … valid_lft 86345sec` — the `dynamic` flag and the lease lifetime are the proof it came from the DHCP server rather than the seed. | B1737 |
| FIXED B1737 | DEFECT | blocker | **Carrier lived in the same flags word userspace writes, so bringing a link up cleared it.** The reference keeps carrier out of `dev->flags` entirely — it is `__LINK_STATE_NOCARRIER` in `dev->state`, owned by the driver through `netif_carrier_on`/`off` — and derives `IFF_RUNNING` / `IFF_LOWER_UP` at read time in `dev_get_flags`, while `__dev_change_flags` never takes the volatile bits from a caller. Here carrier was a stored `IFF_RUNNING` bit in the writable word, so an ordinary "bring this link up" cleared it and the link reported no carrier the instant anyone administered it. The manager, having just brought the device up itself, was told there was no carrier and refused: `Connection 'eth0' is not available on device eth0 because device has no carrier`. Now `IfaceEntry::carrier` is a separate driver-owned bit, `iff::dev_get_flags` derives the two reported bits from it, and `IFF_VOLATILE` is masked out of every userspace write. | Guest: `<NO-CARRIER,BROADCAST,MULTICAST,UP>` → `<BROADCAST,MULTICAST,UP,LOWER_UP>`, and `WIRED-PROPERTIES.CARRIER: off` → the device reaching `connected`. Hosted: five tests covering derivation while down/up/with/without carrier, that a caller cannot forge the bits, and that the volatile mask keeps every administrative bit writable. | B1737 |
| FIXED B1737 | DEFECT | high | **Two ways to read interface flags, and the link dump used the raw one.** `snapshot_in_ns` reported flags while `iface_flags` returned the stored word, and the `RTM_GETLINK` builder discarded the snapshot's value and re-read the raw one — so the derived carrier bits never reached the wire and a device with carrier still reported `NO-CARRIER`. `iface_flags` is now the single accessor and derives, as the reference funnels every reader through `dev_get_flags`. | `rtnetlink/iface.rs` took `stack.ifaces.iface_flags(id)` and dropped `snap.flags`. | B1737 |
| FIXED B1889 | INFRA | med | `cgroup::selftest::run()` ran once during rootfs setup before `sched::cgroup::install()` had installed the task-resolution and migration hooks, so its predictable `mkdir='fail'`/`rmdir='fail'` result looked like a boot failure. The pre-install invocation is deleted; the one post-install invocation remains the only cgroup self-test owner. | `cargo test -p kmain --lib --features debug-cgroup --no-fail-fast` and `cargo check -p kmain --features debug-cgroup` pass; source has exactly one `cgroup::selftest::run()`, after `sched::cgroup::install()`. | B1889-cgroup-selftest-order |
| FIXED 0eafc98ba | DEFECT | high | **`make qemu-x86-debug` fails every time: PID 1 gets ESRCH writing its own pid into its own cgroup, and the service manager freezes.** At guest t≈28.5 s, `write("1")` to `/init.scope/cgroup.procs` returns `-3`, and the manager prints `Failed to create /init.scope control group: No such process` then `Freezing execution.` — so the boot never reaches a login and the target appears to hang. The cause is `sched::cgroup::migrate_hook` resolving the writer through a registry scan (`resolve_user_pid` / `lookup_by_vpid`) that misses for the caller's own vpid, instead of simply using the calling task the way the `vpid == 0` path already does. The reference never returns ESRCH for a process writing its own pid into a cgroup it just created. It reproduces only under `debug-all` because `debug-syscall`'s per-syscall trace widens the window; the defect is not the tracing. Compilation is clean — this is not the feature-gated-compile-error class. | Full boot capture with `--features debug-all`: the ESRCH write, both manager errors, and the freeze. `crates/kernel/sched/src/cgroup.rs` ~156-178. | unowned |

### B1739-cgroup-exit-side-table

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1739 | DEFECT | high | **A task's exit state was recorded in a map keyed by tid, beside the task, where it could disagree with the task itself.** `CGROUP_EXITS: BTreeMap<u32, Weak<Task>>` was consulted by `migrate_hook` to refuse a migration for a task on its way out. The reference has no such table: `cgroup_procs_write_start` tests `PF_EXITING` ON THE TASK. Two records of one fact can disagree, and this one did — an instrumented boot caught the gate refusing PID 1, a task that is plainly neither reaped nor a zombie, so its tid was in the table while it was alive. Replaced with `Task::exiting`, set at the head of both exit paths exactly where the table was written, so the flag is per-Task-object and a recycled tid cannot inherit a stale answer. | Instrumented boot: `[cgprobe] exit-gate ESRCH vpid=1` while `resolve` succeeded, so the refusal came from the table and not from the lookup. Hosted: `the_exiting_flag_lives_on_the_task_and_starts_clear`. The table, its `Weak` values, and the four imports that existed only for it are gone; `cargo build -p sched` is warning-clean. | B1739 |
| FIXED 0eafc98ba | DEFECT | high | **`make qemu-x86-debug` still fails: PID 1 is still refused its own cgroup, and the cause is NOT the side table.** With the table removed the failure is unchanged — `Failed to create /init.scope control group: No such process` at t≈28.5 s, then `Freezing execution.`, verified on a live `--features debug-all` boot of this branch (log confirmed to be the new kernel: the probe strings are absent). So the ESRCH now comes from one of the other two exits in `migrate_hook`: the vpid resolve returning nothing, or returning a task that is genuinely reaped/zombie, or `cgroup::migrate_process` itself refusing. Note `lookup_by_vpid` falls back to "any group member if the leader has already exited" — a fallback that can hand back a DEAD thread, which the exit gate would then correctly refuse; that is the first thing to check. The earlier instrumented run measured the OLD code and named the table, so it does not narrow the remaining paths. | Live `debug-all` boot on this branch, t=28.5 s, both manager errors present. | unowned |

### B1740-cgroup-skip-exiting

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1740 | DEFECT | blocker | **`make qemu-x86-debug` froze at boot because `migrate_hook` reported ESRCH for a task on its way out.** The reference does not: `cgroup_migrate_add_task` returns without acting when `PF_EXITING` is set — *"@task either already exited or can't exit until the end"* — and the part that CAN fail, `cgroup_procs_write_start`, rejects only a pid that resolves to nothing (ESRCH) or a kthread pinned by `PF_NO_SETAFFINITY` (EINVAL). It never turns "this task is leaving" into an error, because the write asks for a state the task is about to reach anyway: nothing to move, nothing to report. The service manager took the ESRCH at its word — `Failed to create /init.scope control group: No such process`, then `Freezing execution.` — and every debug boot hung at t≈28.5 s. The destination is now returned as the source, so the caller sees a move from where it already is. | A full `--features debug-all` boot now reaches userspace and answers on serial: `boot-smoke: PASS … in 253s`, with **zero** `control group` or `Freezing execution` lines, where every previous debug boot froze. Hosted: `a_migration_naming_an_exiting_task_is_skipped_not_refused` and `a_migration_naming_an_unknown_pid_is_still_esrch`, so the one failure the reference does report is still reported. Positive control: restoring the ESRCH fails the first with `an exiting task must be skipped, not refused: Esrch`. | B1740 |
| FIXED B1740 | DEFECT | high | **A third AF_PACKET fixture was missed when NICs began registering administratively down, so two workspace tests failed.** B1735 corrected registration to match `ether_setup` and fixed the two fixtures in the `net` crate that assumed registration implied UP; `crates/kernel/socket/src/packet_tests.rs` has the identical "register, never bring up, expect transmit" shape and was not touched, so `packet_tx_ring_kick_skips_payload_materialization` and `packet_tx_ring_sendmmsg_kick_skips_each_payload_import` returned `Err(Enetdown)`. The transmit path is right — the reference refuses a down link — and the fixture now brings the link up as the sibling two do. | `cargo test -p socket` 44 passed / 0 failed. Same defect class the B1735 commit message described, one call site short. | B1740 |

### C269-stack-gates-in-routine-path

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED C269 | COVERAGE | high | **`frame-gate` and `stack-gate` existed but ran nowhere, so both sat RED on `main` for a month while every routine gate stayed green.** They were reachable only by typing the Makefile target by hand: `make ci` ran `lint-ratchet audit-counts matrix-gate hosted-gate test-build-gate test build build-debug`, and `.githooks/pre-push` ran `lint-ratchet`, `hosted-gate`, `test-build-gate`, `feature-gate` and (main only) `boot-smoke` — neither included them. The only other home was `.github/workflows/pr.yml`, which has taken no automatic trigger since 2026-07-04, so the gates have never executed in CI either. What got through: an 8192 B lock array inlined into per-namespace state (B1727), giving a function reachable from softirq receive a 9256 B frame and leaving the worst interrupt-reachable path 2.5 KiB from the guard page — the intermittent serial `#DF`. A per-function ceiling structurally cannot catch a chain that sums, and a type-check cannot see a frame at all; only these two, read off a linked ELF, can. Both are now in `make ci` and in the pre-push hook for any push touching kernel/driver/arch surfaces, bypassable with `SKIP_STACK_GATE=1`. Wired now specifically because B1727 and B1728 took both gates green on both arches for the first time, so they cannot start red and be ignored again. | `make -j2 frame-gate stack-gate` on this branch: 4/4 PASS, rc=0. Positive control — `make -j2 stack-gate STACK_DEPTH_CEILING=5000` exits **2** with `make: *** [Makefile:291: stack-gate-x86] Error 1`, so a failure propagates through the new wiring rather than being swallowed. `bash -n .githooks/pre-push` clean. | C269 |

### C270-irq-stack-depth-domain

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED C270 | COVERAGE | high | **The interrupt path was invisible to the stack-depth gate, which is why the `#DF` was found by dying rather than by a check.** The walker follows direct call edges only, and every hardware interrupt handler in this kernel is reached through a registered function pointer — the MSI vector table, the x86 line-handler table, the nine softirq slots, the NAPI poll list, the tick-poll hook and the exit-to-user hook. So the receive path measured **8 bytes** deep and `drv_serial::deliver` 32, and the gate's own docstring recorded the gap rather than closing it ("INTERRUPTS AND EXCEPTION ENTRY are not modelled"). The target sets are finite and enumerable — each table has a handful of registration sites, all in this tree — so they are now named in `tools/irq-edges-<arch>.tsv` and merged into the call graph before the walk. With 23 edges resolved on x86_64 the interrupt path reports its real depth for the first time: **8704 B of the 16384 B interrupt stack**. | `make irq-gate-x86` PASS at a 12000 B ceiling, printing 8704 (`oxide_irq_exit_to_user`) and 8432 (`oxide_irq_dispatch`). Positive control: `--irq-fail 8000` exits **1** naming both roots. Without the map the same roots report 7600/7872, and `rx_isr_msi` reports 8. | C270 |
| FIXED C270 | COVERAGE | high | **The hardirq stack had no budget of its own.** It is a different 16384 B from the task stack with a different worst case: the entry asm deliberately does not re-switch when an interrupt arrives while already on it, and the softirq drain runs with interrupts unmasked, so a second entry lands on top of whatever the drain has already spent. A single ceiling shared with task stacks cannot express that. `--irq-roots` / `--irq-fail` add a second domain rooted at the two functions the entry asm calls after switching SP, with a 12000 B ceiling — the 4 KiB below the stack size is not slack, it is the nested interrupt's stack. | New `irq-gate` target, in `make ci` and in the pre-push hook alongside `frame-gate`/`stack-gate`. | C270 |
| FIXED C270 | COVERAGE | med | **The gate's pass/fail decision had no test.** `--self-test` covered the accounting — prologue summation, the arch return-address difference, recursion, indirect flagging, the blocking tail, allowlist identity — and stopped short of the layer that turns a number into an exit code, which is the layer that actually merges or blocks a defect. Extracted as `verdict()` and tested with a positive control per branch, each asserting the intended verdict fires AND the others stay empty so a rule that fires on everything cannot pass. The case that matters: a path one byte deeper than its recorded budget must be `worse`; a ratchet written `>=` instead of `>` lets a path grow without limit one byte at a time with every run green. | Positive control run: relaxing that comparison makes `--self-test` fail with `AssertionError: []`; restoring it passes. | C270 |

| FIXED de2421536 | DEFECT | high | Journal ring wrap hard-coded block 1 instead of `s_first`, and the old contiguous `data_at_first + i` writer did not wrap individual payload positions. | FIXED a7c7de71 | MISSING | med | `IPV6_RECVPATHMTU` was stored and never read by anything: nothing produced a path-MTU notification and nothing delivered one. Implemented as the reference does — a SINGLE-SLOT replace-in-place cell on the IPv6 socket (NOT the extended-error queue), filled at the size refusal when the socket forbade fragmentation, and drained at the top of the IPv6 datagram receive AHEAD of the datagram queue with no receive flag selecting it. The receive carries no payload, names the unreachable destination, and returns one `IPV6_PATHMTU` control message. | Decisions in `crates/kernel/net/src/sock_opts/sol_ipv6/pathmtu.rs` (ungated), 7 hosted tests. Cell on `Ipv6Opts` (`state.rs`); producer `sock_v6.rs::note_path_mtu` at all three IPv6 send failure seams; consumer `sock_io.rs::take_path_mtu` at the top of `recvfrom_opts`. Positive controls: dropping the fragmentation-ban condition turns `a_notification_needs_the_option_the_refusal_and_the_fragmentation_ban` RED; ignoring the option bit on drain turns `an_ordinary_receive_drains_the_cell_and_an_unset_option_does_not` RED; restoring the `want.any()` shortcut ahead of the notification branch turns `a_drained_notification_is_the_whole_control_answer` RED. All GREEN restored. | B1964 |
| FIXED B1966 | MISSING | high | The generic socket-option state was held in THREE places — the internet socket's option block, the netlink socket's own fields, and the virtual-socket family's own fields — where the reference has one socket base under every family. It is now one type, `net::sock_base::SockBase`, embedded by `SockOpts` (`opts.base`), `NetlinkSocket` (`base`) and `VsockSocket` (`base`): the reuse/keepalive/broadcast/urgent-inline switches, the two buffer budgets and the receive-buffer lock, both timeouts, `SO_PRIORITY`, `SO_MARK`, the timestamp word and key, the device binding, the credential and label switches and the generic flag/scalar area each have exactly one home. One `SockBase::apply` stores every admitted write and one read table answers from the base directly, so no family snapshots a second copy: `SockView` now carries only the socket's identity (`sock`, `acceptconn`, `socket_type`, `protocol`, `netns_cookie`, `napi_id`). Netlink and the virtual-socket family gained the mark, the priority, the timestamp selection and the device binding with the base. | `net` `sock_base::tests` (7), `netlink` `sol_socket::tests` (5), `net` `vsock_socket::sol_socket::tests` (4). Positive controls: routing the label switch back into the generic flag word fails `the_credential_and_label_switches_have_one_home_each`; letting the base store an unresolved interface index fails `the_device_binding_is_handed_back_to_the_family`, `the_four_options_netlink_had_no_home_for_now_read_back` and `a_device_binding_is_resolved_before_it_is_stored`. All GREEN restored. | B1966 |
| FIXED B1966 | DEFECT | med | The virtual-socket family answered SOL_SOCKET with a table of its own: five identity options plus hand-rolled timeval arithmetic for the two timeouts, with no `EDOM` screen, no negative-seconds rule and no capability ladder — a second copy of transforms the generic owner already had, which disagreed with it. Both directions now run the one import, the one admission ladder and the one value table, and the zero-copy switch is the generic flag rather than a private bool beside it. The family keeps only what the reference keeps: `SO_ZEROCOPY`'s transport gate on the write, and the `SOL_VSOCK` options. | `net` `vsock_socket::sol_socket::tests::{both_timeouts_live_on_the_shared_base, the_generic_surface_stores_and_reads_back, the_identity_options_answer_from_the_socket_shape}`; `vsock_socket::zerocopy` and `lifecycle_tests` now assert through the one table. | B1966 |
| FIXED B1966 | MISSING | med | A netlink unicast ignored the destination's receive-buffer budget and always enqueued, so the send timeout that had just been given a home had no wait to bound. The budget now decides: the message is taken when the destination's queue was empty (the first message always fits, so an over-sized one cannot wedge the port) or when the result fits the budget, and only while the destination is not congested. A refused message fails a non-blocking send with `EAGAIN` and otherwise blocks the SENDER on the destination's sender wait list, bounded by the sender's own send timeout; a drained destination releases them. A message the destination's filter dropped still reports the length the caller handed over. | `netlink` `admission::tests` (3), `ports::tests::a_unicast_is_admitted_by_the_destination_receive_budget`. Positive control: making `attach_verdict` always admit turns all three admission tests and the ports test RED; restored GREEN. | B1966 |
| FIXED B1966 | DEFECT | med | The IPv6 send-control admission for the extension-header ancillary numbers existed in two copies — the canonical one in `net::sock_opts::sol_ipv6::pktoptions` and a private one in `socket::control_raw::parse_v6` — which disagreed on the duplicate/replace ordering and had no type-2 routing screen in one of them. `parse_v6`'s seven extension-header arms now call `pktoptions::admit_one`, and the private `parse_ext`/`parse_routing` copies are deleted. | `socket` `control_raw_tests::the_extension_header_admission_is_the_same_owner_the_option_write_uses` asserts each case through the send parser AND through the owner directly. Positive control: re-introducing a private copy turns it RED; restored GREEN. | B1966 |
| FIXED B1966 | DEFECT | med | Found by the first guest run of the differential corpus: `getsockopt(SOL_SOCKET, SO_BINDTODEVICE)` on a netlink descriptor answered `ENOPROTOOPT` where the reference answers the bound interface's NAME (and a zero length when the socket has no binding). The read is now one owner, `SockBase::bound_device_name` plus one copyout, reached by the internet, netlink and virtual-socket paths alike; the buffer screen runs before the interface is resolved, so a short buffer is `EINVAL` rather than the name's own refusal. | Guest differential `sockopt_bindtodevice\|lo` row now matches the Linux oracle byte-for-byte on both arches; `net` `sock_base::tests::the_device_binding_read_screens_the_buffer_before_it_resolves`. | B1966 |
| FIXED B1966 | DEFECT | med | Found by the same run: `SO_NETNS_COOKIE` answered zero in the initial network namespace, because it published the stack-internal namespace index rather than the namespace's global namespace-tree identity. | FIXED B1966 | COVERAGE | med | Carried from B1964: the differential corpus had been verified against a host oracle but never run against the Oxide guest. It has now run on BOTH arches and matches the Linux oracle byte-for-byte — 68 records each, `diff` clean. It found the two defects above on its first guest run. | `make smoke-sockopt-diff-x86` PASS, `make smoke-sockopt-diff-arm` PASS (`sockopt-diff-smoke: PASS - <arch> exact Linux differential`). | B1966 |
| FIXED B1966 | INFRA | low | The differential harness dropped the corpus's first record. The normalizer now strips a leading prompt before matching, on both sides. | `tools/boot-smoke-sockopt-diff.sh::normalize_records`; both arches now report 68 records. | B1966 |

| FIXED 471ffa21a | DEFECT | low | Row 438 was investigated as "no `exec_update_lock` anywhere". A writer-exclusion latch of that name already existed and was taken by execve and Landlock TSYNC. | FIXED 471ffa21a | DEFECT | high | `pidfd_getfd` judged the target's credentials and then fetched the fd as two unlocked reads. A concurrent `execve` between them yields a descriptor from a process that has just become setuid or dropped dumpability — the check passed against credentials that no longer exist. Linux holds `exec_update_lock` for reading across both halves. | read side now held across check+fetch; `sched::rwsem` 5 tests, both admission predicates positive-controlled | B1967 |
| FIXED 471ffa21a | MISSING | med | No sleeping reader-writer semaphore existed. `sync::RwLock` is a spinlock, and the `exec_update_lock` write span covers the address-space swap, fd-table unshare and credential commit — allocating, faulting, preemptible — so a contended acquirer had no correct primitive to wait on. | `crates/kernel/sched/src/rwsem.rs`, writer-pessimistic, gate arithmetic ungated and hosted-tested | B1967 |

| FIXED ab95839e9 | DEFECT | high | `PIDFD_GET_INFO` reported `pid`/`tgid` in the TARGET's pid namespace while `ppid` already came from the reader's — so one reply mixed numbers from two namespaces, and the first two named different processes (or none) in the reader's. | `snapshot_task` now answers every number in the reader's namespace | B1967 |

| FIXED ab95839e9 | DEFECT | med | `PIDFD_GET_INFO` from a pidfd passed into an unrelated pid namespace answered as though the target were visible, instead of EREMOTE. `EREMOTE` did not exist in the errno table. | `syscalls/src/pidfd/info.rs`; `Errno::Eremote` added | B1967 |

| FIXED ab95839e9 | DEFECT | med | The result mask was not trimmed to the caller's struct length, so a short `struct pidfd_info` could come back with a bit set for a field it never received — an instruction to read uninitialised memory. | `pidfs_ioctl::mask_fitting`, positive-controlled | B1967 |
| FIXED (this branch) | DEFECT | HIGH | A userfaultfd write-protect barrier lapsed silently when the page was reclaimed. The write-protect walk left swap and migration leaves alone, and neither non-present encoding had anywhere to record the state, so eviction dropped it: the fault that brought the page back rebuilt the leaf from the mapping's (writable) permissions and the next write took the ordinary path instead of being reported. The reference carries the bit in the swap entry itself and preserves it across swap-out, swap-in, fork and the write-protect walk. | `hal::pt_walker::explicit_root_tests::a_write_protected_page_that_is_evicted_comes_back_write_protected` — reinstating the old `uffd_wp_step` swap/migration arm turns it and 2 siblings RED across all three crates (hal 2 failed, hal-x86_64 1, hal-aarch64 1); restored → 125/170/165 pass. | B1968 |
| FIXED (this branch) | DEFECT | MED | A *read* fault on a write-protected absent address resolved the page and re-applied the protection afterwards (`Intercept::ResolveThenProtect`), publishing a writable leaf in between. The two steps took the page-table lock separately, so a peer thread of the same address space writing in that window escaped the barrier once, unreported. The fill now builds the leaf already protected and publishes it in the single store that makes it visible. | `vmm::tests_rmap_cow::a_read_fault_over_a_protected_hole_installs_the_page_already_protected`; `hal-{x86_64,aarch64}::vmm::tests::a_leaf_asked_for_the_barrier_is_never_published_writable`. Making the fill ignore `install_uffd_wp` turns the vmm test RED (381 passed / 1 failed); making either arch packer ignore `PageFlags::UFFD_WP` turns the arch test RED (169/1 and 164/1). | B1968 |
| FIXED (this branch) | DEFECT | MED | The fill ioctls carried the identical window: a `MODE_WP` fill mapped the page with the VMA's own (writable) permissions and re-protected the installed leaf as a second store. Holding the page-table lock across both does NOT close it — the racing writer is userspace and never takes that lock — so a peer thread's write in between escaped the barrier once. The barrier is now folded into the permissions before anything is published, and the leaf helper it replaced no longer exists to be reached the old way. | `fs::userfaultfd::work::leaf::tests::a_write_protecting_fill_installs_the_marker_and_removes_write_permission`, rewritten to drive `fill_page_flags` — the function the fill actually calls — instead of the removed post-hoc leaf rewrite. Making `fill_page_flags` ignore its `wp` argument → RED (1038 passed / 1 failed); restored → 1039 / 0. | B1968 |
| FIXED (this branch) | DEFECT | MED | Reclaim's own write-protect step (`replace_present_4k_flags_if_pa_at_root`) rebuilt the leaf from VMA-derived flags, dropping the barrier BEFORE the eviction that was supposed to carry it — so the fix above could not have worked without this. A protection rewrite now preserves the state the leaf already carried. | `hal::pt_walker::explicit_root_tests::a_protection_rewrite_keeps_the_barrier_the_leaf_already_carried`; dropping the carry → RED (124 passed / 1 failed). | B1968 |
| FIXED (this branch) | DEFECT | MED | `clear_swap_4k_at_root` compared the raw leaf word against a freshly packed swap entry. Once a swap leaf can also carry write-protect state, that comparison refuses exactly the protected entries — leaking their swap slots for the life of the mapping. Now compared by decoded identity. | `hal::pt_walker::explicit_root_tests::tearing_down_a_protected_swap_leaf_still_releases_its_slot`; restoring the raw comparison → RED (124 passed / 1 failed). | B1968 |
| FIXED (this branch) | DEFECT | MED | Fork rebuilt the child's leaves from VMA protection and installed child swap leaves with no write-protect state, so a child that DID inherit the monitor lost every barrier. The reference copies the parent's leaf and clears the state only when the destination is not registered; a child inherits a registration only when the monitor asked for fork events. | `vmm::tests_swap_fork::a_child_inherits_the_swap_barrier_exactly_when_it_inherits_the_monitor` and `an_unregistered_parent_hands_its_child_no_barrier`; forcing the child's swap leaf unprotected → RED (381 passed / 1 failed). | B1968 |
| FIXED (this branch) | DEFECT | LOW | Fault-around installed speculative neighbour leaves inside userfaultfd-armed ranges, overwriting barriers no fault ever touched and pre-empting the minor faults a MINOR registration exists to receive. The reference suppresses fault-around entirely for `VM_UFFD_WP`/`VM_UFFD_MINOR` mappings. | `vmm::uffd::tests::faulting_around_is_suppressed_on_a_range_a_monitor_is_watching`. | B1968 |

| FIXED f489ea13f | MISSING | med | `clone(CLONE_SYSVSEM)` WITHOUT `CLONE_THREAD` got its own empty SysV `SEM_UNDO` list. | FIXED f04ebd124 | MISSING | med | A stop reached ONE parent. | FIXED 6cadc0852 | DEFECT | med | `clock_settime(CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID)` / `(CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID)` returned EPERM and read the user timespec on the way. | FIXED 6cadc0852 | DEFECT | med | `clock_nanosleep` on a DYNAMIC per-thread CPU clock naming the caller returned EOPNOTSUPP where the reference returns EINVAL: `classify_clock` collapsed the static id and a pid-0 encoding to one value, and the two have different callback tables. `ClockSpec` now carries the clock-id provenance. | `posix_clock::tests::static_and_dynamic_cpu_clocks_are_distinguishable_and_answer_differently`. | FIXED 6cadc0852 | DEFECT | med | `CpuMeasure::Sched` sampled `utime + stime`. It reads `sum_exec_runtime_ns`, with a matching process-wide total on `ThreadGroup`. | `sched::tests::cpu_clock_measure` 4 tests. | FIXED B1974 | DEFECT | LOW | `bind_screen::classify_v4`'s local-route fallback tested only the TABLE (`RT_TABLE_LOCAL`), not the route's TYPE. Confirmed live, not theoretical: closing it required also giving the loopback prefix's own local-table row LOCAL type, because it was inserted with the unicast default and the type-aware test would otherwise have refused every 127.0.0.0/8 bind (the `127.0.0.53` stub-resolver regression). | `net::bind_screen::tests::a_local_table_row_classifies_by_route_type_not_by_the_table_it_sits_in`. Closed with one family-agnostic admission token (`net::sock_admit::AddrAdmission`): the hook runs above the family branch in `049_bind.rs`/`042_connect.rs`, and the vsock and netlink entry points DEMAND the token, so skipping it is a compile error rather than an ordering convention.
